Cure Issues

1. Final-Core holes: remove unsolved holes from the trusted/final pipeline.
   Goal:
   - Source holes remain allowed during elaboration so Cure can emit :hole_goal diagnostics.
   - Final Core must contain no `{:hole, _}`.
   - `unsafe` type holes are always compile errors.
   - `unsafe` proof holes are accepted only by an explicit unsafe path, warned unconditionally, then erased.
   - `unsafe` body holes are accepted only by an explicit unsafe path, warned unconditionally, then lowered to runtime traps.
   - No unsafe path may leave `{:hole, _}` in final Core.

   Change:
   - `Kernel.check/3` must reject `{:hole, name}` instead of accepting it at any expected type.
   - `Kernel.check_def/2` must reject definitions whose type or body contains holes.
   - `Kernel.validate_certificate/2` must reject holes via `check_def/2`.
   - `Env.certify/2` must reject pending definitions and hole-containing bodies.
   - Pending definitions must use a non-Core pending marker/body, not `{:hole, "__pending__"}`.
   - Serializers must reject holes in final artifacts.
   - Emit/codegen must reject unsolved holes before erasure/lowering.
   - Antigen check-mode assays must expect final-kernel rejection of holes.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/core/term.ex
   - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
   - lib/cure/core/env.ex
   - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/erase.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/check_mode.ex
   - lib/antigen/assays/elab.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/decode_probe.ex
   - lib/antigen/assays/serialization.ex
   - lib/antigen/coverage.ex

2. Primitive equality: remove `{:eq, ...}`, `{:refl, ...}`, and `{:rewrite, ...}` from final Core.
   Goal:
   - Equality is represented only by canonical builtin-inductive `Eq`.
   - Final Core equality proposition:
     `{:data, :Eq, [ty], [lhs, rhs]}`
   - Final Core reflexivity proof:
     `{:ctor, :refl, [value]}`
   - Surface `rewrite` may remain, but must elaborate to canonical Eq eliminator/transport before final Core.
   - No final Core path should produce or accept:
     `{:eq, ty, a, b}`
     `{:refl, a}`
     `{:rewrite, proof, motive, body}`
     `:veq`
     `:vrefl`

   Change:
   - Remove primitive equality nodes from `Term.term?/1`, traversal, shift/subst, external encoding, and decoding.
   - Add/keep rejection helper such as `Term.has_primitive_equality?/1`.
   - Remove kernel inference/checking rules for primitive `eq`, `refl`, and `rewrite`.
   - Remove `:veq` and `:vrefl` values.
   - Remove primitive equality evaluation, quoting, conversion, normalization, serialization, certification traversal, positivity traversal, erasure, relevance traversal, resolution, substitution, unification, and totality closure support.
   - Rewrite or retire Antigen generators/assays that manufacture primitive equality.
   - Keep `{:prim, :eq, ...}` numeric/boolean primitive equality separate for now; it is not the same thing as propositional equality.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/core/term.ex
   - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
   - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
   - lib/cure/core/value.ex
   - lib/cure/core/quote.ex
   - lib/cure/core/conv.ex
   - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex
   - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
   - lib/cure/core/certificate.ex
   - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
   - lib/cure/core/builtins.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/erase.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/relevance.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/totality_closure.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/resolution.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/subst.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
   - lib/antigen/coverage.ex
   - lib/antigen/bisect.ex
   - lib/antigen/runner.ex
   - lib/mix/tasks/antigen.ex
   - lib/antigen/challenge.ex
   - lib/antigen/shrink.ex
   - lib/antigen/assays/totality_closure_assay.ex
   - lib/antigen/assays/rewrite.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/rewrite.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/equality.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/serialization.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/malformed.ex

   - lib/antigen/generators/indexed.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/dep_match.ex

3. Surface rewrite: replace primitive rewrite with canonical Eq eliminator/transport.
   Goal:
   - Keep user-facing `rewrite` syntax if desired.
   - Do not lower it to `{:rewrite, ...}`.
   - Lower it to canonical equality elimination/transport/subst over builtin `Eq`.
   - Proof and motive handling must be checked through ordinary Core terms.
   - Erasure removes proof evidence where valid.

   Temporary safe state:
   - Until canonical Eq eliminator exists, reject `rewrite_expr` with:
     `:rewrite_requires_canonical_eq_eliminator`

   Permanent target:
   - Add canonical eliminator/transport representation, likely as typed builtin/global:
     `Eq.rec`
     `Eq.ndrec`
     `subst`
     `transport`
   - Elaborate:
     `rewrite proof in body`
     into ordinary applications of that eliminator.
   - Kernel checks the eliminator using ordinary function application and builtin Eq typing.
   - No primitive rewrite node survives.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
   - lib/cure/core/builtins.ex
   - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/erase.ex
   - lib/antigen/assays/rewrite.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/rewrite.ex

4. Proof-token equality: remove atom/token proofs from trusted stdlib and old type checker.
   Goal:
   - No atom should inhabit arbitrary equality.
   - `:cure_refl` must not be treated as a universal proof token.
   - Equality proofs must be canonical checked `Eq` values.

   Change:
   - Remove or quarantine `Std.Proof`.
   - Remove or rewrite `Std.Equal` as wrappers around canonical builtin Eq.
   - Remove `subtype?(:atom, {:eq, _, _, _}), do: true`.
   - Remove/rewrite old `Cure.Types.Equality` token-proof system.
   - Update diagnostics that mention old `Std.Equal.trans/2`, `Std.Equal.cong/2`, or token proof APIs.

   Main files:
   - lib/std/proof.cure
   - lib/std/equal.cure
   - lib/cure/types/type.ex
   - lib/cure/types/equality.ex
   - lib/cure/compiler/errors.ex

5. Legacy top-level `proof` containers: remove permissive proof blocks.
   Goal:
   - Proofs should be ordinary erased total definitions, not a separate unchecked/permissive declaration form.
   - `with proof <ident>` may remain only if it binds real checked evidence.

   Change:
   - Remove top-level `proof` declaration parsing.
   - Remove permissive proof-container checking.
   - Remove bypass where proof containers avoid dependent elaboration.
   - Remove proof-container support from stdlib preloader and REPL help.
   - Keep the word `proof` only for real evidence-binding syntax if needed.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
   - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
   - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex
   - lib/cure/repl.ex

6. `.cureproof` artifacts: remove weak proof artifacts from the trusted path.
   Goal:
   - Side-channel proof artifacts must not be trusted unless independently checked by the kernel/normalizer/totality checker.
   - Unknown proof/certificate shapes must reject, not pass.
   - Missing SMT solver must not mean success.

   Change:
   - Demote `.cureproof` collection to diagnostics or remove.
   - Remove permissive verifier behavior.
   - Stop accepting `:cure_refl` as arbitrary proof.
   - Stop accepting unknown refinement/SMT/totality shapes as `:ok`.
   - Stop publishing proof artifacts by default.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/project/proof.ex
   - lib/cure/project/proof/verifier.ex
   - lib/cure/compiler.ex
   - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex
   - lib/cure/project.ex

7. Refinements and SMT: remove warning-only trust from final/static/dependent mode.
   Goal:
   - Refinements remain useful, but must be checked sugar over dependent machinery.
   - SMT may solve obligations, but unknown/unavailable must reject unless explicitly unsafe/trusted.
   - Base type does not automatically satisfy refinement type.

   Change:
   - Remove/mode-gate:
     `subtype?(t, {:refinement, base, _, _}), do: subtype?(t, base)`
   - Unknown refinement obligations must be errors in final/static mode.
   - SMT `:unknown` must not become `:sat`.
   - Solver unavailable must reject final obligations.
   - Legacy SMT-dependent module must not be trusted as the dependent type checker.
   - Refinement syntax should lower to:
     Sigma/dependent pair,
     erased proof argument,
     bounded newtype,
     or smart constructor.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/types/type.ex
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
   - lib/cure/smt/solver.ex
   - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex
   - lib/cure/types/guard_refinement.ex

8. `Any`: remove gradual `Any` from static/dependent/final mode.
   Goal:
   - `Any` may remain in REPL/dynamic/rookie/interop mode.
   - `Any` must not be both top and bottom in the static/dependent checker.
   - Unknown type must not silently become `Any` in final mode.

   Change:
   - Remove `subtype?(:any, _)` from static/dependent mode.
   - Remove universal subtyping for type holes and unresolved type variables.
   - Replace fallback-to-`:any` with errors in final/static mode.
   - Constructor parameter defaults, operator/call failures, branch joins, record failures, pattern failures, and unknown AST nodes must not become `Any` in final mode.
   - Keep REPL synthetic `Any` behind a hard boundary.

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/types/type.ex
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
   - lib/cure/repl.ex

9. Universes: remove fixed `Type 0..2` ceiling.
   Goal:
   - Cure should support universe polymorphism and cumulativity like Agda/Lean-style practical dependent languages.
   - Replace fixed integer ceiling with level expressions.

   Target level language:
   - `zero`
   - `succ(level)`
   - `max(level, level)`
   - `param(name)`
   - `meta(id)` during elaboration only

   Target typing rules:
   - `Type u : Type (succ u)`
   - `Pi : Type (max u v)`
   - `Sigma : Type (max u v)`
   - `Type u <= Type v` when constraint `u <= v` is solved
   - Final Core rejects unsolved level metas and level constraints.

   Change:
   - Replace integer-only `{:type, level}` or generalize it to level expressions.
   - Remove `@ceiling 2`.
   - Replace bounded level search with level metavariables and constraints.
   - Make builtin types/functions universe-polymorphic where required.
   - Especially:
     `Eq.{u} : (A : Type u) -> A -> A -> Type u`
     `refl.{u,A,x} : Eq.{u} A x x`

   Main files:
   - lib/cure/core/universe.ex
   - lib/cure/core/term.ex
   - lib/cure/core/value.ex
   - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
   - lib/cure/core/builtins.ex
   - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
   - lib/antigen/generators/universes.ex

10. Universe-polymorphic globals: add level parameters to declarations and global references.
    Goal:
    - Store polymorphic definitions once, instantiate them by level arguments.
    - Follow Lean’s model structurally:
      constants/globals carry universe arguments.

    Change:
    - Replace:
      `{:global, name}`
      with something like:
      `{:global, name, level_args}`
    - Global declarations need:
      `name`
      `level_params`
      `type`
      `body`
      `status`
      `quantities`
    - Final Core rejects malformed universe applications and unsolved level args.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/core/term.ex
    - lib/cure/core/env.ex
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/core/quote.ex
    - lib/cure/core/conv.ex
    - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/resolution.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/totality_closure.ex
    - lib/antigen generators touching globals

11. Legacy SMT-backed dependent type system: remove from trusted dependent checking.
    Goal:
    - Cure should have one dependent type story:
      Core indexed families + Pi/Sigma/dependent records + canonical Eq + total type-level computation.
    - SMT/refinements may be sugar/automation only.

    Change:
    - Delete, quarantine, or rewrite `Cure.Types.Dependent`.
    - It must not independently decide dependent correctness by SMT satisfiability or broad compatibility.
    - If kept, it should elaborate into Core obligations.

    Main file:
    - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

12. Totality: make Core certificates authoritative.
    Goal:
    - Surface totality checker may be advisory.
    - Core certificate checking should be the final authority for definitions used in types.
    - Unknown coverage/termination must not count as proven total in final mode.

    Change:
    - Demote or remove weak surface totality gates.
    - Definitions reducible during typechecking must be:
      total,
      pure,
      closed,
      hole-free,
      meta-free,
      primitive-equality-free,
      certificate-checked.
    - Partial/effectful runtime definitions may exist, but must not reduce in types.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/types/totality.ex
    - lib/cure/core/certificate.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/totality_closure.ex

13. Primitive operations: remove `{:prim, op, args}` from final Core as the operation model.
    Goal:
    - Keep primitive literals/types in Core:
      `{:int_type}`
      `{:int_lit, n}`
      `{:float_type}`
      `{:float_lit, f}`
    - Replace primitive operation nodes:
      `{:prim, :add, [x, y]}`
      with typed primitive global applications:
      `{:app, {:app, {:global, :int_add, levels}, x}, y}`

    Reference direction:
    - Lean models primitives/ops mostly as constants plus ordinary application, with trusted reducers/folders keyed by constant names.
    - Agda models primitives as named implementations with explicit checked types.
    - Cure should model primitive ops as typed global constants with attached trusted reducer/lowering metadata.

    Target primitive declaration:
    - name
    - type
    - arity
    - totality
    - effects
    - reducible_at_typecheck?
    - evaluator/reducer hook
    - runtime lowering hook

    Example:
    - `Int.add : Int -> Int -> Int`
    - `Int.sub : Int -> Int -> Int`
    - `Int.mul : Int -> Int -> Int`
    - `Int.eq  : Int -> Int -> Bool`
    - `Int.lt  : Int -> Int -> Bool`
    - `Float.add : Float -> Float -> Float`
    - `Float.eq` likely runtime-only or carefully classified because floating equality is tricky.
    - `Int.div` should not remain plain `Int -> Int -> Int`; use `NonZeroInt`, erased proof argument, or return `Option/Result`.

    Primitive classifications:
    - `:logic_total`
      pure, total, deterministic, reducible in types
    - `:runtime_total`
      pure and total at runtime, but not reducible in typechecking
    - `:partial`
      must return Option/Result or require proof/precondition
    - `:effectful`
      never reducible in types
    - `:unsafe`
      explicit unsafe boundary only

    Change:
    - Remove `{:prim, op, args}` from final Core term taxonomy.
    - Remove kernel `infer_prim/3` as the typing mechanism.
    - Add primitive global declarations to environment/builtins.
    - Evaluator reduces saturated primitive globals by name.
    - Emitter lowers saturated primitive globals by name.
    - Serialization encodes primitive ops as globals/apps, not prim nodes.
    - Antigen primitive generators must generate typed primitive global applications instead of `{:prim, ...}`.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/core/term.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/value.ex
    - lib/cure/core/quote.ex
    - lib/cure/core/conv.ex
    - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex
    - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
    - lib/cure/core/builtins.ex
    - lib/cure/core/env.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/core_bridge.ex if present
    - lib/cure/elab/resolution.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/subst.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/totality_closure.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/primitive.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/mutation.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/serialization.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/malformed.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/conv_pair.ex
    - lib/antigen/coverage.ex
    - emitter/backend files that lower BEAM/Erlang ops

14. Primitive comparisons: add proof-producing comparison APIs instead of relying only on booleans.
    Goal:
    - Runtime booleans are fine:
      `Int.lt : Int -> Int -> Bool`
    - Dependent programming needs evidence-producing comparisons:
      `Nat.compare : (m : Nat) -> (n : Nat) -> Comparison m n`
    - Stdlib `Ord` should not primarily return atoms.

    Change:
    - Add total ordering datatype:
      `Ordering = Less | Equal | Greater`
    - Add indexed/evidenced comparison types where useful:
      `Comparison m n`
    - Branching over comparison results should refine local context.
    - This lets guarded branch linting/refinement work without relying on Z3 for common cases.

    Main files:
    - stdlib Ord/comparison files
    - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
    - pattern-match/refinement checker files
    - branch refinement / guard refinement files

15. Dependent pattern matching: keep practical indexed matching, do not chase full Agda.
    Goal:
    - Cure should support the subset needed for executable indexed programs:
      constructor refinement,
      impossible branch detection,
      explicit motives,
      exhaustiveness after index refinement.
    - Cure should not aim for full Agda interactive case splitting/with-abstraction machinery initially.

    Change:
    - Improve motive checking/synthesis for annotated dependent matches.
    - Add impossible-constructor pruning based on index unification.
    - Conservative kernel coverage may remain, but elaborator should insert/justify impossible branches.
    - Any impossible branch accepted by elaboration must be justified by checkable index contradiction/equality.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex
    - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
    - dep-match Antigen generators/assays

16. Positivity: keep but strengthen tests; do not overcopy Agda internals.
    Goal:
    - Cure needs a conservative positivity checker for inductives.
    - It should be tested against obvious negative and nested/mutual cases.
    - It does not need Agda’s full module/record/telescope machinery.

    Change:
    - Add negative tests for:
      negative recursive occurrence,
      occurrence under another family’s parameter,
      mutual datatype cycles,
      higher-order fields,
      type alias expansion if present.
    - Add positive tests for nested strictly-positive cases that should pass.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/positivity.ex
    - positivity assay/test files

17. Final Core invariant: enforce globally.
    Final Core must contain:
    - no holes
    - no unresolved term metas
    - no unresolved level metas
    - no unresolved constraints
    - no primitive propositional equality
    - no token proofs
    - no unchecked SMT claims
    - no `Any` fallback
    - no effectful type-level computation
    - no partial type-level computation
    - no `{:prim, op, args}` operation nodes once primitive globals land

    Final Core may contain:
    - Pi
    - Sigma
    - dependent records or their Core encoding
    - indexed data
    - canonical builtin Eq
    - primitive literals/types
    - typed primitive globals
    - ordinary application
    - total certified globals

18. Recommended implementation order.
    Phase 1 — trust-boundary cleanup:
    1. Remove final hole acceptance.
    2. Remove primitive propositional equality from final Core.
    3. Add temporary rejection for surface rewrite until canonical Eq transport exists.
    4. Remove proof-token equality/std proof stubs.
    5. Remove top-level permissive proof containers.
    6. Remove weak `.cureproof` trust.
    7. Make unknown SMT/refinement obligations reject in final mode.

    Phase 2 — static discipline:
    8. Remove `Any` from static/dependent/final mode.
    9. Make Core certificates authoritative for type-level reduction.
    10. Quarantine/rewrite legacy SMT-backed dependent checker.

    Phase 3 — universes:
    11. Replace fixed `Type 0..2` with universe polymorphism.
    12. Add level constraints and cumulativity.
    13. Add universe parameters to globals.
    14. Make builtin Eq universe-polymorphic.

    Phase 4 — primitive model:
    15. Replace `{:prim, op, args}` with typed primitive globals.
    16. Add primitive registry with type/effects/totality/reducer/lowering metadata.
    17. Classify primitives into logic-total/runtime-total/partial/effectful/unsafe.
    18. Rework emitter/evaluator/Antigen around saturated primitive globals.

    Phase 5 — practical dependent programming:
    19. Implement canonical Eq transport/eliminator.
    20. Rewrite surface `rewrite` to canonical transport.
    21. Strengthen indexed pattern refinement and impossible branch handling.
    22. Add proof-producing comparison APIs.
    23. Expand Antigen for canonical Eq, universes, primitive globals, and final-Core invariants.

0. Correction from earlier diffs.
   - `Cure.Core.Env` is defined in:
     - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
   - It is not in:
     - lib/cure/core/env.ex
   - Any patch touching `Env.add_def/5`, `Env.certify/2`, pending defs, or certified defs should target `lib/cure/core/inductive.ex`.

1. Project/release builds currently default to no type checking.
   Problem:
   - Static/release/publish flows can compile without type checking unless explicitly opted in.
   - That makes the trusted pipeline dependent on caller discipline.

   Files:
   - lib/cure/project.ex
     - `compiler_opts/1` defaults:
       `check_types: Keyword.get(opts, :type_check, false)`
     - `compile_project/2` defaults:
       `check? = Keyword.get(opts, :check_types, false)`
     - Dependency compile results are ignored:
       `_ = Cure.Compiler.compile_file(...)`

   - lib/cure/release.ex
     - `Cure.Project.compile_project(project, output_dir: ebin_dir, emit_events: false)`
     - This does not pass `check_types: true`, so release inherits the project default.

   - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex
     - Builds tarballs from files.
     - Does not require a successful checked build before publish.

   Required change:
   - Static/project/release/publish mode must default to checked compilation.
   - `--no-type-check` should be dev/REPL-only, or require explicit unsafe/dynamic mode.
   - Dependency compile errors must be propagated, not ignored.
   - Publishing should require a fresh successful checked build.

2. Compiler opt-out must be mode-gated.
   Problem:
   - `Cure.Compiler` exposes `check_types: false` / `--no-type-check`.
   - That is acceptable for fast dev iteration, but not for final artifacts.

   Files:
   - lib/cure/compiler.ex
   - CLI/mix task files that pass compiler opts
   - lib/cure/project.ex
   - lib/cure/release.ex

   Required change:
   - Add explicit compilation modes:
     - `:interactive`
     - `:dev`
     - `:static`
     - `:release`
     - `:publish`
   - Allow `check_types: false` only in `:interactive` / `:dev`.
   - Reject `check_types: false` in `:static`, `:release`, and `:publish`.

3. Stdlib preload silently ignores compile failures.
   Problem:
   - Stdlib JIT/preload compiles with `check_types: false`.
   - It logs compile errors but returns `:ok`.
   - That is unsafe if the preloaded stdlib is treated as trusted.

   File:
   - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

   Suspicious behavior:
   - JIT compile uses:
     `check_types: false`
   - Error path logs but still returns:
     `:ok`

   Required change:
   - Split stdlib preload into:
     - dev cache mode: may log and continue
     - trusted prelude mode: must check and fail on error
   - The dependent/static compiler should import only a checked prelude.

4. Effect checking is tied to event emission.
   Problem:
   - Function effect checking only runs under `if emit?`.
   - Turning off event emission can disable effect checking.

   File:
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

   Suspicious behavior:
   - `check_function_def/5` performs effect inference/checking only inside:
     `if emit? do ... end`

   Required change:
   - Effect checking must run independently of event emission.
   - `emit_events` should only control reporting, not checking.
   - The checker should accumulate effect errors regardless of `emit?`.

5. Declared effect mismatches do not fail type checking.
   Problem:
   - `maybe_check_declared_effects/5` emits an `:effect_error` event, but its result is ignored.
   - Functions with undeclared effects can still typecheck successfully.

   File:
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

   Suspicious behavior:
   - Calls like:
     `maybe_check_declared_effects(...)`
     are executed for side effects/events only.
   - The returned effect error is not added to `type_errors`.

   Required change:
   - `maybe_check_declared_effects/5` must return errors.
   - `check_function_def/5` must include those errors in the function’s type-check result.
   - In static/release mode, undeclared effect = compile error.

6. Extern declarations classify effects but do not enforce them.
   Problem:
   - Extern functions validate shape and infer effects, but declared effects are not enforced against inferred extern effects.

   File:
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

   Relevant areas:
   - `validate_extern_decl`
   - extern branch in `check_function_def/5`
   - `maybe_check_declared_effects/5`

   Required change:
   - Extern declarations must require explicit effect annotations when effectful.
   - Inferred extern effects must be checked against declared effects.
   - Unannotated externs should default to rejected or `unsafe extern`, not pure.

7. Extern arity is not checked against Cure parameter count.
   Problem:
   - Extern metadata may contain `{module, fun, arity}`.
   - Codegen ignores the declared external arity and calls the remote function with the Cure parameter count.

   Files:
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
   - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

   Suspicious behavior:
   - `validate_extern_decl` validates extern shape, but not arity agreement.
   - `compile_extern_function` destructures:
     `{m, f, _ext_arity}`
     and uses `length(params)` for wrapper/call generation.

   Required change:
   - Check:
     `external_arity == length(cure_params)`
   - Mismatch should be a compile error.
   - If currying/adapter behavior is intended, require explicit wrapper syntax.

8. Effect inference treats unknown AST forms as pure.
   Problem:
   - `Cure.Types.Effects` falls back to returning the current effect accumulator for unknown nodes.
   - New/unsupported AST forms silently become pure.

   File:
   - lib/cure/types/effects.ex

   Suspicious behavior:
   - Fallback:
     `defp do_infer(_, _env, acc), do: acc`

   Required change:
   - Unknown AST in effect inference should be:
     - error in static/final mode
     - warning/advisory only in dev mode
   - Add clauses for missing AST forms:
     - pickup
     - record update
     - attribute access
     - comprehension
     - pipe/call variants
     - match/case variants
     - external/resource/runtime constructs

9. Transitive effect tracking appears broken.
   Problem:
   - `Effects.infer_call_effects/2` looks for `{:effun, ...}` in the type environment.
   - Function signatures are generally registered as `{:fun, ...}`, not `{:effun, ...}`.
   - This means calls to effectful Cure functions may not propagate effects.

   Files:
   - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
   - lib/cure/types/effects.ex
   - lib/cure/types/env.ex if present

   Required change:
   - Store effect annotations in function types.
   - Either:
     - use `{:effun, params, return, effects}`
     - or add effects metadata to all function env entries.
   - `infer_call_effects` must read that metadata.
   - Calling an effectful function inside a pure function must error.

10. Runtime-partial stdlib functions are typed as pure total.
    Problem:
    - Several stdlib externs are documented as raising, unsafe, nondeterministic, or effectful, but their Cure signatures are pure ordinary functions.

    Files:
    - lib/std/string.cure
      - `to_int`
      - `to_float`
      - `to_atom`
    - lib/std/regex.cure
      - `compile_bang`
    - lib/std/system.cure
      - `exit`
      - `system_info`
    - lib/std/gen.cure
      - random functions
    - lib/std/math.cure
      - partial/domain-sensitive float math and division-like operations

    Required change:
    - Replace raising APIs with `Result`/`Option`, or mark as effectful/unsafe.
    - `String.to_atom` should not be pure total; it can exhaust the atom table.
    - `System.exit` should return `Never` or an effectful exit type, not `Atom`.
    - Random generation must be effectful/nondeterministic or state-threaded.
    - Float ops should generally not reduce during typechecking.

11. `System.system_info(key: Atom) -> T` is an unchecked polymorphic foreign call.
    Problem:
    - A single extern can claim any return type `T`.
    - This is effectively an unsafe cast from BEAM runtime values into arbitrary Cure types.

    File:
    - lib/std/system.cure

    Required change:
    - Replace with specific typed wrappers:
      - `scheduler_count : Unit -> Int`
      - `otp_release : Unit -> String`
      - etc.
    - Or return an explicit dynamic value type:
      - `Dynamic`
      - `Any` behind unsafe boundary
      - `Result(SystemInfoValue, Error)`
    - Do not allow arbitrary `T` in static/dependent code.

12. Legacy dependent routing misses important constructs.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Elab.Program.dependent?/1` decides whether a module goes through the dependent elaborator.
    - It catches indexed types, sigma, rewrite, `Eq`, `refl`, implicit params, and some projections.
    - It does not clearly route refinement types, legacy Pi/Sigma modules, type holes, proof containers, or all dependent annotations into Core.

    File:
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex

    Required change:
    - Stop using a fragile “does this AST look dependent?” all-or-nothing router.
    - Route static/dependent mode through one pipeline.
    - Or require each feature to explicitly lower into Core obligations.
    - A module should not remain on the legacy checker path merely because it lacks obvious GADT syntax.

13. Legacy `Nat` resolves to `Int`.
    Problem:
    - In the legacy type resolver, `"Nat"` resolves to `:int`.
    - In dependent Core/std, `Nat` is an inductive Peano type.
    - This is a semantic split.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/type.ex

    Suspicious behavior:
    - `Nat` handled as integer in legacy resolver.

    Required change:
    - In static/dependent mode, `Nat` must mean the inductive `Nat`.
    - If machine natural numbers are desired, name them differently:
      - `UInt`
      - `NaturalInt`
      - `MachineNat`
    - Do not silently equate logical `Nat` and runtime `Int`.

14. Legacy Pi/Sigma modules are parallel dependent type systems.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Pi` and `Cure.Types.Sigma` implement dependent behavior outside Core.
    - They use surface AST substitution/reduction, not canonical Core checking.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/pi.ex
    - lib/cure/types/sigma.ex
    - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex

    Required change:
    - Remove them from the trusted path.
    - Either:
      - elaborate Pi/Sigma syntax into Core Pi/Sigma
      - or quarantine these modules as legacy/advisory only.
    - All trusted dependent checking should go through Core.

15. Legacy Pi erasure/substitution appears too weak.
    Problem:
    - `Pi.apply_return` substitutes explicit params but ignores implicit/erased dependency details.
    - `Pi.erase_implicits` resolves return AST without proper dependent substitution.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/pi.ex

    Required change:
    - Do not use this as a trusted dependent function implementation.
    - Lower to Core Pi and use Core substitution/evaluation.
    - If kept for diagnostics, mark as approximate.

16. Legacy Sigma admits `Any` and shallow subtyping.
    Problem:
    - `Sigma.instantiate` can return `:any`.
    - `Sigma.subtype?` compares resolved field types without proper dependent binder semantics.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/sigma.ex

    Required change:
    - Static mode: missing second field/type should be an error, not `Any`.
    - Dependent pair subtyping must be Core-level or removed.
    - Do not treat shallow resolved equality as proof of dependent-pair compatibility.

17. Type-level reducer is parallel to Core reducer.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Reduce` reduces surface expressions using `CoreBridge` and special cases.
    - This risks diverging from Core normalization/conversion.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex

    Required change:
    - Trusted type-level computation should use Core evaluation/conversion only.
    - Surface reducer may be advisory, but not authoritative.
    - CoreBridge must not lower to retired forms like `{:prim, ...}` once primitive globals land.

18. Parser silently turns some malformed/unknown type syntax into `Any`.
    Problem:
    - Unknown binary specifier/type syntax becomes `{:type, :any}`.
    - This makes parse/typing errors disappear into dynamic typing.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex

    Suspicious behavior:
    - Unknown binary specifier token path returns:
      `{:type, :any}`

    Required change:
    - In static/final mode, unknown type syntax should be a parse/type error.
    - `Any` should require explicit spelling and explicit dynamic/unsafe mode.

19. Type resolver fallback-to-Any is too broad.
    Problem:
    - Many resolver failures become `:any`.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/type.ex

    Suspicious behaviors:
    - `resolve(nil), do: :any`
    - refinement/type annotation fallback to `:any`
    - unknown generic fallback:
      `resolve(_), do: :any`
    - unknown lower-case/non-uppercase type name resolves to `:any`
    - `Any`, type holes, and type vars act as universal subtypes.

    Required change:
    - Static/final mode:
      - `nil` type = error
      - unknown type = error
      - unresolved type var = constraint/metavariable, not universal subtype
      - unsolved type hole = diagnostic + rejection before final Core
    - Dynamic/REPL mode may keep fallback behavior.

20. `Cure.Types.Holes` should be updated to the new hole policy.
    Problem:
    - Current legacy type-hole behavior uses `{:type_hole, :infer}` plus subtype silence.
    - New policy distinguishes type holes, proof holes, body holes, and unsafe forms.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/holes.ex

    Required change:
    - Model hole kinds explicitly:
      - `:type`
      - `:proof`
      - `:body`
    - Emit `:hole_goal` diagnostics.
    - Reject unsolved type holes before final Core.
    - Reject unsolved proof holes unless `unsafe`.
    - Reject unsolved body holes unless `unsafe`.
    - Add non-disableable warning for any `unsafe`.
    - Do not lower ordinary holes to runtime traps.

21. `unsafe` keyword and AST need to be added deliberately.
    Problem:
    - The proposed future unsafe-hole behavior is not represented in the lexer/parser yet.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex

    Required change:
    - Add `unsafe` as a keyword.
    - Parse unsafe holes as distinct AST nodes, not as ordinary `{:hole, ...}`.
    - Enforce:
      - `unsafe` + type hole = compile error
      - `unsafe` + proof hole = warning + unchecked proof accepted then erased
      - `unsafe` + body hole = warning + runtime trap lowering
    - Any `unsafe` occurrence emits an unavoidable compiler warning.
    - No `unsafe` hole may leave `{:hole, _}` in final Core.

22. Function call fallback returns `Any`.
    Problem:
    - Unknown functions or non-function values applied to args can return `:any`.
    - Argument inference errors are also swallowed.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Suspicious areas:
    - function/call inference around `do_infer`
    - `infer_args`
    - `params_match?`

    Required change:
    - Unknown function = error in static/final mode.
    - Non-callable value applied to arguments = error.
    - Argument inference error must propagate.
    - `Any` should not make parameters match in static/final mode.

23. Branch joins use `Any` as compatibility escape hatch.
    Problem:
    - `if`, `pickup`, and other branch joins treat `Any` as compatible with anything.
    - This masks real branch type mismatches.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Static mode:
      - condition must be Bool
      - branch types must join through real join/subtyping
      - no `Any` compatibility unless explicit dynamic boundary
    - Dependent mode:
      - branch refinements should elaborate to Core matches/motives.

24. Map/list/record inference can crash or hide errors.
    Problem:
    - Some collection inference paths pattern-match on successful inference:
      `{:ok, kt, e} = do_infer(...)`
    - Other paths turn failures into `Any`.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Propagate inference errors rather than crashing or returning `Any`.
    - Static mode should reject heterogeneous maps/lists unless explicitly typed as dynamic.

25. Record field failures become `Any`.
    Problem:
    - Missing/unknown record fields and failed field resolution can become `:any`.
    - Record default mismatches may only warn.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Unknown field = error in static/final mode.
    - Missing required field = error.
    - Default value type mismatch = error, not warning.
    - Failed default inference must not be treated as success.

26. Non-exhaustive destructuring is warning-only.
    Problem:
    - Destructuring in `let` can be non-exhaustive and produce a runtime raise.
    - In total/static code, that should not be accepted silently.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - In static/final/total code:
      - non-exhaustive destructuring = error
    - In runtime partial code:
      - allowed only with explicit partial/unsafe marker or Result-like handling.

27. Pattern refinement has many advisory `Any` fallbacks.
    Problem:
    - Pattern refinement uses `Any` when it cannot determine tuple/list/map/record/constructor shapes.
    - Unsupported binary segment shapes are skipped.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/pattern_refinement.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Split into:
      - advisory lint/refinement
      - trusted static checking
    - Trusted mode should error on unsupported pattern typing when it matters.
    - Advisory mode may skip with diagnostics.

28. Legacy unification strips refinements.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Unify` strips refinements before unification.
    - This is okay for rough shape inference, but not for proving refined compatibility.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/unify.ex

    Required change:
    - Do not use this unifier as proof that refinements hold.
    - Refined compatibility must generate obligations.
    - Static mode should not let `Any` unify with everything.

29. Refinement unknown still compiles.
    Problem:
    - Unknown refinement obligations produce warnings and no errors.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/types/refinement.ex
    - lib/cure/types/guard_refinement.ex
    - lib/cure/smt/solver.ex

    Required change:
    - Final/static mode:
      - `:unknown` = error
      - solver unavailable = error
      - timeout = error unless explicitly unsafe/trusted
    - Dev mode:
      - may warn and continue only outside final artifacts.

30. Guard refinement/exhaustiveness is advisory when solver unavailable.
    Problem:
    - If solver is unavailable, unreachable/dead-clause analysis can return no findings.
    - That is fine for linting, but not for trusted exhaustiveness/refinement.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/guard_refinement.ex

    Required change:
    - Treat this module as advisory unless it produces checkable evidence.
    - Trusted impossible-branch handling should come from Core/index unification and canonical equality, not SMT-only unknowns.

31. Surface totality accepts unknown coverage.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Totality` documents/implements unknown as non-error.
    - `coverage_ok?(:unknown)` returns true.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/totality.ex

    Required change:
    - Keep as advisory only, or change final mode:
      - unknown coverage = error
      - unknown termination = error
    - Core certificates should be authoritative for type-level reduction.

32. Env certification currently needs stronger body checks.
    Problem:
    - Certification should not rely only on closedness.
    - Pending body, hole body, primitive equality body, unsolved metas, and eventually primitive op nodes must be rejected.

    File:
    - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex

    Required change:
    - `Env.certify/2` should reject:
      - missing/pending body
      - open body
      - holes
      - primitive propositional equality
      - unresolved metas
      - unsolved level constraints
      - non-final primitive op nodes once primitive globals land
    - Certification should probably not be public without passing through `Kernel.validate_certificate/2`.

33. Primitive operations are spread across multiple layers.
    Problem:
    - `{:prim, op, args}` is handled in kernel/eval/emit/serialize/Antigen/CoreBridge.
    - This creates a second operation/application mechanism.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/core/term.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/quote.ex
    - lib/cure/core/conv.ex
    - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex
    - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
    - lib/antigen/generators/*

    Required change:
    - Replace primitive op nodes with typed primitive globals.
    - Keep primitive literals/types.
    - Add primitive registry with:
      - type
      - arity
      - totality
      - effects
      - reducer
      - runtime lowering
    - Typechecking should use ordinary global/app rules.

34. Primitive division/remainder are currently too weakly typed.
    Problem:
    - Primitive arithmetic has generic binary typing like `Int -> Int -> Int`.
    - Division/remainder are partial for zero divisor.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
    - stdlib math/int files

    Required change:
    - Replace:
      `Int.div : Int -> Int -> Int`
    - With one of:
      - `Int.div : Int -> NonZeroInt -> Int`
      - `Int.div : (a : Int) -> (b : Int) -> Proof(b != 0) -> Int`
      - `Int.checked_div : Int -> Int -> Option(Int)`
    - Same for remainder.

35. Float primitives need type-level reduction policy.
    Problem:
    - Float equality/order/arithmetic are machine/runtime semantics.
    - They should not accidentally become definitional equality inside types.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/std/math.cure

    Required change:
    - Classify float primitives as runtime-total or partial/domain-sensitive.
    - Do not reduce floats in typechecking unless semantics are deliberately specified.
    - Avoid using float comparison as proof-relevant equality.

36. Stdlib `Ord` should stop returning atoms.
    Problem:
    - `Ord` uses atoms like `:lt`, `:eq`, `:gt`.
    - This is untyped and does not support branch refinement well.

    File:
    - lib/std/ord.cure

    Required change:
    - Add:
      `data Ordering = Less | Equal | Greater`
    - Protocol returns `Ordering`, not atoms.
    - Later add indexed/evidenced comparison for `Nat`, vectors, bounds, etc.

37. Runtime `Eq` protocol should be named/segregated from propositional `Eq`.
    Problem:
    - Runtime equality protocol `Eq` and propositional equality `Eq` can confuse users and implementation paths.

    File:
    - lib/std/eq.cure
    - compiler/elaborator name resolution

    Required change:
    - Consider renaming runtime protocol to:
      - `Equal`
      - `DecEq`
      - `RuntimeEq`
    - Or namespace it clearly:
      - `Std.Eq.eq`
      - Core builtin `Eq`
    - Ensure name resolution never confuses protocol equality with propositional equality type.

38. Dynamic stdlib modules use `Any` heavily.
    Problem:
    - Modules such as access/iter/fsm/app/actor/supervisor use `Any`.
    - These are useful, but should not contaminate the static dependent core.

    Files:
    - lib/std/access.cure
    - lib/std/iter.cure
    - lib/std/fsm.cure
    - lib/std/app.cure
    - lib/std/actor.cure
    - lib/std/supervisor.cure

    Required change:
    - Mark these modules as dynamic/runtime libraries.
    - Static/dependent mode should require explicit import boundary:
      - `use dynamic Std.Iter`
      - `unsafe use Std.Access`
      - or similar.
    - Do not allow `Any`-heavy modules in trusted total/type-level code.

39. Auto-prelude comments already list dependent-clean gaps.
    Problem:
    - The dependent compiler excludes some stdlib modules because they are not dependent-clean.
    - This is a useful inventory that should become tracked work.

    File:
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex

    Noted exclusions:
    - `Std.Core` due `pickup`
    - `Std.Equal` due `:cure_refl`
    - `Std.Refine` not dependent-clean
    - protocols excluded

    Required change:
    - Convert these comments into explicit compatibility gates/tests.
    - Port or quarantine each module:
      - remove Std.Equal
      - rewrite Std.Refine over canonical obligations
      - decide protocol lowering for dependent compiler
      - make Std.Core dependent-clean or split dynamic parts.

40. Bless/advisor suggests `Any` as a normal fix.
    Problem:
    - Diagnostics/advisor may recommend annotating as `Any` to silence type mismatch.
    - That is the wrong default for static/dependent Cure.

    File:
    - lib/cure/bless/advisor.ex

    Required change:
    - Only suggest `Any` in dynamic/interop mode.
    - Static mode suggestions should prefer:
      - more precise type
      - explicit sum/Result/Option
      - proof obligation
      - dynamic boundary marker
      - unsafe marker if truly intended.

41. LSP formatting hides unknown types as `Any`.
    Problem:
    - UI fallback may render unknown as `Any`, which can mislead users into thinking dynamic typing was inferred.

    File:
    - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

    Required change:
    - Render unknown distinctly:
      - `<unknown>`
      - `_`
      - `?T`
      - unresolved type hole diagnostic
    - Reserve `Any` for explicit user-written/dynamic type.

42. Proof artifact binary format is unsafe for package trust.
    Problem:
    - `.cureproof` artifacts use Erlang external term serialization.
    - If any verifier loads untrusted package artifacts using `binary_to_term`, this is a supply-chain risk unless strictly safe and versioned.
    - Even with safe deserialization, the certificate semantics are currently weak.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/project/proof.ex
    - lib/cure/project/proof/verifier.ex
    - package registry/publisher files

    Required change:
    - Do not trust `.cureproof` yet.
    - If retained:
      - use canonical JSON/S-expression/Core certificate format
      - include version/schema/hash
      - reject unknown fields
      - never deserialize unsafe external terms from packages
      - independently verify all certificates.

43. Publish should require checked dependencies.
    Problem:
    - Project compilation of dependencies can ignore errors.
    - Publish/build steps can package files without checked dependency closure.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/project.ex
    - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex
    - lib/cure/project/package.ex
    - lib/cure/project/registry.ex

    Required change:
    - Publishing requires:
      - dependencies resolved
      - dependencies compiled successfully
      - current project checked successfully
      - final Core artifacts generated or validated
    - Dependency compile errors must abort.

44. Transparency/security warnings should not affect type soundness, but release policy should be explicit.
    Problem:
    - Registry/signature/transparency failures may degrade to warnings in some flows.
    - This is separate from type soundness but matters for trusted packages.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/project/registry.ex
    - lib/cure/project/package.ex
    - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex

    Required change:
    - Define package trust modes:
      - insecure/dev
      - verified
      - reproducible/release
    - Release/publish mode should fail on signature/transparency verification errors.

45. Codegen may compile unchecked or dynamically typed code into runtime traps.
    Problem:
    - Some language constructs are documented as runtime-raising when not statically proven.
    - That is okay for partial runtime code, but not for total/static code.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex

    Required change:
    - Static/total mode should reject constructs that can runtime-raise unless:
      - return type is Result/Option
      - function is marked partial/effectful
      - construct is explicitly unsafe
    - Runtime traps should be explicit in Core/lowering, not accidental.

46. Protocols need a dependent/static story.
    Problem:
    - Protocols provide ad-hoc polymorphism, but dependent compiler auto-prelude excludes protocols.
    - Runtime protocols may interact poorly with erased arguments, proofs, and totality.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - protocol parser/elaborator files
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
    - stdlib protocol files

    Required change:
    - Decide one:
      - protocols are runtime-only, forbidden in type-level computation
      - protocols elaborate to dictionary arguments
      - selected protocols have total erased dictionaries
    - Protocol methods used in types must be total, pure, and dictionary resolution must be deterministic/checkable.

47. Imports/prelude should distinguish trusted Core stdlib from runtime stdlib.
    Problem:
    - Current stdlib contains both dependent-clean logical definitions and dynamic/runtime/extern modules.
    - Importing all stdlib uniformly risks letting dynamic constructs into static checking.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
    - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex
    - lib/std/*.cure

    Required change:
    - Split stdlib into tiers:
      - `Std.Core.Checked`
      - `Std.Data`
      - `Std.Proof`
      - `Std.Runtime`
      - `Std.Dynamic`
      - `Std.Unsafe`
    - Static/dependent mode imports only checked tiers by default.
    - Runtime/dynamic tiers require explicit import.

48. Final artifact format should encode final-Core invariants.
    Problem:
    - Serializers and external encodings should not merely serialize terms; they should enforce final-Core readiness.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
    - lib/cure/core/term.ex
    - project/publish artifact files

    Required change:
    - Before serialization, reject:
      - holes
      - primitive propositional equality
      - unresolved metas
      - unsolved universe constraints
      - non-final primitive op nodes once removed
      - unsafe markers
      - Any/dynamic-only terms in static artifacts
    - Add artifact version and feature flags.

49. Antigen should get final-Core invariant assays.
    Problem:
    - Many Antigen generators currently target old forms or skip incomplete definitions.
    - It needs a dedicated final-Core gate assay.

    Files:
    - lib/antigen/assays/*
    - lib/antigen/generators/*
    - lib/antigen/coverage.ex
    - lib/antigen/challenge.ex

    Required tests:
    - final Core rejects holes
    - final Core rejects primitive propositional equality
    - final Core rejects unresolved metas
    - final Core rejects unknown universe constraints
    - final Core rejects Any fallback in static mode
    - final Core rejects unchecked SMT/refinement unknown
    - final Core rejects effectful type-level computation
    - final Core rejects partial type-level computation
    - final Core accepts canonical builtin Eq
    - final Core accepts typed primitive global applications once implemented

50. Add mode-sensitive checker configuration everywhere.
    Problem:
    - Many fixes depend on distinguishing dev/REPL/dynamic from static/release/final.
    - Current code often has booleans like `check_types` and `emit_events`, not a coherent mode.

    Main files:
    - lib/cure/compiler.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/project.ex
    - lib/cure/release.ex
    - lib/cure/repl.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex

    Required change:
    - Introduce one compile/check mode enum:
      - `:repl`
      - `:dev`
      - `:dynamic`
      - `:static`
      - `:release`
      - `:publish`
    - Make these policies mode-sensitive:
      - Any fallback
      - unknown SMT
      - holes
      - unsafe
      - effect checking
      - non-exhaustive patterns
      - runtime traps
      - extern trust
      - type checking required
      - stdlib import tier

51. Optimization runs after type checking and is not rechecked.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Compiler.compile_string/2` checks the original AST, then optimizes, then codegens.
    - Optimizer passes can rewrite expressions after the type checker has accepted the program.
    - There is no second type-checking pass, no proof that each optimization preserves typing, and no final Core validation for the optimized output.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler.ex
      - `compile_string/2`
      - `compile_and_load/2`
      - pipeline order:
        `parse -> maybe_check -> maybe_optimize -> codegen`
    - lib/cure/optimizer.ex
    - lib/cure/optimizer/inline.ex
    - lib/cure/optimizer/constant_fold.ex
    - lib/cure/optimizer/monomorphise.ex
    - lib/cure/optimizer/pipe_inline.ex

    Required change:
    - In static/release/publish mode, either:
      - optimize before type checking, then check the optimized AST, or
      - check before and after optimization, or
      - make each optimization produce a verified preservation certificate.
    - Until then, disable optimizer passes in final/static mode unless a post-optimization check is run.

52. Inliner purity check is a hardcoded name whitelist, not the effect system.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Optimizer.Inline.has_side_effects?/1` only recognizes a small list:
      `print`, `println`, `put_chars`, `throw`, `spawn`, `send`, `print_int`, `print_float`.
    - Effectful extern calls, runtime APIs, process functions, file/system/time APIs, and user effectful functions can be incorrectly classified as pure and inlined.

    File:
    - lib/cure/optimizer/inline.ex
      - `has_side_effects?/1`
      - `inlinable?/3`

    Required change:
    - The inliner must consume the real effect metadata from the type/effect checker.
    - A function is inlinable only if:
      - checked pure,
      - total or runtime-total as appropriate,
      - not extern unless explicitly marked pure-inline-safe,
      - not partial,
      - not unsafe,
      - not dependent on dynamic `Any`.
    - Static/release mode should reject inlining if effect metadata is missing.

53. Inliner substitution is not capture-aware enough.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Optimizer.Inline.substitute/2` recursively replaces variables by name.
    - It only handles a small AST fragment.
    - It does not handle lexical shadowing under lambdas, match arms, let patterns, comprehensions, or nested bindings.
    - It can either fail to substitute where needed or substitute into a scope where the same name was rebound.

    File:
    - lib/cure/optimizer/inline.ex
      - `substitute/2`

    Required change:
    - Replace name-substitution with a scope-aware substitution pass.
    - Track binders introduced by:
      - lambdas,
      - let/destructuring,
      - match arms,
      - pickup arms if they bind,
      - comprehensions,
      - with-abstraction,
      - function-local definitions.
    - Or disable inlining for any body containing binders until the substitution pass is hygienic.

54. Constant folding is a separate evaluator from Core/runtime semantics.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Optimizer.ConstantFold` evaluates integer arithmetic, comparisons, booleans, and string concat using Elixir semantics.
    - Core evaluation has its own primitive folding.
    - BEAM codegen has another lowering semantics.
    - These can drift, especially for division, remainder, booleans, floats, and future primitive globals.

    File:
    - lib/cure/optimizer/constant_fold.ex

    Required change:
    - Constant folding in static mode should call the same primitive registry/evaluator as Core normalization.
    - Do not maintain a separate arithmetic semantics in the optimizer.
    - Recheck after folding.
    - Fold only primitives classified as `:logic_total` or otherwise compile-time-safe.

55. Constant folding uses Erlang booleans, while Core Bool is inductive.
    Problem:
    - `ConstantFold` rewrites comparisons and boolean operations to literals with `subtype: :boolean`.
    - Legacy codegen compiles boolean literals to Erlang booleans.
    - Core evaluation folds Bool results to `{:vctor, :True, []}` / `{:vctor, :False, []}`.
    - This confirms a representation split between legacy runtime Bool and dependent Core Bool.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/optimizer/constant_fold.ex
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
    - lib/std/bool.cure

    Required change:
    - Define one explicit Bool boundary:
      - legacy/runtime `Bool` as BEAM boolean, or
      - Core/stdlib `Bool` as inductive `True | False`.
    - Do not let both silently represent the same type in static/dependent code.
    - If both are needed, name them separately:
      - `Bool` for logical inductive Bool,
      - `ErlangBool` or `RuntimeBool` for BEAM boolean.
    - Add explicit conversions at FFI/codegen boundaries.

56. Protocol dispatch picks the first registered implementation by method name, not by argument type.
    Problem:
    - `compile_function_call` can fall back to `resolve_protocol_call/3`.
    - `resolve_protocol_call/3` calls `ProtocolRegistry.list_impls_by_method(name)` and uses the first result.
    - It does not dispatch based on the static type of the receiver or a runtime guard.
    - This can call the wrong implementation when multiple impls define the same method.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
      - `resolve_protocol_call/3`
    - lib/cure/types/protocol_registry.ex
      - `list_impls_by_method/1`

    Required change:
    - Protocol call resolution must be type-directed.
    - Either:
      - elaborate protocols to dictionary arguments, or
      - generate a dispatch function with checked runtime guards, or
      - monomorphise protocol calls based on static type.
    - “First implementation wins” must be removed.

57. Protocol registry is a mutable public ETS table.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.ProtocolRegistry` uses `:ets.new(..., [:set, :public, :named_table])`.
    - Any process can mutate the registry.
    - The registry affects codegen/import resolution.
    - This is not deterministic or trustworthy for static/release builds.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/protocol_registry.ex

    Required change:
    - Do not use a public mutable global table as the source of truth for checked protocol resolution.
    - For static builds, protocol impls should come from:
      - the module AST,
      - checked dependency metadata,
      - final package manifest,
      - or explicit dictionary elaboration.
    - If ETS remains for REPL/dev, use it only as an interactive cache, not as trusted build state.

58. Protocol guards do not match Cure’s dependent/static type model.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Protocol.type_guard/3` uses BEAM predicates like `is_integer`, `is_binary`, `is_boolean`, `is_list`, etc.
    - This cannot distinguish:
      - `List(Int)` from `List(String)`,
      - refined types,
      - indexed types,
      - dependent records,
      - erased proof arguments,
      - logical `Nat` vs runtime `Int`,
      - inductive `Bool` vs BEAM boolean.
    - It also uses `map_get('__struct__', V)` in a guard for records, which is a runtime representation check, not a type proof.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

    Required change:
    - Runtime protocol guards must be explicitly runtime-only.
    - Static/dependent protocol use should elaborate to dictionaries or monomorphised functions.
    - Do not use BEAM guards as evidence of dependent/refined/static types.

59. Runtime Bool/protocol Bool mismatch is concrete.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Bool` defines Bool as an inductive builtin:
      `type Bool = False | True`
    - Core folds comparison results to `True` / `False` constructors.
    - `Protocol.type_guard("Bool")` checks `is_boolean`.
    - Legacy codegen likely treats literal booleans as BEAM booleans.
    - These are different runtime representations.

    Files:
    - lib/std/bool.cure
    - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Decide whether source `Bool` is:
      - an inductive ADT everywhere, or
      - a primitive BEAM bool everywhere, or
      - two explicitly different types.
    - The current mixed model is too easy to make unsound or at least incoherent.
    - Protocol dispatch, codegen, Core evaluation, and stdlib must agree.

60. Codegen compiles unknown AST nodes to `:undefined`.
    Problem:
    - `do_compile_expr/2` fallback emits a warning and returns the atom `:undefined`.
    - That means unsupported AST can become a valid runtime value.
    - If events are off, the warning may not be visible.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
      - fallback in `do_compile_expr/2`

    Required change:
    - In static/release/publish mode, unknown AST node in codegen must be a hard error.
    - In dev/REPL mode, fallback may exist only if explicitly marked diagnostic.
    - Never silently compile unknown language constructs to a normal value.

61. Nested containers in expression position are compiled and loaded at compile time, and failures become `:undefined`.
    Problem:
    - `do_compile_expr/2` handles `{:container, meta, body}` by dispatching and loading the nested module at compile time.
    - If compilation/loading fails, it returns `:undefined`.
    - This combines compile-time side effects with silent recovery.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
      - `{:container, meta, body}` branch in `do_compile_expr/2`

    Required change:
    - Nested container compile/load failure must be a hard compile error in static/final mode.
    - Avoid compile-time VM loading as part of expression compilation for trusted builds.
    - If nested containers are valid values, represent them in final Core/module metadata rather than as compile-time-loaded BEAM modules.

62. Binary literal fallback produces an empty binary.
    Problem:
    - `compile_bytes_literal/2` returns `{:bin, line, []}` for unknown input.
    - Unsupported or malformed byte literal content becomes `<<>>`.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
      - `compile_bytes_literal/2`

    Required change:
    - Unknown byte literal format must be a compile error.
    - Do not replace malformed data with empty binary.

63. Parser recovery skips unknown supervisor/app body tokens.
    Problem:
    - `parse_sup_items/3` and `parse_app_items/3` skip unknown leading tokens by parsing one expression and continuing.
    - This may hide malformed declarations inside structured containers.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
      - `parse_sup_items/3`
      - `parse_app_items/3`

    Required change:
    - In static/release mode, unknown items inside `sup`/`app` containers should be parse errors.
    - Recovery can exist for editor mode, but the parser must record an error and final compilation must fail.

64. Unknown binary specifier token becomes `Any`.
    Problem:
    - `parse_bin_specifier/1` returns `{:type, :any}` for unknown specifier tokens.
    - This is another parse-level fallback to dynamic typing.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
      - `parse_bin_specifier/1`

    Required change:
    - Unknown binary specifier should be a parse/type error in static mode.
    - If editor recovery needs a placeholder, use an error placeholder, not `Any`.

65. Decorator expressions infer as `Any`.
    Problem:
    - `do_infer(env, {:decorator, _meta, _})` returns `{:ok, :any, env}`.
    - Standalone or misplaced decorators can survive as dynamic values.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - Decorators should not be expression-level values in static mode.
    - Misplaced decorators should be parse/check errors.
    - Only recognized decorators attached to valid declarations should be accepted.

66. Misattached `@builtin` decorators can be silently dropped.
    Problem:
    - Parser comments say `@builtin` on indexed/GADT or alias forms is silently dropped because those forms lack an `attach_decorator` clause.
    - For prelude/builtin registration this is dangerous: a missing builtin marker changes Core behavior.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/program.ex

    Required change:
    - In prelude/static mode, a misattached or unsupported `@builtin` decorator must be a compile error.
    - Unknown builtin key or wrong declaration shape must reject.
    - Do not silently ignore builtin decorators.

67. SMT translator approximates untranslatable nodes as `true`.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.SMT.Translator.do_translate/1` fallback logs a warning and emits SMT `true`.
    - This can make an obligation weaker than the source predicate.
    - In refinement checking, weakening unknown syntax to `true` can incorrectly prove obligations.

    File:
    - lib/cure/smt/translator.ex
      - fallback `do_translate/1`

    Required change:
    - In static/final mode, untranslatable SMT AST must return an error/unknown that fails the obligation.
    - Never approximate an untranslatable predicate as `true` for trusted checking.
    - Dev/lint mode may warn, but must not use the result as proof.

68. Unknown function in SMT translation becomes an SMT comment, not a failure.
    Problem:
    - `do_translate({:function_call, ...})` returns `["(; unknown: #{name} ;)"]` for unknown functions.
    - Depending on surrounding syntax, this can generate malformed or vacuously weakened queries.

    File:
    - lib/cure/smt/translator.ex

    Required change:
    - Unknown predicate/function in a proof obligation must fail translation.
    - Supported uninterpreted functions should be declared explicitly and typed.
    - Unknown functions should not be represented as comments.

69. CoreBridge treats surface variables as globals.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.CoreBridge.to_core({:variable, ..., name})` lowers to `{:global, String.to_atom(name)}`.
    - In type-level predicates, many variables are local binders or parameters, not globals.
    - This can make type-level reduction/equality check the wrong term.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex

    Required change:
    - CoreBridge must take a context mapping source variables to de Bruijn indices or local references.
    - Free globals must be distinguished from local parameters.
    - Do not translate every variable string to a global atom.

70. CoreBridge interns arbitrary variable/function names as atoms.
    Problem:
    - `String.to_atom(name)` is used for surface names in type-level CoreBridge.
    - User source can create unbounded atoms during compilation.
    - This is also not hygienic for module-scoped names.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex

    Required change:
    - Use a symbol table/interning layer with bounded lifetime, or existing atoms only for known globals.
    - Preserve module qualification and source scope.
    - Avoid `String.to_atom/1` on arbitrary user names in untrusted package builds.

71. Source parsing/codegen uses `String.to_atom/1` widely on user-controlled names.
    Problem:
    - Parser, codegen, app/fsm/sup compilers, and project config convert many user strings into atoms.
    - This is normal for BEAM compilers to some extent, but it needs a policy because atoms are not garbage collected.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
    - lib/cure/types/type.ex
    - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex
    - lib/cure/types/protocol_registry.ex
    - lib/cure/app/*.ex
    - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
    - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex
    - lib/cure/project.ex
    - lib/cure/release.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/*.ex

    Required change:
    - For compiler-internal identifiers, use a controlled symbol table.
    - For external package/artifact decoding, use `String.to_existing_atom/1` only after validating against a known table.
    - For runtime string-to-atom APIs, mark unsafe/effectful or use existing atoms only.
    - Add package-build limits for number/length of generated atoms.

72. `String.to_atom` stdlib wrapper is unsafe and typed as pure total.
    Problem:
    - `Std.String.to_atom` wraps `:erlang.binary_to_atom/1`.
    - Creating atoms from arbitrary strings can exhaust the BEAM atom table.
    - The function is typed as a normal pure total conversion.

    File:
    - lib/std/string.cure
      - `to_atom`

    Required change:
    - Remove from checked stdlib or move to `Std.Unsafe.String`.
    - Prefer `to_existing_atom : String -> Result(Atom, Error)`.
    - If atom creation is kept, mark it unsafe/effectful and unavailable in total/static code.

73. Pair/tuple element access is partial but typed as total polymorphic.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Pair.element(index: Int, tuple: Tuple) -> T` wraps `:erlang.element/2`.
    - Out-of-range index raises.
    - The return type `T` is unconstrained and can be any type.

    File:
    - lib/std/pair.cure

    Required change:
    - Replace with:
      - dependent tuple index where possible,
      - `Option(T)` / `Result(T, Error)`,
      - or explicit unsafe partial API.
    - Do not allow arbitrary `T` from tuple element access in static/dependent code.

74. Map get extern is partial but typed as total.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Map.get` wraps `:maps.get/2`.
    - `:maps.get/2` raises if the key is absent.
    - It is typed as returning `V`.

    File:
    - lib/std/map.cure

    Required change:
    - Replace default `get` with safe:
      `get : Map(K,V) -> K -> Option(V)` or `Result(V, Error)`.
    - Keep raising form as `get_bang` in unsafe/partial runtime tier.
    - Static code should not treat partial map lookup as total.

75. `Std.Access` intentionally raises but lives in normal stdlib.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Access` uses `:erlang.error/1` and many `Any` APIs.
    - This is a dynamic runtime convenience module, not checked/static/dependent stdlib.

    File:
    - lib/std/access.cure

    Required change:
    - Move to dynamic/runtime tier.
    - Mark raising APIs as partial/unsafe.
    - Provide safe alternatives returning `Option`/`Result`.
    - Do not import into checked dependent prelude.

76. `Std.Test` assertion APIs raise but are typed as normal functions.
    Problem:
    - Assertion helpers call `:erlang.error/1`.
    - They are useful test APIs, but not total functions.

    File:
    - lib/std/test.cure

    Required change:
    - Put under test-only/runtime tier.
    - Mark as effectful/exceptional or return `Result`.
    - Exclude from total/static code.

77. `Std.Gen` randomness is effectful/stateful but mostly typed as ordinary functions.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Gen` wraps `:rand.uniform/1` and `:rand.seed/2`.
    - Randomness is nondeterministic/stateful.
    - It should not be pure or reducible in types.

    File:
    - lib/std/gen.cure

    Required change:
    - Mark random functions effectful/state-threaded.
    - Use an explicit RNG state type for pure deterministic generators, or an effect for ambient randomness.
    - Exclude ambient randomness from type-level computation.

78. `Std.System.system_time` and process/system operations need effect classification.
    Problem:
    - `Std.System.system_time`, `timestamp_ms`, `timestamp_us`, `self`, `node`, `system_info`, and `exit` are runtime/system operations.
    - Some are nondeterministic, some inspect VM state, and `exit` halts the node.

    File:
    - lib/std/system.cure

    Required change:
    - Mark time/system introspection as effectful or runtime-only.
    - `exit` should return `Never` or an effectful termination type, not `Atom`.
    - `system_info : Atom -> T` should be removed; use typed wrappers or `Dynamic`.

79. Process/actor/fsm/supervisor stdlib APIs are stateful but not consistently effect-marked.
    Problem:
    - Actor/FSM/Supervisor/Process functions spawn, stop, send, link, monitor, mutate trap-exit, and inspect process state.
    - Many signatures are ordinary functions returning `Pid`, `Atom`, `Any`, or `Bool`.

    Files:
    - lib/std/actor.cure
    - lib/std/fsm.cure
    - lib/std/supervisor.cure
    - lib/std/process.cure
    - lib/std/app.cure

    Required change:
    - Put these modules in runtime/effectful stdlib tier.
    - Add effects:
      - spawn/process state,
      - messaging,
      - registry/app state,
      - supervision side effects.
    - For static/dependent code, require explicit runtime boundary.

80. HTTP/JSON/Regex extern modules need tiering and partiality classification.
    Problem:
    - `Std.Http` is I/O.
    - `Std.Json.decode` may fail; encode can raise for unsupported values in runtime implementation.
    - `Std.Regex.compile_bang` raises.
    - Some APIs already return `Result`, but the module tier/effect story is not enforced.

    Files:
    - lib/std/http.cure
    - lib/std/json.cure
    - lib/std/regex.cure
    - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex
    - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_regex.ex

    Required change:
    - HTTP must be effectful.
    - JSON encode/decode should return `Result` where runtime failure is possible.
    - `compile_bang` belongs in unsafe/partial tier.
    - Static/dependent prelude should not import these by default.

81. Codegen import resolution trusts loaded BEAM modules, not checked Cure metadata.
    Problem:
    - `resolve_import/3` checks whether a loaded module exports a function/arity.
    - It does not check Cure type metadata, effects, totality, unsafe status, or checked artifact provenance.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

    Required change:
    - Static builds should resolve imports through checked Cure module metadata, not just BEAM exports.
    - BEAM export presence is insufficient evidence for type-safe calls.
    - Dynamic/interop imports should require explicit extern/unsafe boundary.

82. Compiler allows direct `Erlang` module reference.
    Problem:
    - `cure_module_to_atom("Erlang")` maps directly to `:erlang`.
    - This creates an escape hatch around typed extern declarations if callable through module syntax.

    File:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

    Required change:
    - Direct Erlang module calls should require explicit `@extern`/unsafe FFI declarations.
    - Static/dependent code should not be able to call arbitrary Erlang BIFs by module reference.

83. Derive expansion runs in codegen, after type checking.
    Problem:
    - `@derive` expansion is mentioned in codegen.
    - If derive creates functions after type checking, those generated functions are not checked in the ordinary checker pipeline.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
    - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

    Required change:
    - Derive expansion should happen before type checking.
    - Generated code must be typechecked like user code.
    - Or derive must produce checked Core/certified declarations.

84. Monomorphisation uses legacy type resolver and `Any` in specialization decisions.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Optimizer.Monomorphise` uses `Type.resolve/1`, defaults missing return type to `:any`, infers maps as `{:map, :any, :any}`, lambdas as `{:fun, param_types, :any}`, and skips/continues based on incomplete local inference.
    - This can produce or omit specializations based on the legacy gradual checker, not the dependent/static type information.

    File:
    - lib/cure/optimizer/monomorphise.ex

    Required change:
    - Monomorphisation should consume checked typed IR, not raw surface AST plus legacy resolver.
    - In static/dependent mode, do not specialize based on `Any`.
    - Recheck specialized definitions.

85. Pattern-match exhaustiveness warnings are not enough for total/static code.
    Problem:
    - `PatternChecker.check_match` warnings for non-exhaustive matches do not necessarily fail compilation.
    - Runtime code may raise for unmatched cases.
    - This is acceptable for partial runtime code, not total/static/type-level code.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/types/pattern_checker.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex

    Required change:
    - Static/total mode:
      - non-exhaustive match = error.
    - Runtime partial mode:
      - allowed only with explicit partial/unsafe marker.
    - Dependent mode:
      - use indexed coverage/impossibility, not only legacy pattern coverage.

86. `throw` is typed as `Never`, but effect/partiality is not enforced.
    Problem:
    - `do_infer({:throw, ...})` returns `:never`.
    - That is fine as a bottom type, but throwing is also an exception effect.
    - If effect checking is disabled or warning-only, exception-throwing code can inhabit total-looking functions.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
    - lib/cure/types/effects.ex
    - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

    Required change:
    - `throw` must add an exception effect that is enforced independently of event emission.
    - Total/type-level functions must reject `throw`.
    - `Never` should not erase the effect.

87. `PathRefinement` ignores unsupported guards.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.PathRefinement` explicitly ignores anything outside a small guard fragment.
    - That is fine for advisory narrowing, but not for trusted branch reasoning.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/path_refinement.ex

    Required change:
    - Keep as lint/advisory.
    - Trusted branch refinement must come from checked evidence:
      - Dec results,
      - indexed comparison results,
      - canonical equality,
      - Core pattern matching.
    - Unsupported guards in trusted refinement contexts should produce obligations or errors.

88. `PatternRefinement` emits warnings for ignored refinements.
    Problem:
    - There are `:refinement_ignored` diagnostics in pattern refinement.
    - Ignored refinements are not safe if the code relies on them for static/dependent correctness.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/pattern_refinement.ex

    Required change:
    - Advisory mode may warn and continue.
    - Static/dependent mode must not silently proceed when a required refinement cannot be represented.
