    - Convert ignored trusted refinements into errors or explicit obligations.

89. `CoreBridge` and SMT both create a parallel “type-level expression” semantics.
    Problem:
    - `CoreBridge` reduces some surface expressions through Core.
    - SMT translator proves other surface expressions through SMT.
    - Legacy checker also has its own type/refinement machinery.
    - These can disagree.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex
    - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex
    - lib/cure/smt/translator.ex
    - lib/cure/smt/solver.ex
    - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

    Required change:
    - There should be one authoritative type-level semantics:
      - Core normalization/conversion for definitional equality,
      - canonical obligations for propositional/refinement facts.
    - SMT may solve obligations only if the result is represented as checked evidence or explicit trusted unsafe evidence.
    - Surface expression reducers should not be authoritative.

90. Package publish reads source with bang functions and may package unchecked/partially built state.
    Problem:
    - `Project.Publisher` uses `File.read!` in several places.
    - It builds tar contents from source/files, not necessarily from a checked final artifact graph.
    - Errors become exceptions rather than structured build failures.

    File:
    - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex

    Required change:
    - Publish should be driven by a successful checked build result.
    - Use structured errors, not `File.read!`, in publish path.
    - Include final checked artifact hashes/metadata.
    - Do not package if any check/build/proof/certificate step is stale or missing.
91. Stdlib signature loading is not a checked prelude.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Stdlib` parses stdlib files and extracts signatures from metadata.
    - It does not type-check stdlib bodies before installing those signatures.
    - `load_module/2` silently drops a stdlib module on read/lex/parse/extract failure via `else _ -> acc`.
    - This can leave stdlib calls missing or typed through fallback paths.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex
      - `load_module/2`
      - `collect_signatures/1`
      - `signature_from_meta/1`
      - `build_signature/2`

    Required change:
    - Static/dependent mode must import a checked prelude, not parsed metadata.
    - Per-module stdlib load failure must be a hard error for checked builds.
    - Parsed signatures should be accepted only after the defining stdlib module checks successfully.
    - Dev/REPL may keep best-effort loading, but it must be mode-gated.

92. Stdlib signature extraction erases effect/totality/extern/unsafe metadata.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Stdlib.build_signature/2` returns only:
      `{:fun, param_types, ret_type}`
    - It does not preserve:
      - effects,
      - totality,
      - extern status,
      - partiality,
      - unsafe status,
      - erased arguments,
      - proof arguments,
      - checked/certified status.
    - Calls to stdlib functions therefore cannot enforce effect or totality boundaries reliably.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex

    Required change:
    - Stdlib signatures should carry a rich function type/metadata record:
      - params,
      - return,
      - effects,
      - totality,
      - purity,
      - extern/primitive/runtime status,
      - unsafe/dynamic tier,
      - checked artifact hash.
    - `Env.extend/3` should store that richer function metadata.

93. Stdlib signature builder defaults missing parameter and return types to `Any`.
    Problem:
    - `build_signature/2` maps malformed/missing parameter types to `:any`.
    - Missing return type also becomes `:any`.
    - This silently weakens stdlib APIs.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex
      - `build_signature/2`

    Required change:
    - In checked/static prelude mode:
      - missing parameter type = error,
      - malformed parameter = error,
      - missing return type = error unless inference/checking fills it,
      - no fallback to `Any`.
    - Dynamic stdlib mode may keep permissive behavior, but must be explicit.

94. Stdlib ADT/type extraction loses namespace and can collide.
    Problem:
    - `adt_from_container/1` converts an enum name to:
      `String.to_atom(String.downcase(name))`
    - It does not include the module namespace.
    - Two stdlib/user modules with the same enum name can collapse to the same legacy ADT atom.
    - This also diverges from dependent Core, where names should be qualified/stable.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex
      - `adt_from_container/1`

    Required change:
    - Type identities must be module-qualified.
    - Do not downcase type names into bare atoms.
    - Use a controlled symbol table or qualified Core name representation.

95. Type environment usage tracking does not work.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Env.lookup/2` calls `mark_used(env, name)` but discards the returned environment.
    - The function returns only the lookup result, so `env.used` is not updated.
    - This is mostly diagnostics/lint, but it means unused-variable warnings can be wrong.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/env.ex
      - `lookup/2`

    Required change:
    - Either return `{result, env}` from lookup, or remove usage mutation from lookup.
    - Do not pretend the current side-effect-free call updates `env.used`.

96. Scope popping silently no-ops on underflow.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Types.Env.pop_scope/1` returns the env unchanged if asked to pop the root scope.
    - That can hide checker bugs where scopes are unbalanced.

    File:
    - lib/cure/types/env.ex
      - `pop_scope/1`

    Required change:
    - In static/checker-internal mode, popping the root scope should be an internal error.
    - REPL/dev recovery may keep no-op behavior only if explicitly mode-gated.

97. Core environment silently overwrites definitions, families, and constructors.
    Problem:
    - `Env.add_def/5` uses `Map.put`.
    - `Inductive.declare/3` uses `Map.put` for families, constructors, and constructor-to-family mapping.
    - Duplicate definitions or constructor names silently replace earlier entries.
    - This can corrupt dependent checking, especially because constructor names are globally keyed by bare atom.

    File:
    - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
      - `Cure.Core.Env.add_def/5`
      - `Cure.Core.Inductive.declare/3`

    Required change:
    - Reject duplicate global definitions unless explicitly shadowing in a local elaboration phase.
    - Reject duplicate family names.
    - Reject duplicate constructor names, or namespace constructors by family/module.
    - Error message should include both original and conflicting owner.

98. Constructor namespace is global and unqualified.
    Problem:
    - `ctors` and `ctor_to_family` are maps keyed only by constructor atom.
    - Two types with a constructor named `Nil`, `Some`, `Ok`, `Error`, etc. cannot coexist safely.
    - Runtime emission also lowers nullary constructors to bare atoms.

    Files:
    - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
    - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
    - stdlib ADT files

    Required change:
    - Give constructors stable qualified identities:
      - `{module, family, ctor}`
      - or globally unique symbol IDs.
    - Surface names can remain short through resolution, but Core should not use bare atoms.
    - Runtime representation should avoid collisions, e.g. tagged tuples or qualified atoms for non-builtin constructors.

99. Nullary constructor emission collides with atoms and other constructors.
    Problem:
    - `Cure.Elab.Emit.lower/3` emits a nullary constructor as:
      `{:atom, line, name}`
    - That means a value constructor `Ok` and an atom `:Ok` have the same runtime representation.
    - Two unrelated nullary constructors with the same name also collide.
    - `Bool` is specially lowered to BEAM booleans, adding another representation exception.

    File:
    - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
      - `lower/3` for `{:ctor, name, args}`

    Required change:
    - Use a collision-free runtime representation for ADT constructors.
    - Possible policy:
      - only builtin `Bool` lowers to BEAM booleans,
      - all other nullary constructors lower to tagged tuples or qualified atoms.
    - Make atom/constructor interop explicit, not accidental.

100. Imported dependent globals may emit as local calls.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Elab.Emit.lower/3` lowers `{:global, name}` to either `name()` or `fun name/arity`.
     - It does not encode module qualification.
     - `compile_forms/3` intentionally emits only local definitions, but the env can contain imported definitions.
     - A local body referencing an imported global may emit as a call to a non-existent local function.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
       - `lower/3` for `{:global, name}`
       - `lower_app_spine/4`

     Required change:
     - Core globals need owning module metadata.
     - Emit local calls only for local definitions.
     - Emit remote calls or inline/certified references for imported definitions.
     - Final Core/env should distinguish local def, imported checked def, builtin primitive, and extern.

101. Erasure mishandles partial application of functions with erased arguments.
     Problem:
     - `Erase.erase/2` drops erased arguments only when the application spine has at least as many args as the callee quantity vector.
     - If a function is partially applied to an erased argument, `length(args) < length(quantities)`, so the erased argument is kept.
     - Example shape:
       `{A : Type 0} -> Int -> Int`
       partially applied to `A` should erase to the original function, not keep runtime `A`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/erase.ex
       - `erase/2` for `{:app, _, _}`

     Required change:
     - Erasure must drop erased arguments positionally even for partial applications.
     - Need an explicit representation for partially applied functions after erased args are removed.
     - Add tests for:
       - erased leading arg,
       - erased middle arg,
       - partial application ending before first present arg,
       - over-application after erased args.

102. Emit hole check currently erases before checking for holes.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Elab.Emit.reject_holes/2` computes:
       `body = Erase.erase(env, def_body(env, name))`
       then checks `Erase.has_hole?(body)`.
     - A hole inside an erased proof/index argument can disappear before rejection.
     - Under the new hole policy, unsolved proof holes may erase only if explicitly marked `unsafe`; ordinary unsolved proof holes must reject before erasure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
       - `reject_holes/2`

     Required change:
     - Check original body for holes before erasure.
     - Distinguish ordinary proof holes from explicitly unsafe proof holes before erasure.
     - Emit/codegen should never be the first place holes are rejected.

103. Relevance checking accepts missing quantity metadata.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Elab.Relevance.check/4` returns `:ok` when `quantities` is not a list.
     - For definitions that should carry erased arguments, missing quantity metadata can bypass the erasure-soundness check.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/relevance.ex
       - `check/4`

     Required change:
     - In final/static/dependent mode, a definition with parameters must have an explicit quantity vector.
     - Missing quantity metadata should be an internal error or default to all-present only at declaration construction, not inside the relevance checker.
     - Relevance checker should not silently accept malformed metadata.

104. Relevance checker treats unknown leaves as harmless.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Elab.Relevance.walk/4` falls through:
       `defp walk(_leaf, _depth, _site, _st), do: :ok`
     - New Core forms can be introduced and accidentally treated as relevance-neutral.
     - This is risky for erasure soundness.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/relevance.ex

     Required change:
     - Replace catch-all success with explicit known leaves only.
     - Unknown Core form in relevance checking should error in static/final mode.
     - Add an assay that fails when a new runtime-relevant Core node is not handled.

105. Emit application lowering assumes erased applications have already been normalized correctly.
     Problem:
     - `lower_app_spine/4` uses `present_arity(env, name)` and splits the already-erased args by count.
     - This is only safe if `Erase.erase/2` has removed erased arguments correctly and the remaining args are positionally aligned.
     - Because partial erased applications are currently suspect, this downstream code can call functions with wrong argument lists.

     File:
     - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
       - `lower_app_spine/4`
       - `present_arity/2`

     Required change:
     - Fix erasure first.
     - Then make emitter verify the emitted spine arity equals the erased function arity.
     - If a global has erased arguments, emitter should use the quantity vector, not only the count, when validating lowering.

106. Optimizer silently ignores unknown pass names.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Optimizer.run_pass/2` returns `{:ok, ast, 0}` for an unknown pass.
     - A misspelled optimization flag therefore silently does nothing.

     File:
     - lib/cure/optimizer.ex
       - `run_pass/2`

     Required change:
     - Unknown optimization pass should return `{:error, {:unknown_pass, pass}}`.
     - CLI/dev tooling may display a suggestion, but should not silently succeed.

107. Optimizer pass failures are not structured.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Optimizer.optimize/2` assumes every pass returns `{ast, count}`.
     - A pass error or malformed return crashes instead of producing a structured compile error.
     - Static/release builds should not depend on optimizer exceptions.

     File:
     - lib/cure/optimizer.ex
       - `apply_pass/3`
       - `apply_single/2`

     Required change:
     - Optimization passes should return `{:ok, ast, count}` or `{:error, reason}`.
     - Compiler should propagate optimizer errors.
     - Post-optimization typecheck should run in static/release/publish mode.

108. PGO stale-profile detection hashes only metadata, not the function body.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.PGO.Profile` says `def_hash` is `phash2` of the function metadata.
     - Changing a function body without changing metadata may keep the same profile entry.
     - PGO can then guide optimization using stale runtime data.

     File:
     - lib/cure/pgo/profile.ex
     - PGO loader/optimizer integration files

     Required change:
     - Hash the checked function body or final Core definition, not only metadata.
     - Include compiler version, optimization-relevant flags, and primitive/runtime version.
     - Drop profile entries when the body hash differs.

109. Guard simplification assumes guards are pure and terminating.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Optimizer.GuardSimplify` applies boolean algebra like:
       `P and P -> P`
       `true or P -> true`
       `false and P -> false`
     - If `P` contains a function call, effect, exception, nontermination, or partial operation, these rewrites change behavior.

     File:
     - lib/cure/optimizer/guard_simplify.ex

     Required change:
     - Simplify only guards proven pure, total, and compile-time-safe.
     - Use effect/totality metadata.
     - In static mode, reject effectful guards or do not simplify them.
     - In runtime mode, preserve evaluation behavior unless purity is known.

110. Pipe identity elimination is name-based and shadowing-unsafe.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Optimizer.PipeInline` rewrites `x |> identity` to `x` when the call name is `"identity"` or `"Std.Core.identity"`.
     - A local function named `identity` can shadow the stdlib function.
     - If the local identity is effectful/partial/non-identity, the rewrite is wrong.

     File:
     - lib/cure/optimizer/pipe_inline.ex

     Required change:
     - Resolve the callee to a checked global identity definition before rewriting.
     - Require proof or trusted metadata that the function is actually identity.
     - Do not optimize by unqualified string name.

111. Derived methods are generated with missing or weak type annotations.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Derive.derive_eq/3` generates params with empty metadata:
       `{:param, [], "a"}`
       `{:param, [], "b"}`
     - `derive_functor/3` leaves `f` untyped.
     - Several derived functions omit return types.
     - Legacy checker will infer/fallback through `Any`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Derived code must include complete parameter and return types.
     - Derive should run before type checking.
     - Generated definitions must be checked like user code.
     - In dependent/static mode, derive should produce Core or checked surface AST with no `Any` holes.

112. Derived Eq/Ord are semantically wrong for Cure’s type goals.
     Problem:
     - Derived Eq uses Erlang structural equality `a == b`.
     - Derived Ord uses Erlang term ordering and returns atoms `:lt`, `:gt`, `:eq`.
     - That does not respect user-defined Eq/Ord protocols, canonical Eq, indexed values, erased proofs, or proof-producing comparison.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex
       - `derive_eq/3`
       - `derive_ord/2`

     Required change:
     - Derived runtime equality should call the runtime equality protocol/dictionary, not Erlang universal equality.
     - Derived ordering should return `Ordering`, not atoms.
     - Do not derive Ord for fields without checked Ord implementations.
     - Do not confuse runtime Eq with propositional Eq.

113. Derived JSON can generate invalid JSON.
     Problem:
     - `derive_json/2` inserts field names directly into JSON string fragments:
       `"\"#{field}\":"`
     - It relies on nested `to_json` calls but does not validate field-name escaping or field implementations.
     - This is runtime string construction, not typed serialization.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex
       - `derive_json/2`

     Required change:
     - JSON derivation should build an intermediate JSON value or call a checked encoder.
     - Field names and string values must be escaped by the JSON library.
     - Generated `to_json` should require field-level JSON implementations.

114. Pattern compiler converts unknown patterns to wildcard-like output.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Compiler.PatternCompiler` has a fallback path for unknown patterns.
     - Comments indicate unknown patterns become wildcard/best-effort literal handling.
     - In static/final mode, unsupported pattern compilation should not silently widen to wildcard.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown pattern form = compile error in static/release mode.
     - Editor/dev mode may produce recovery patterns only if final compilation fails.
     - Add tests for unsupported pattern forms not becoming catch-alls.

115. Pattern compiler creates atoms from pattern variable names.
     Problem:
     - `PatternCompiler` uses `String.to_atom/1` for generated variable names and some literal/pin handling.
     - This is another unbounded atom creation path from source.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Use a bounded symbol table for generated compiler variables.
     - Validate and limit source-derived atom creation.
     - Atom literals should be explicit and possibly interned through a controlled atom table in package builds.

116. Export-types silently lies with `bytes` for unmappable types.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.ExportTypes.Protobuf.map_type/1` maps:
       - `nil` to `bytes`,
       - `Any` to `bytes`,
       - refinement/dependent/unmappable types to `bytes /* E068 ... */`.
     - It only emits warnings.
     - Generated protobuf schemas can therefore claim a field is arbitrary bytes when Cure had a richer or unknown type.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/export_types.ex
     - lib/cure/export_types/protobuf.ex

     Required change:
     - In normal export mode, unmappable type should be an error.
     - Provide an explicit `--lossy` or `--dynamic` export mode for `bytes` fallback.
     - `Any` should not silently become bytes in a supposedly typed schema.
     - Refinement/dependent erasure policy must be explicit.

117. Export-types silently skips aliases.
     Problem:
     - Protobuf emitter returns `""` for `kind: :alias`.
     - This can omit public type aliases from exported API with no hard failure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/export_types/protobuf.ex
       - `emit_def/1`

     Required change:
     - Either:
       - emit alias documentation/mapping,
       - expand aliases at use sites,
       - or error that alias export is unsupported.
     - Do not silently drop public types in checked export mode.

118. Export-types does not sanitize protobuf identifiers.
     Problem:
     - Message, enum, field, and variant names are emitted from Cure names with minimal transformation.
     - Reserved protobuf keywords, invalid characters, leading digits, duplicate field names after snake_case, and enum naming collisions are not checked.

     File:
     - lib/cure/export_types/protobuf.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate generated protobuf identifiers.
     - Detect duplicate names after transformation.
     - Escape/rename reserved words deterministically.
     - Report exact source type/field causing the export error.

119. Release builder skips missing applications and continues.
     Problem:
     - `copy_applications/5` records missing applications in `skipped`, logs a warning, and still returns `:ok`.
     - The release may be built without required applications.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `copy_applications/5`

     Required change:
     - In release mode, missing application = hard error.
     - `skipped` should be allowed only with an explicit dev/test option.
     - The final `.rel` should never silently omit required apps.

120. Release builder stores resolved app closure in the process dictionary.
     Problem:
     - `copy_applications/5` uses:
       `Process.put({__MODULE__, :resolved_apps}, ...)`
     - `resolved_apps/2` reads that process dictionary key later.
     - This is hidden mutable state and can leak between release builds in the same process if an error path skips cleanup.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex

     Required change:
     - Thread resolved applications explicitly through the release build data.
     - Avoid process dictionary for build-critical state.
     - Clear state in `after` if retained temporarily.

121. Release atom conversion can create new atoms from config.
     Problem:
     - `to_existing_atom/1` tries `String.to_existing_atom/1`, then falls back to `String.to_atom/1`.
     - Release config can create arbitrary atoms.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `to_existing_atom/1`

     Required change:
     - For release app names, require known/loaded application atoms or a bounded symbol table.
     - Unknown app names should be structured errors, not new atoms.
     - Avoid `String.to_atom/1` in package/release paths.

122. Lockfile reuse only checks top-level constraints.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.Lock.resolve_with_lock/2` verifies only top-level constraints against locked versions.
     - It does not verify:
       - transitive dependency constraints,
       - dependency list consistency,
       - hash presence,
       - registry metadata consistency,
       - whether path/git deps changed.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/lock.ex
       - `resolve_with_lock/2`

     Required change:
     - Validate the entire locked dependency graph.
     - Check transitive constraints against locked versions.
     - Verify required hashes for registry dependencies.
     - Mark lock stale when dependency metadata changes.

123. Lockfile parser/rendering is too fragile for package trust.
     Problem:
     - `parse_kv/1` assumes exactly one `=`.
     - Invalid lines can crash rather than return structured errors.
     - Rendered names/dependencies are interpolated without robust TOML escaping.
     - `String.to_atom(key)` interns arbitrary lockfile keys.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/lock.ex

     Required change:
     - Use structured parse errors.
     - Do not intern arbitrary lockfile keys as atoms.
     - Escape TOML strings properly.
     - Reject invalid lockfile schema rather than accepting defaults like `"0.0.0"`.

124. Registry base URL override mutates global application env.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.Registry.with_base_url/2` uses `Application.put_env/3`.
     - This is global VM state.
     - Concurrent registry operations/tests can observe each other’s base URL.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/registry.ex
       - `with_base_url/2`

     Required change:
     - Pass registry base URL through an explicit client/config struct.
     - Avoid global application env mutation in resolver/publisher paths.
     - Test-only overrides should be process-local or dependency-injected.

125. Registry manifests are not content-addressed in the same way tarballs are.
     Problem:
     - `fetch_manifest/2` fetches JSON over HTTP and accepts the decoded manifest shape.
     - `fetch_tarball/3` verifies tarball hash, but manifest content is not tied to a declared hash/signature at this layer.
     - Resolver decisions can therefore depend on mutable registry JSON unless higher layers pin it.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/registry.ex
       - `fetch_manifest/2`
       - `fetch_json/1`

     Required change:
     - Manifest metadata should be signed or content-addressed.
     - Lockfile should pin manifest hash as well as tarball hash.
     - Resolver should operate on verified registry snapshots.

126. Registry cache writes are non-atomic.
     Problem:
     - `write_to_cache/2` writes directly to the final cache path.
     - A crash or concurrent writer can leave a partial file.
     - Reads verify hash, which helps, but non-atomic writes still create unnecessary failure/race windows.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/registry.ex
       - `write_to_cache/2`

     Required change:
     - Write to a temp file, fsync if desired, then atomic rename.
     - Consider file locking or content-addressed immutable writes.
     - Keep read-side hash verification.

127. Final artifact decoders intern arbitrary names as atoms.
     Problem:
     - `Term.from_external/1` and `Serialize.decode/1` use `String.to_atom/1` for:
       - globals,
       - data family names,
       - constructor names,
       - primitive op names,
       - branch constructor names.
     - These formats may eventually be read from packages or external artifacts.
     - That is an atom exhaustion and namespace-hygiene risk.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/core/term.ex
     - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex

     Required change:
     - Decode through a symbol table.
     - Use existing known symbols only for trusted builtins.
     - Reject unknown symbols unless declared in the artifact’s signature table.
     - Avoid `String.to_atom/1` on untrusted artifact content.

128. Lexer atom literals intern source text immediately.
     Problem:
     - `lex_atom_or_colon/1` creates atom tokens using `String.to_atom/1`.
     - Source packages can create arbitrary atoms at lex time, before typechecking or safety policy.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex

     Required change:
     - Tokenize atom literals as strings/symbol IDs first.
     - Intern only after validation and with limits.
     - Provide separate APIs for:
       - existing BEAM atoms,
       - Cure symbolic atoms,
       - unsafe atom creation.

129. Source-name-to-atom conversion is pervasive and needs a compiler symbol table.
     Problem:
     - Parser, elaborator, codegen, protocol code, app/fsm/sup compilers, and project/release code call `String.to_atom/1` on source/config-derived names.
     - This is both a resource issue and a name-hygiene issue.

     Representative files:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
     - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex
     - lib/cure/elab/resolution.ex
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex
     - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex
     - lib/cure/app/resource.ex
     - lib/cure/app/verifier.ex
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/release.ex

     Required change:
     - Introduce a Cure symbol/name table.
     - Core should use symbol IDs or qualified names, not arbitrary BEAM atoms.
     - BEAM atoms should be generated only at final codegen for validated local symbols.
     - External/package decoding should never create atoms from unchecked strings.

130. Retired `if` syntax still compiles permissively.
     Problem:
     - `E085` says `if` is retired in favor of `pickup`, but the parser still accepts it for legacy sources.
     - That may be fine for migration, but static/release mode should not silently accept removed constructs.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/errors.ex
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Mode-gate retired syntax.
     - Dev/migration mode may accept and warn.
     - Static/release/publish mode should either reject or rewrite before checking, then check the rewritten AST.

131. Compiler docs/errors already admit some constructs previously compiled to `undefined`.
     Problem:
     - `E043` says raw `spawn`/`receive` had no code generation and previously compiled silently to `undefined`.
     - Codegen still has generic unknown-AST recovery elsewhere.
     - This indicates unsupported constructs need a hard final gate.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/compiler/errors.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
     - parser/codegen paths for concurrency primitives

     Required change:
     - Unsupported construct must be rejected before codegen.
     - Codegen should not contain fallback-to-value behavior in final mode.
     - Add regression tests for every retired/unsupported construct.

132. `@builtin` handling is intentionally permissive for user look-alikes.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Elab.Program` comments say ordinary user code declaring the same decorator is ignored in some cases.
     - For builtin registration, silent ignoring is dangerous because it can make a prelude declaration look checked while failing to register the intended builtin.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/elab/program.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - In checked prelude mode, malformed/misplaced/unknown `@builtin` must be an error.
     - In user modules, either forbid `@builtin` entirely or require an internal compiler mode.
     - Do not silently ignore builtin-looking decorators.

133. `Cure.Project.Json.encode/1` raises instead of returning structured errors.
     Problem:
     - Internal registry/signing JSON encoder raises `ArgumentError` for unsupported keys/values.
     - Registry/publish paths should return structured errors, not crash.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex

     Required change:
     - Add `encode_safe/1` returning `{:ok, json}` or `{:error, reason}`.
     - Use structured errors in registry/publisher/signing paths.
     - Keep raising version only for tests/internal assertions.

134. `Cure.Project.Json` has no depth/size limits.
     Problem:
     - Registry JSON decoding can parse arbitrarily nested arrays/objects.
     - This can be a resource risk for malicious or corrupted registry responses.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex
     - lib/cure/project/registry.ex

     Required change:
     - Add maximum JSON size before decode.
     - Add nesting-depth and element-count limits.
     - Registry responses should be schema-validated after decoding.

135. `Cure.Types.Stdlib` caches signatures in `:persistent_term` without version/config keying.
     Problem:
     - Cache key is fixed:
       `{__MODULE__, :signatures_v3}`
     - It does not include:
       - stdlib source dir,
       - Cure/compiler version,
       - static/dynamic mode,
       - feature flags,
       - dependency/prelude tier.
     - Changing stdlib source override or compiler mode can reuse stale signature data.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex

     Required change:
     - Include source-dir hash/compiler version/mode in cache key.
     - Or remove persistent cache from static builds.
     - `reload/0` should not be required for correctness.

136. Story/docs extraction silently skips parse failures.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Story.Outline` documentation says it reads/parses files silently and skips parse errors.
     - This is acceptable for docs previews, but not for generated design artifacts used as build/release evidence.

     File:
     - lib/cure/story/outline.ex

     Required change:
     - Keep best-effort docs mode separate.
     - Any docs/spec generation used by release/publish should report parse failures as errors.
     - Do not use skipped docs output as evidence of project completeness.

137. `Cure.Fix` silently reverts rewrites that change AST.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Fix` says any rewrite that changes the AST is silently reverted.
     - This is safe for not changing semantics, but poor for migration workflows because failed rewrites can disappear.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fix.ex

     Required change:
     - Report skipped/reverted rewrites explicitly.
     - Static migration tools should fail or summarize every unapplied fix.
     - Do not silently claim a file was fixed if semantic-preservation failed.

138. `Core.Serialize.build_all/1` is O(n²) and can be abused by large artifacts.
     Problem:
     - `build_all/1` appends with `acc ++ [t]` for every item.
     - Large serialized terms can become quadratic to decode.

     File:
     - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex

     Required change:
     - Accumulate reversed list and reverse once.
     - Add artifact size/depth limits for external decode.

139. `Core.Serialize` and external Core formats lack artifact schema/versioning.
     Problem:
     - Final Core S-expression encoding has no explicit version, feature set, universe policy, primitive registry version, or builtin table hash.
     - Independent re-validation needs to know exactly which language fragment is encoded.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/core/serialize.ex
     - lib/cure/core/term.ex
     - project artifact/publisher files

     Required change:
     - Add artifact header:
       - format version,
       - Cure core version,
       - universe policy,
       - builtin registry hash,
       - primitive registry hash,
       - final-Core invariant flags.
     - Reject unknown/unsupported versions.

140. `Env.certify/2` is public and does not prove certification.
     Problem:
     - `Env.certify/2` marks a global certified after only checking closedness.
     - It assumes legitimate callers only call it after `Kernel.validate_certificate/2`.
     - Public API makes accidental or malicious certification possible inside compiler code.

     File:
     - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex
       - `Cure.Core.Env.certify/2`

     Required change:
     - Make raw certification private or require an unforgeable certificate token returned by `Kernel.validate_certificate/2`.
     - At minimum, `certify/2` should re-run all final readiness checks:
       - definition exists,
       - body present,
       - type/body kernel-check,
       - totality certificate,
       - closed,
       - no holes,
       - no primitive equality,
       - no unresolved metas/levels.

141. Dependency/release config parsing creates atoms from unknown application names.
     Problem:
     - Release/app/resource config conversion uses `String.to_atom/1` in several places.
     - User package config can create atoms before validation.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
     - lib/cure/app/resource.ex
     - lib/cure/app/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse config names as strings first.
     - Validate against declared modules/apps/resources.
     - Intern only validated compiler-generated symbols.
     - Use existing atoms for OTP apps where appropriate.

142. Docs/doctest examples compile with `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Doc.Doctests` wraps expressions in:
       `fn main() -> Any = ...`
     - This is useful for examples, but it can mask type holes and dynamic fallback in documentation tests.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/doctests.ex

     Required change:
     - Checked doctest mode should infer or require precise types.
     - `Any` doctest wrapping should be dev/docs-only.
     - Static docs should fail when examples require dynamic fallback.

143. Cure.Blss/Advisor suggests `Any` as a quick fix.
     Problem:
     - Advisor has a fix kind:
       `"annotate as Any"`
     - That is actively counter to the static/dependent direction.

     File:
     - lib/cure/bless/advisor.ex

     Required change:
     - In static mode, do not suggest `Any`.
     - Prefer:
       - precise annotation,
       - sum type,
       - `Option`/`Result`,
       - explicit dynamic boundary,
       - proof obligation,
       - unsafe only when appropriate.

144. Doctor says missing Z3 means checks will be skipped.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Doctor` reports:
       “Z3 not found ... SMT-backed refinement checks will be skipped.”
     - That is acceptable for advisory mode, but not for final/static mode.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doctor.ex

     Required change:
     - Doctor should distinguish:
       - advisory SMT unavailable,
       - final/static SMT obligations cannot be discharged.
     - Static/release readiness should fail if required solver/certificate backend is unavailable.

145. PGO recorder uses public ETS.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.PGO.Recorder` uses public named ETS for profile counters.
     - Runtime profile data influences optimization.
     - Public mutation can poison PGO data.

     File:
     - lib/cure/pgo/recorder.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat PGO as untrusted optimization input.
     - Use protected/private ETS or a GenServer owner.
     - Validate profile data before use.
     - Never let PGO affect typechecking or final-Core validity.
146. Actor/FSM/App/Sup container compilers generate Elixir source strings and compile them directly.
     Problem:
     - `actor`, FSM callback-mode, `app`, and `sup` containers are compiled by generating Elixir source strings and feeding them to `Code.compile_string/2`.
     - This bypasses the normal Cure codegen path and makes these containers depend on a separate ad-hoc Cure-AST-to-Elixir translator.
     - Generated code is not represented as checked Cure Core or even normal Cure MetaAST after expansion.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Container callback bodies should lower through the ordinary Cure pipeline or a typed IR.
     - If Elixir source generation remains, it must be treated as an unsafe backend with:
       - structured errors,
       - post-generation validation,
       - no fallback-to-default behavior,
       - no unchecked expression subset.
     - Static/release mode should not trust untyped generated Elixir.

147. Generated Elixir diagnostics are suppressed.
     Problem:
     - Container compilers call:
       `Code.with_diagnostics([log: false], fn -> Code.compile_string(...) end)`
     - Diagnostics are discarded as `_diagnostics`.
     - Warnings in generated code can indicate bad patterns, unreachable clauses, unused variables, or compilation issues.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Capture diagnostics and surface them as Cure compiler diagnostics.
     - In release/static mode, generated-code warnings should either fail or require explicit policy.
     - Never discard generated backend diagnostics silently.

148. Generated Elixir compilation uses pattern matching that can crash instead of returning structured errors.
     Problem:
     - Container compilers expect:
       `[{^mod_atom, bytecode}] = result`
       `{:module, ^mod_atom} = :code.load_binary(...)`
     - If compilation returns a different module list, warnings-only result, no module, or a different module, the compiler crashes.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `{:error, reason}` on backend mismatch.
     - Include generated-module name, source span, and diagnostics.
     - Static/release builds should never crash out of the compiler process for malformed generated code.

149. Cure AST to Elixir translator falls back to `inspect(other)`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.FSM.Compiler.cure_ast_to_elixir/1` converts unsupported AST nodes to `inspect(other)`.
     - Unsupported Cure syntax in actor/FSM/app callbacks can become an Elixir literal/tuple/string instead of failing.
     - This is the generated-source equivalent of codegen’s old `:undefined` fallback.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
       - `cure_ast_to_elixir/1`

     Required change:
     - Unknown AST in callback translation must be a compile error in static/release mode.
     - Editor/dev recovery may produce a placeholder only if final compilation fails.
     - Add regression tests for unsupported callback AST not compiling.

150. Unknown generated-source operators default to `+` or unary `-`.
     Problem:
     - `binary_op_to_elixir/1` falls back to `"+"`.
     - `unary_op_to_elixir/1` falls back to `"-"`.
     - An unrecognized operator can silently change meaning into arithmetic.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown operator in generated callbacks must be an error.
     - Only a closed set of Cure operators should translate.
     - Do not default unknown operators to valid arithmetic.

151. Generated Elixir variable/function/attribute names are interpolated without a checked symbol layer.
     Problem:
     - `cure_ast_to_elixir/1` emits variable names, function names, and attribute names as raw strings:
       - `name`
       - `"#{name}(...)"`
       - `"#{obj}.#{attr}"`
     - This assumes the parser has fully sanitized all names and that Cure names map directly to valid Elixir identifiers.
     - It also bypasses Cure name resolution, module qualification, protocol dispatch, effects, and imports.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Generated code should use Erlang abstract forms or a typed backend IR, not raw interpolated source strings.
     - If string generation remains, every emitted identifier must come from a validated symbol table.
     - Function calls in callbacks should resolve through Cure’s checked environment.

152. Callback bodies are not clearly type/effect checked before generated-source compilation.
     Problem:
     - Actor/FSM/App callback clauses are rendered directly from parsed `match_arm` bodies into Elixir.
     - The ordinary Cure function checker may not see these callback bodies as checked functions with declared input/output/effects.
     - Effects like send/spawn/throw/IO can appear through generated source without the normal effect checker.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat callbacks as functions with explicit checked types:
       - `on_start`
       - `on_stop`
       - `on_message`
       - `on_transition`
       - `on_enter`
       - `on_exit`
       - `on_timer`
       - `on_phase`
     - Type and effect check each callback before backend generation.
     - Do not generate backend code from unchecked callback AST.

153. Actor callback return shapes are normalized permissively.
     Problem:
     - `do_on_start` treats any unrecognized return as `{:ok, other}`.
     - `do_on_message` treats any returned value as a new payload by merging it into actor state.
     - Bad callback returns therefore become valid state transitions.

     File:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Define explicit callback return types:
       - `ActorStartResult`
       - `ActorMessageResult`
       - `ActorStopResult`
     - Reject callbacks whose bodies do not produce the required type.
     - Runtime fallback should not reinterpret arbitrary values as state.

154. Actor unmatched messages silently preserve state.
     Problem:
     - Generated actor `do_on_message` defaults:
       `_ -> payload`
     - An actor with missing or misspelled message patterns silently ignores messages.
     - This may be fine as a runtime policy, but it should be explicit.

     File:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Require explicit catch-all if ignored messages are intended.
     - Static analysis should warn or error on uncovered message patterns depending on mode.
     - For typed actor messages, exhaustiveness should be checked against the message ADT.

155. FSM callback return shapes are normalized permissively.
     Problem:
     - Generated FSM transition handling accepts only some tagged forms, but unexpected callback returns often fall through to failure/no-op behavior rather than a static error.
     - `on_start` accepts `{:ok, partial}` and merges arbitrary partial data into state.
     - Lifecycle callbacks default to `:ok`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Define typed callback result ADTs:
       - `TransitionResult`
       - `LifecycleResult`
       - `TimerResult`
     - Check callback bodies statically.
     - Reject arbitrary partial maps/values unless explicitly dynamic.

156. FSM with no non-wildcard transition can start in `:unknown`.
     Problem:
     - `determine_initial_state/1` returns `nil` when no non-wildcard transition exists.
     - Compilation then uses:
       `String.to_atom(String.downcase(initial_state || "unknown"))`
     - This creates a runtime state `:unknown` that was not declared by the user.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Missing initial state should be a verifier error unless explicitly declared.
     - Do not invent `:unknown` as a runtime state.
     - FSM state space should be closed and declared.

157. FSM invalid next-state callback result falls back to the first allowed target.
     Problem:
     - In callback mode:
       `actual_next = if next in allowed_targets, do: next, else: hd(allowed_targets)`
     - If a callback returns an illegal next state, the runtime silently chooses the first allowed transition.
     - That hides callback bugs and makes transition choice depend on table ordering.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Illegal callback next state should be a compile-time type error if possible.
     - At runtime, it should return a structured failure, not pick `hd(allowed_targets)`.
     - For nondeterministic transitions, require explicit disambiguation.

158. FSM transition ambiguity is warning-only.
     Problem:
     - `check_on_transition_coverage/7` returns `:ambiguous_transition_warning` for multiple targets from the same `(from,event)`.
     - Callback mode may rely on user code to disambiguate.
     - In static mode, ambiguity should be explicit in the transition result type.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - If multiple transitions share `(from,event)`, require:
       - mutually exclusive guards,
       - explicit priority,
       - or a callback result type that names the chosen next state.
     - Ambiguous transition tables should be errors in static/release mode.

159. FSM hard-event validation ignores wildcard interactions.
     Problem:
     - `check_hard_events/4` counts distinct events only where `t.from == from and from != "*"`.
     - Wildcard transitions can still interact with a hard state/event.
     - A wildcard outgoing event may violate the “hard event must be sole outgoing event” policy.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Hard-event validation must include wildcard transitions that apply to the same source state.
     - Expand wildcard edges before checking hard-state exclusivity.
     - Add tests for hard events plus wildcard transitions.

160. FSM runtime event sending returns `:ok` even when dispatch may fail.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.FSM.Runtime.send_event/2` and `/3` call `dispatch_event/3`, record the event, and return `:ok`.
     - If the pid is dead, unregistered, not an FSM, or dispatch falls back incorrectly, callers still see success.
     - Event history can record events that were never processed.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/runtime.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `Result`/`{:ok | :error}` from event dispatch.
     - Record history only after successful dispatch or explicitly record attempted dispatch separately.
     - Std.Fsm signatures should expose the possibility of failure.

161. FSM runtime falls back to raw `:gen_statem.cast` when module cannot be resolved.
     Problem:
     - `dispatch_event/3` uses raw `:gen_statem.cast(pid, event)` if registry lookup fails.
     - This lets untyped/ad-hoc pids receive FSM events without checked module metadata.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/runtime.ex

     Required change:
     - For typed/static FSM APIs, require a registered checked FSM module.
     - Unknown pid/module should return an error.
     - Raw fallback belongs only in dynamic/unsafe runtime APIs.

162. Actor and FSM registries use public ETS tables.
     Problem:
     - Actor and FSM runtimes create named public ETS tables:
       - `:cure_actor_registry`
       - `:cure_fsm_registry`
     - Any process can mutate registry contents.
     - Registry data affects lookup, event dispatch, health checks, caller metadata, and module resolution.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/runtime.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/runtime.ex

     Required change:
     - Use protected/private ETS owned by the runtime GenServer.
     - Or route all reads/writes through the GenServer.
     - Treat registry data as runtime state, not public mutable build/type metadata.

163. Actor/FSM runtime lookup by name is not uniqueness-checked.
     Problem:
     - `lookup(name)` uses `:ets.match_object` and returns the first matching entry.
     - Multiple running actors/FSMs can share the same name unless insertion enforces uniqueness.
     - The returned pid can depend on ETS ordering.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/runtime.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/runtime.ex

     Required change:
     - Enforce unique names at spawn time or return `{:error, :ambiguous}`.
     - Registry should index by name directly if names are meant to be unique.
     - Std.Actor/Std.Fsm lookup signatures should reflect failure/ambiguity.

164. App compiler accepts unexpected root `start_link/0` return values as success.
     Problem:
     - Generated app `start_root_module/1` does:
       `other -> {:ok, other}`
     - If the root supervisor returns a malformed value, the app still starts successfully.

     File:
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Accept only OTP-valid start return shapes.
     - Malformed root returns should become `{:error, {:invalid_start_return, other}}`.
     - Static app verification should require root module to implement checked `start_link/0`.

165. App lifecycle hooks silently default to `:ok`.
     Problem:
     - `on_start`, `on_stop`, and `on_phase` generated cases all include catch-all `_ -> :ok` or equivalent defaults.
     - Missing pattern coverage silently succeeds.
     - Bad hook return values are usually treated as success.

     File:
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Define checked hook result types.
     - Require explicit catch-all if default success is intended.
     - Bad hook return should be a type error or runtime error, not silent success.

166. App phase matching duplicates `phase_args` in the generated tuple.
     Problem:
     - `render_phase_block/2` generates:
       `case {phase_args, start_type, phase_args} do`
     - The first and third tuple elements are identical.
     - If the intended pattern is `(args, start_type, phase_args)` or similar, this makes two distinct positions indistinguishable.

     File:
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Clarify the intended three-argument phase pattern shape.
     - Generate a tuple with the actual distinct values.
     - Add tests where phase clauses distinguish first and third arguments.

167. Sup compiler applies defaults even when metadata is malformed.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Sup.Compiler` uses defaults when extraction fails:
       - invalid strategy -> `:one_for_one`
       - invalid intensity -> `3`
       - invalid period -> `5`
       - invalid shutdown -> `5000`
     - This is safe only if verifier always runs and hard-fails before compilation.
     - Direct compiler calls can bypass verifier and silently change semantics.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/sup/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Compiler should either call the verifier itself or reject malformed metadata directly.
     - Defaults should apply only to omitted fields, not malformed fields.
     - Static/release path must prove verifier ran.

168. Sup compiler defaults missing child module to `"Unknown"`.
     Problem:
     - `render_child_spec/1` uses:
       `module_path = Keyword.get(meta, :module, "Unknown")`
     - If verification is skipped, the generated supervisor can reference `Cure.Actor.Unknown` or similar.

     File:
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Missing child module must be a compile error.
     - Never invent `"Unknown"` module names in backend output.
     - Add backend-level assertions even if verifier exists.

169. Sup compiler creates child IDs from arbitrary strings as atoms.
     Problem:
     - `render_child_spec/1` does:
       `String.to_atom(id)`
     - Child IDs are user-controlled.
     - This contributes to unbounded atom creation and namespace collision.

     File:
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Use validated symbol IDs or strings for child IDs where OTP permits.
     - If atoms are required, intern through the compiler symbol table with limits.
     - Reject invalid/duplicate IDs before codegen.

170. Sup verifier converts variable names to atoms.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Sup.Verifier.literal_atom/1` accepts variables as atoms via:
       `String.to_atom(name)`
     - A misspelled unquoted variable can become a valid atom instead of being rejected as an expression.

     File:
     - lib/cure/sup/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Require explicit atom literals for strategy/restart/shutdown options.
     - Do not treat arbitrary variable identifiers as atom literals in static mode.
     - If soft keywords are desired, parse them as dedicated tokens/AST.

171. App verifier converts phase names and literals to atoms.
     Problem:
     - `literal_atom_value/1` and `to_atom_safe/1` call `String.to_atom/1`.
     - App phase names and app metadata can intern arbitrary atoms.

     File:
     - lib/cure/app/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Phase names should be validated symbols, not arbitrary atoms.
     - Use existing declared phase names or compiler symbol IDs.
     - Avoid `String.to_atom/1` in verifier paths.

172. Legacy ADT parameterized constructor arguments are registered as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - In the legacy type checker, parameterized ADT variants are registered with:
       `param_types = List.duplicate(:any, length(raw_params))`
     - The comment says return type is the load-bearing fact.
     - This means `Some("x")`, `Some(1)`, and `Some(anything)` can share the same constructor typing regardless of declared parameter types.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `register_adt_container/4`

     Required change:
     - Constructor parameter types must come from the variant declaration.
     - Generic ADTs should instantiate type parameters, not erase argument types to `Any`.
     - Static/dependent mode should reject malformed constructor arguments.

173. Legacy ADT identity lowercases type names into bare atoms.
     Problem:
     - `register_adt_container/4` uses:
       `String.to_atom(String.downcase(name))`
     - This loses module namespace and case distinctions.
     - `Foo.Bar.Option` and `Other.Option` can collapse.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Type identities must be qualified and stable.
     - Do not use downcased bare atoms for ADT identity.
     - Align legacy ADT naming with Core qualified symbols or retire legacy ADT path from static mode.

174. PascalCase expression heuristic treats any uppercase identifier as a constructor.
     Problem:
     - Codegen lowers a bare PascalCase variable in expression position as a nullary ADT constructor:
       `constructor?(name)` based only on first character case.
     - This is not environment-driven.
     - A local uppercase binding or imported value can be miscompiled as a constructor.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_variable/2`
       - `constructor?/1`

     Required change:
     - Constructor resolution must use the checked environment.
     - Do not infer constructors from naming convention in backend codegen.
     - Parser/name resolver may use case as syntax sugar, but final codegen needs resolved symbols.

175. Qualified constructor codegen discards the module path.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Nat.Z()` is compiled using only the last segment:
       `qualified_ctor_tail(name)`
     - Runtime tag remains bare.
     - Qualified constructors from different modules collide if their last segment matches.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

     Required change:
     - Qualified constructor identities must preserve module/family qualification.
     - Runtime representation should be collision-free.
     - Bare constructor tags should be resolved only after proving uniqueness in scope.

176. Record construction codegen accepts malformed field forms into map fields.
     Problem:
     - `compile_record_construction/3` maps known field forms to `:map_field_assoc`, but the fallback is:
       `other -> other`
     - A malformed record field can be emitted directly into Erlang map syntax, producing invalid or surprising backend forms.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown record field form should be a codegen error.
     - Record construction must receive only checked field associations.
     - Backend should assert field-form invariants.

177. Record default compilation uses atom conversion for field names.
     Problem:
     - `apply_record_defaults/4` uses:
       `String.to_atom(field_name)`
     - Field names are source-derived.
     - This adds another atom creation path and bypasses a symbol table.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

     Required change:
     - Field names should be compiler symbols or validated atoms.
     - Record runtime representation must define whether field keys are atoms, strings, or qualified symbols.
     - Avoid arbitrary `String.to_atom/1`.

178. `assert_type` accepts `Any` as expected type and therefore proves nothing.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer({:assert_type, ...})` succeeds when:
       `expected == :any`
     - In static/dependent code, `assert_type(expr, Any)` can mask inference failure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Static mode should either forbid `assert_type(..., Any)` or require explicit dynamic boundary.
     - `assert_type` should not be used to silence unresolved types in final artifacts.

179. Pickup guards allow `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `type_check_pickup_clause/2` accepts guard type `:any`:
       `when gt in [:bool, :any]`
     - This lets non-boolean or unknown guard expressions control flow.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Static/final mode: pickup guards must be exactly `Bool`.
     - Dynamic mode may allow `Any` with a warning.
     - Dependent mode should elaborate guards into Dec/evidence where needed.

180. Record update accepts unknown fields through `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `resolve_record_field/3` can return `:any`.
     - Record update accepts update value when expected type is `:any`.
     - Unknown field updates can pass type checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown record field in update = error in static/final mode.
     - `Any` should not be the fallback field type.
     - Record type metadata must be complete before checking updates.

181. Attribute access on unknown field returns `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer({:attribute_access, ...})` returns:
       `resolve_record_field(env, obj_type, field_name)`
     - If the field cannot be resolved, it can become `:any`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown field access should be a type error in static/final mode.
     - Dynamic access should require a dynamic/unsafe boundary.
     - Missing field diagnostics should include object type and field name.

182. Std.Json `Obj(List(JsonPair))` runtime encoder expects tuple pairs.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Json` declares:
       `Obj(List(JsonPair))`
       and `JsonPair` is a record with fields `key` and `value`.
     - Runtime encoder in `:cure_std_json.encode/1` expects object entries shaped like:
       `{k, v}`
     - A `JsonPair{key: k, value: v}` record compiles to a map-like record, not necessarily a two-tuple.

     Files:
     - lib/std/json.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex

     Required change:
     - Align the runtime representation:
       - either `Obj(List((String, Value)))`,
       - or make encoder accept `%{__struct__: :json_pair, key: k, value: v}`.
     - Add round-trip tests for `Obj([pair("x", Str("y"))])`.

183. Std.Json decoder widens all integers to Float.
     Problem:
     - `:cure_std_json.to_value/1` converts integers using:
       `{:num, n * 1.0}`
     - This loses integer precision for large JSON integers.
     - `Value.Num(Float)` cannot represent JSON’s integer/decimal distinction safely.

     Files:
     - lib/std/json.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex

     Required change:
     - Represent JSON numbers precisely:
       - `JsonInt(Int)` / `JsonFloat(Float)`,
       - decimal string,
       - arbitrary precision number type,
       - or explicit lossy policy.
     - Do not silently widen large integers to floats in checked code.

184. Std.Json encode can raise despite signature `Value -> String`.
     Problem:
     - Runtime `encode(other)` raises `ArgumentError`.
     - Surface signature says:
       `fn encode(v: Value) -> String`
     - If representation mismatch or unchecked dynamic values reach it, it is partial.

     Files:
     - lib/std/json.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex

     Required change:
     - If `Value` is truly closed and checked, remove the catch-all raise and make impossible cases unreachable.
     - If dynamic values can reach it, return `Result(String, JsonError)` or mark partial/unsafe.
     - Runtime and type representation must agree.

185. Std.Time formatting/conversion uses raising DateTime functions.
     Problem:
     - `format_iso8601/1` uses `DateTime.from_unix!/2`.
     - Invalid or out-of-range microsecond values raise.
     - Surface signature says:
       `format_iso8601 : Instant -> String`

     Files:
     - lib/std/time.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_time.ex

     Required change:
     - Either prove `Instant.micros` is in valid range via refinement/dependent constructor,
       or return `Result(String, ParseError)`,
       or mark as partial.
     - Do not expose raising runtime conversion as pure total.

186. Std.Time zone parser accepts out-of-range explicit offsets.
     Problem:
     - `canonical_zone/1` accepts any `+HH:MM` / `-HH:MM` shape and parses HH/MM without range validation.
     - `+99:99` becomes an offset rather than an error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_time.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate:
       - hours range,
       - minutes range,
       - maximum legal UTC offset if desired.
     - Return `OutOfRange` / `InvalidFormat` instead of accepting impossible offsets.

187. Std.String repeat is partial for negative counts.
     Problem:
     - `Std.String.repeat` wraps `:binary.copy/2`.
     - Negative counts raise.
     - Signature says:
       `repeat : String -> Int -> String`

     File:
     - lib/std/string.cure

     Required change:
     - Use `NonNegativeInt` for the count,
       or return `Result(String, Error)`,
       or define negative count behavior explicitly.

188. Std.String split documentation and implementation disagree.
     Problem:
     - Docs say split on every occurrence of `sep`.
     - Implementation wraps `:binary.split/2`, which does not split globally unless given the `:global` option.
     - This is not a type-soundness issue, but it is a stdlib contract bug.

     File:
     - lib/std/string.cure

     Required change:
     - Either call `:binary.split/3` with `[:global]`,
       or change docs/name to `split_once`.
     - Add tests for `"a,b,c"`.

189. Std.Math `safe_div` returns `0` on division by zero.
     Problem:
     - `safe_div(a, b)` returns `0` when `b == 0`.
     - This hides failure as a valid numeric value.
     - The name “safe” is misleading for dependent/static code.

     File:
     - lib/std/math.cure

     Required change:
     - Replace with:
       - `checked_div : Int -> Int -> Option(Int)`
       - `div_or : Int -> Int -> Int -> Int`
       - `div : Int -> NonZeroInt -> Int`
     - Avoid silently choosing `0` for invalid division.

190. Std.Core generic comparisons are unconstrained.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Core` defines:
       `eq(x: T, y: T) -> Bool = x == y`
       `lt(x: T, y: T) -> Bool = x < y`
       and related generic comparisons.
     - This bypasses protocol constraints and lets arbitrary `T` use runtime term comparison/equality.

     File:
     - lib/std/core.cure

     Required change:
     - Generic equality/comparison should require protocol/dictionary evidence:
       - `Eq(T)`
       - `Ord(T)`
     - Or restrict these helpers to concrete primitive types.
     - Do not use runtime universal comparison as checked polymorphic comparison.

191. Std.Eq runtime protocol uses Float equality directly.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Eq` has:
       `impl Eq for Float`
       `fn eq(a: Float, b: Float) -> Bool = a == b`
     - Float equality has NaN/precision edge cases and should not feed proof-relevant equality.

     File:
     - lib/std/eq.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep runtime float equality clearly separated from propositional equality.
     - Consider `PartialEq` / `ApproxEq` for floats.
     - Do not allow Float Eq evidence to imply canonical `Eq`.

192. Std.Refine predicates are runtime Bool helpers, not proofs.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Refine` exposes predicates such as:
       `positive? : Int -> Bool`
       `percentage? : Int -> Bool`
       `probability? : Float -> Bool`
     - These are useful runtime predicates, but they are not evidence that a refinement holds.

     File:
     - lib/std/refine.cure

     Required change:
     - Split:
       - runtime predicates,
       - checked smart constructors returning refined values or `Result`,
       - proof-producing Dec/evidence APIs.
     - Do not treat predicate calls as trusted refinement proofs without generated obligations.

193. Std.Iter is entirely dynamic via `Atom -> Any`.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Iter` models iterators as `Atom -> Any`.
     - This is a pragmatic lazy stream encoding, but it is not statically precise.
     - It also uses sentinel atoms like `:step`.

     File:
     - lib/std/iter.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep in dynamic/runtime stdlib tier.
     - For static/dependent stdlib, define:
       `Iterator(T)` or `Stream(T)` with a typed step result:
       `Unit -> Option((T, Iterator(T)))`
     - Avoid `Any` and `Atom` sentinels in checked code.

194. Std.Gen `one_of` can call `rand_uniform(0)` for empty lists.
     Problem:
     - `one_of(list, default)` does:
       `Std.List.nth(list, rand_uniform(Std.List.length(list)) - 1, h)`
       inside branch `[h | _]`, so empty lists are avoided.
     - That branch is safe, but the type does not express non-empty input when default is not desired.
     - More generally, generator APIs use runtime randomness under ordinary function signatures.

     File:
     - lib/std/gen.cure

     Required change:
     - Add `NonEmptyList(T)` variants for APIs that require non-empty collections.
     - Classify ambient random functions as effectful/stateful.
     - Pure generator APIs should thread RNG state explicitly.

195. Std.Pair `element` is an arbitrary cast as well as partial indexing.
     Problem:
     - `element(index: Int, tuple: Tuple) -> T` both:
       - may raise for bad index,
       - returns arbitrary `T`.
     - This is stronger than just “partial”; it is a dynamic cast.

     File:
     - lib/std/pair.cure

     Required change:
     - For static code, replace with typed tuple projections.
     - Dynamic tuple element access should return `Option(Dynamic)` or require unsafe.
     - Do not allow caller-chosen `T`.

196. Std.Map `keys` and `values` claim arbitrary element types.
     Problem:
     - `keys(map: Map) -> List(K)`
     - `values(map: Map) -> List(V)`
     - But `Map` is not parameterized at the type level here.
     - The caller can instantiate `K`/`V` arbitrarily.

     File:
     - lib/std/map.cure

     Required change:
     - Make `Map(K,V)` a real parameterized type,
       or return `List(Dynamic)` in dynamic mode.
     - Do not expose unbound polymorphic return types from unparameterized runtime maps.

197. Std.Set inherits untyped Map representation.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Set` is represented as plain `Map` with `true` values.
     - Functions like `to_list(set) -> List(T)` recover arbitrary `T` from an unparameterized map.

     File:
     - lib/std/set.cure

     Required change:
     - Define `Set(T)` as an abstract/parameterized type.
     - Or keep `Std.Set` dynamic-only.
     - Do not let untyped map keys become arbitrary static `T`.

198. Std.Show is unconstrained and can format arbitrary `T` only through runtime shape assumptions.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Show` has protocol-like APIs, but some helpers use direct runtime conversion externs.
     - If generic show is exposed without evidence, it risks another arbitrary `T` operation.

     File:
     - lib/std/show.cure

     Required change:
     - Require `Show(T)` dictionary/protocol evidence for generic show.
     - Concrete primitive conversions are fine as typed primitive globals/externs.
     - Do not infer showability from arbitrary runtime values.

199. Pattern compiler binary segment fallback uses `:default` for missing or non-integer sizes.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Compiler.PatternCompiler` uses `:default` when segment sizes are missing or not integer.
     - Unsupported binary pattern annotations can become Erlang default binary matching semantics.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Static/release mode should reject unsupported binary segment sizes/specifiers.
     - Editor recovery may use defaults only while marking the AST invalid for final compilation.

200. Pattern compiler unknown call in pattern position becomes wildcard.
     Problem:
     - Comment says:
       “Unknown call in pattern position -- best effort: wildcard.”
     - This can make a malformed constructor pattern match too broadly.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/pattern_compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown pattern call should be a compile error in static/release mode.
     - Constructor patterns must resolve through checked ADT metadata.
     - No unknown pattern should widen to wildcard in final code.

201. Parser top-level recovery tolerates unknown lines by consuming expressions.
     Problem:
     - Parser has recovery paths for unknown top-level lines, supervisor bodies, and app bodies.
     - Recovery is valuable for LSP/editor mode, but final compilation should fail if recovery happened.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Carry a `recovered?` / parse-error flag through the parse result.
     - Static/release/publish mode rejects recovered parses.
     - LSP mode may return partial AST plus diagnostics.

202. Decorator attachment silently drops unsupported forms.
     Problem:
     - Parser comments say decorators attached to indexed/GADT/alias forms can be silently dropped because there is no `attach_decorator` clause.
     - This can make annotations like `@builtin`, `@derive`, or future `@unsafe` disappear.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Unsupported decorator target must be a diagnostic.
     - In static/release mode, unsupported/misplaced decorator = error.
     - No decorator that changes semantics may be silently ignored.

203. OTel tracing helpers swallow instrumentation errors.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Otel` catches/rescues errors in several tracing helpers and returns `:ok`.
     - This is fine for observability, but should be explicitly isolated from compiler correctness.

     File:
     - lib/cure/otel.ex

     Required change:
     - Keep tracing best-effort, but ensure compiler/checker decisions never depend on tracing success.
     - Add comments/tests that OTel failures cannot change compile/check results.
     - Do not use tracing success as build evidence.

204. PGO profile loading skips individual file failures.
     Problem:
     - PGO loading says individual file failures are skipped.
     - PGO is untrusted optimization input, so skipping is acceptable only if optimization never affects type soundness.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/pgo.ex
     - lib/cure/pgo/profile.ex
     - lib/cure/optimizer.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat missing/corrupt PGO as optimization-only.
     - Recheck after PGO-guided optimization.
     - Report skipped profiles in verbose/release diagnostics.

205. Antigen triage catches exceptions broadly.
     Problem:
     - `Antigen.Triage` rescues/catches during shrinking/triage.
     - For a testing tool this can be useful, but broad catch can hide generator or oracle bugs.

     File:
     - lib/antigen/triage.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - expected candidate rejection,
       - generator crash,
       - oracle crash,
       - infrastructure crash.
     - Infrastructure crashes should fail the assay, not become ordinary non-reducing candidates.

206. SMT solver cold PGO hint can turn `:unknown` into `:sat`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.SMT.Solver.check_sat/3` has:
       `:unknown when pgo_hint == :cold -> :sat`
     - That is catastrophic if used for trusted refinement checking: unknown becomes satisfiable.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/solver.ex

     Required change:
     - PGO must never change logical solver results.
     - Solver result policy must be:
       - `sat`,
       - `unsat`,
       - `unknown`,
       exactly as returned/derived.
     - Performance hints may adjust timeout/retry strategy, not truth value.

207. SMT process treats unrecognized solver output as `:unknown`, then higher layers may weaken it.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.SMT.Process.parse_result/1` returns `:unknown` for unrecognized output.
     - Higher layers sometimes treat unknown as warning/advisory.
     - Combined with translator fallback-to-true, this weakens obligations.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/smt/process.ex
     - lib/cure/smt/solver.ex
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown output is fine as a solver-level result.
     - Static/final obligation checking must reject unknown.
     - Include raw solver output in diagnostics for auditability.

208. `Cure.Fix` semantic-preservation check can silently hide unapplied rewrites.
     Problem:
     - Rewrites that change the AST are reverted.
     - That is conservative, but users/agents may think a migration succeeded when nothing changed.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fix.ex

     Required change:
     - Report every reverted rewrite.
     - Agent/migration mode should produce an explicit “not applied” list.
     - Do not summarize a file as fixed when all meaningful changes were reverted.

209. Formatter best-effort mode must never be used as syntax validation.
     Problem:
     - Formatter catches parse/format errors and preserves input.
     - That is correct for formatting, but it must not be reused as evidence that a file is valid.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/formatter.ex

     Required change:
     - Keep formatter non-destructive.
     - Static/release parser validation must be separate and strict.
     - Formatting success/failure should not affect compile correctness.

210. Generated callback source has no source-span mapping back to Cure AST.
     Problem:
     - Actor/FSM/App/Sup generated Elixir errors point into generated strings.
     - There is no robust mapping from generated Elixir line/column back to Cure callback source spans.
     - This makes generated-backend diagnostics hard to trust or act on.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/actor/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/fsm/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/app/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/sup/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Prefer AST/abstract-form generation with metadata.
     - Or embed source maps from Cure spans to generated Elixir spans.
     - Compile errors in generated callback code should report the original Cure clause.
211. FSM type-safety checker is only a warning stub.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.FSM.TypeSafety.check/1` returns `{:ok, warnings}`.
     - For non-FSM input it returns `{:ok, []}`.
     - It checks only duplicate transitions, wildcard conflicts, and self-loops.
     - It does not actually type-check guards, actions, callback return shapes, payload mutations, hard-event invariants, or state data.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/type_safety.ex

     Required change:
     - Rename this to advisory lint, or make it a real checker.
     - Static/release FSM compilation should fail on type-safety errors.
     - Non-FSM input should be rejected, not accepted with zero warnings.
     - Integrate with the ordinary Cure checker or typed callback IR.

212. FSM “type-safety” findings are warnings even when they are semantic conflicts.
     Problem:
     - Duplicate transitions and wildcard shadowing can change runtime behavior.
     - Current checker returns them as warnings.
     - Static/release mode should not accept ambiguous transition tables by default.

     File:
     - lib/cure/fsm/type_safety.ex

     Required change:
