     - Split findings into:
       - errors: duplicate deterministic transition, illegal target, missing initial state, invalid hard-event shape
       - warnings: benign self-loop, unused state, advisory wildcard note
     - Compilation mode should decide whether warnings fail, but semantic conflicts should be errors.

213. Protocol message payload types are parsed as raw strings and never type-checked.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Protocol.Parser.parse_msg/1` parses `Pong(Int)` into args like `["Int"]`.
     - These are not resolved through Cure’s type checker.
     - Unknown, misspelled, dependent, refined, or module-qualified types in protocols are just strings.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/protocol/parser.ex
     - lib/cure/protocol/script.ex
     - lib/cure/protocol/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Protocol payload types must parse as Cure type ASTs or references.
     - Verify each payload type in the checked environment.
     - Protocol projection should preserve checked payload type metadata.
     - Static actor/FSM protocol integration must use typed messages, not tag strings only.

214. Protocol parser and protocol script disagree about supported constructs.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Protocol.Script` models `:choose` and `:offer`.
     - The parser documentation says `choose` is reserved/future.
     - The verifier documentation claims active/passive branch properties, but parser cannot construct those branches from source.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/protocol/parser.ex
     - lib/cure/protocol/script.ex
     - lib/cure/protocol/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Either remove `choose`/`offer` from the trusted protocol representation until implemented,
       or implement parser + verifier support together.
     - Unsupported protocol constructs should be rejected, not partially modeled.

215. Protocol verifier does not enforce choose/offer active/passive semantics.
     Problem:
     - `Script` docs say:
       - `choose` branches must share the same first-step sender.
       - `offer` branches must share the same first-step receiver.
     - `Cure.Protocol.Verifier.verify/1` only checks role usage, role membership, and reachability.
     - There is no active/passive branch check.

     File:
     - lib/cure/protocol/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Add branch-shape checks:
       - choose branch active role is unique and declared,
       - offer branch passive role is unique and declared,
       - empty branches are rejected or explicitly modeled,
       - branch labels/messages are distinguishable where required.
     - Add protocol assay/test cases for invalid branch ownership.

216. Protocol verifier documentation promises self-deadlock checks that are not implemented.
     Problem:
     - The moduledoc says every non-`END` projected state has an outgoing transition.
     - `verify/1` does not call a self-deadlock/dead-end check.
     - A reachable non-terminal projected state with no outgoing edges can pass if it is reachable.

     File:
     - lib/cure/protocol/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Add projected dead-end checks.
     - Distinguish intentional terminal `END` from accidental terminal states.
     - Return protocol violations for reachable non-`END` states with no outgoing transitions.

217. Protocol projection treats third-party messages as local “idle” transitions.
     Problem:
     - `Script.project/2` adds an `idle ...` transition for messages where the projected role is neither sender nor receiver.
     - This keeps global step alignment but may falsely make an uninvolved role progress through states.
     - That can hide local stuttering behavior and alter temporal properties.

     File:
     - lib/cure/protocol/script.ex

     Required change:
     - Decide whether projections are synchronous global traces or local participant automata.
     - For local participant FSMs, third-party messages should usually be stutter/self-loop or omitted, not forced state advancement.
     - Make this semantic choice explicit and test temporal properties under it.

218. Protocol loop projection is fragile and likely state-order dependent.
     Problem:
     - `Script.do_project/5` for `{:loop, body}` projects the body into `sub_trans`, then computes:
       - `head = first element of sub_trans`
       - `first = List.last(sub_trans)`
       - loop edge from `head.to` to `first.from`
     - Because transitions are accumulated in reverse in parts of the projector, this is easy to get backward.
     - A wrong loop edge changes reachability and temporal checks.

     File:
     - lib/cure/protocol/script.ex

     Required change:
     - Normalize transition order before computing loop edges.
     - Explicitly track loop entry and loop exit state instead of inferring from list head/last.
     - Add tests for one-step and multi-step loops per role.

219. Temporal `always` can return success when the search bound is exceeded.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Temporal.Checker.do_always/6` returns `{:ok, :holds}` when `depth > bound`.
     - That makes a safety property look proven even though exploration was truncated.
     - The module docs claim safety is exhaustive over reachable states, but the implementation has a bound cutoff.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Safety checking should explore the full finite reachable graph without depth cutoff.
     - If a bound is used anyway, exceeding it must return `{:error, :bound_exceeded}`, not `holds`.
     - Add tests where violation occurs only after the current bound.

220. Temporal model construction does not validate initial state or edge targets.
     Problem:
     - `from_fsm/2` builds `states` from transition sources/targets, but `check/3` does not verify:
       - initial is non-nil,
       - initial is in states,
       - every edge source/target is in states,
       - states list is non-empty.
     - Empty transition lists can produce `%{initial: nil, states: [], edges: %{}}`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Add model validation before checking.
     - Empty model should be an error unless explicitly allowed.
     - Initial state must be a declared state.
     - Edge targets/sources must be declared states.

221. Temporal liveness counterexamples are underspecified.
     Problem:
     - `do_eventually/6` and `do_until/7` return `{:violation, []}` when the frontier is exhausted.
     - That loses the witness/counterexample context.
     - For tooling and safety review, an empty trace is not actionable.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Return a meaningful trace or a structured proof of reachable exhaustion.
     - Include visited states and the property that failed.
     - For bounded failures, distinguish:
       - real finite counterexample,
       - inconclusive due to bound.

222. Temporal `until` evaluates subformulae multiple times and can hide errors.
     Problem:
     - `do_until/7` calls `do_check(q, ...)` and `do_check(p, ...)` inside `match?`.
     - If either returns an error, `match?` just returns false and the code can fall through to `{:violation, trail}`.
     - Errors from subformula checking are therefore not reliably propagated.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Evaluate `q` and `p` once each and explicitly handle `{:error, reason}`.
     - Do not convert formula/model errors into ordinary temporal violations.

223. Trace journal uses unsafe `binary_to_term/1` for replay files.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Journal.load/1` reads a file and calls `:erlang.binary_to_term(bin)`.
     - Replay files are path-supplied and can be attacker-controlled.
     - `binary_to_term/1` without `[:safe]` can deserialize unsafe Erlang external terms and intern atoms.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/journal.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `:erlang.binary_to_term(bin, [:safe])` at minimum.
     - Prefer a versioned JSON/S-expression trace format.
     - Validate entry shape:
       `{actor_id, state_before, event, state_after, timestamp_us}`
     - Do not accept arbitrary terms as trace entries.

224. Trace journal table is public ETS.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Journal.ensure_started/0` creates:
       `:ets.new(@table, [:named_table, :public, :ordered_set, ...])`
     - Any process can insert, delete, or rewrite trace entries.
     - Replay and debugging output can be forged or corrupted.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/journal.ex

     Required change:
     - Use protected/private ETS owned by a process.
     - Or route journal writes through a GenServer.
     - Treat trace data as untrusted unless signed/validated.

225. Trace replay ignores event-send failures.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Replay.replay/3` calls:
       `_ = send_event(pid, event)`
     - It then checks state after a fixed sleep.
     - If sending fails, the process is dead, or the cast is lost, replay continues as if delivery happened.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/replay.ex

     Required change:
     - Check and propagate `send_event/2` result.
     - Prefer synchronous checked dispatch for replay.
     - Do not use fixed sleeps as delivery confirmation.
     - Return structured replay failure when an event cannot be delivered.

226. Trace replay treats failed transitions as warnings in output but still returns `{:ok, results}`.
     Problem:
     - Replay outcomes such as `{:error, :state_unchanged}` or `{:error, {:unexpected_state, got}}` are accumulated inside an overall `{:ok, results}`.
     - Callers must inspect every result manually.
     - Automated regression use can accidentally treat failed replay as successful.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/replay.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `{:error, failures, results}` or similar when any replay step fails.
     - Keep `{:ok, results}` only when all steps match.
     - CLI should exit non-zero on replay mismatch.

227. Trace replay can call arbitrary module `start_link/0`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Replay.replay/3` accepts any module atom and calls `module.start_link()`.
     - There is no check that the module is a checked Cure FSM module or that it matches the trace.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/replay.ex

     Required change:
     - Require checked FSM metadata in the trace or replay command.
     - Verify module identity/hash matches the traced module.
     - Reject arbitrary modules unless explicit unsafe/debug mode is requested.

228. Runtime tracer uses global `:dbg.p(:all, ...)`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Trace.start/2` enables tracing for all processes:
       `:dbg.p(:all, [:call, :return_to])`
     - This can perturb the whole VM and capture unrelated calls.
     - For release or CI instrumentation, this is too broad.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/trace.ex

     Required change:
     - Scope tracing to a pid/module/function set.
     - Require explicit opt-in for VM-wide tracing.
     - Ensure tracing failures cannot affect compiler correctness.

229. Trace signature registry is public ETS and untrusted.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Observe.Trace` stores declared signatures/effects in public ETS.
     - Any process can mutate the table.
     - Trace output can therefore display forged types/effects.

     File:
     - lib/cure/observe/trace.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat trace signatures as display-only.
     - Use protected/private ETS or checked artifact metadata.
     - Never use trace registry data for type checking, optimization, or release decisions.

230. Std.Actor signatures claim plain values where runtime returns error tuples or nil.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Actor.spawn : Atom -> Pid`, but `actor_spawn/1` can return `{:error, reason}`.
     - `Std.Actor.lookup : String -> Pid`, but runtime can return `{:error, :not_found}`.
     - `Std.Actor.get_state : Pid -> Any`, but runtime can return `nil` or `{:error, :dead}`.

     Files:
     - lib/std/actor.cure
     - lib/cure/actor/builtins.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `Result(Pid, ActorError)` / `Option(Pid)` / `Result(State, ActorError)`.
     - Do not hide spawn/lookup/state failures behind signatures that claim success.
     - Mark actor APIs effectful/runtime-only.

231. Std.Fsm signatures claim atoms/tuples/pids where runtime returns errors or nil.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Fsm.spawn : Atom -> Pid`, but builtins can return `{:error, reason}`.
     - `Std.Fsm.lookup : String -> Pid`, but builtins can return `{:error, :not_found}`.
     - `Std.Fsm.state : Pid -> Atom`, but builtins can return `{:error, reason}`.
     - `Std.Fsm.caller : Pid -> Pid`, but builtins can return `nil`.

     Files:
     - lib/std/fsm.cure
     - lib/cure/fsm/builtins.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `Result`/`Option` return types.
     - Separate dynamic convenience APIs from checked APIs.
     - Do not claim failed runtime FSM operations return valid `Pid`, `Atom`, or `Tuple`.

232. Std.Supervisor signatures claim plain values where runtime returns errors or nil.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Supervisor.start : Atom -> Pid`, but `sup_start/1` can return `{:error, reason}`.
     - `Std.Supervisor.lookup : Atom -> Pid`, but `sup_lookup/1` returns `nil` on miss.
     - `which_children` / `count_children` can return error tuples, but signatures claim `List(Tuple)` / `Map`.

     Files:
     - lib/std/supervisor.cure
     - lib/cure/sup/builtins.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `Result(Pid, SupervisorError)` and `Option(Pid)`.
     - Do not erase OTP error cases.
     - Mark supervisor APIs effectful/runtime-only.

233. Std.App signatures claim `Atom` / `Any` where runtime returns errors or nil.
     Problem:
     - `ensure_all_started`, `start`, `stop`, and `start_phase` can return error tuples, but signatures claim `Atom`.
     - `get_env` can return `nil`, but signature is `Any`.
     - `app_module/1` falls back to creating `"Cure.App." <> Macro.camelize(...)` atoms for unknown apps.

     Files:
     - lib/std/app.cure
     - lib/cure/app/builtins.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `Result(Unit, AppError)`, `Option(Dynamic)`, or typed app-env APIs.
     - Avoid dynamic fallback module construction in checked APIs.
     - Mark app environment mutation as effectful.

234. Process APIs are effectful but exposed as ordinary externs.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Process.link`, `unlink`, `monitor`, `demonitor`, `trap_exit`, `exit`, and `self` interact with process state/signals.
     - Signatures are ordinary functions returning `Atom`, `Bool`, `Ref`, or `Pid`.
     - `exit(pid, reason)` sends an exit signal but is typed like a value-producing pure function.

     Files:
     - lib/std/process.cure
     - lib/cure/process/builtins.ex

     Required change:
     - Classify process APIs as effectful/runtime-only.
     - `exit` should expose a process/exit effect and a precise return type.
     - Type-level and total code must reject these APIs.

235. HTTP runtime ignores `:inets.start/0` failures.
     Problem:
     - `:cure_std_http.ensure_inets/0` returns `:ok` for any result other than `:ok` or already-started.
     - A failure to start the HTTP runtime is ignored, and the later request error becomes less precise.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_http.ex

     Required change:
     - Return and propagate `{:error, {:inets_start_failed, reason}}`.
     - Expose this through `Std.Http`’s `Result`.
     - Do not silently continue when required runtime services fail to start.

236. HTTP header shape is unchecked at the runtime boundary.
     Problem:
     - `:cure_std_http.do_request/4` maps headers with:
       `fn {k, v} -> ... end`
     - If Cure passes a malformed list element, the runtime crashes with a function-clause error.
     - `Std.Http` should guarantee a header pair type or runtime should validate.

     Files:
     - lib/std/http.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_http.ex

     Required change:
     - Define a checked `Header` record or tuple type.
     - Validate runtime headers and return `HttpError` for malformed input.
     - Avoid accepting raw `List(Tuple)` / dynamic pairs in checked HTTP APIs.

237. Regex invalid-pattern behavior silently degrades to no-op results.
     Problem:
     - `is_match` returns `false` when pattern resolution fails.
     - `run` returns `None`.
     - `scan` returns `[]`.
     - `replace` returns the input unchanged.
     - `split` returns `[input]`.
     - These all hide invalid regex patterns as ordinary no-match/no-op outcomes.

     Files:
     - lib/std/regex.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_regex.ex

     Required change:
     - APIs accepting raw pattern strings should return `Result`.
     - Keep no-error APIs only for already-compiled `Regex`.
     - Invalid pattern should be distinguishable from legitimate no-match.

238. Regex compiled representation is an unchecked map.
     Problem:
     - A compiled regex is any map with `%{__struct__: :regex, handle: re}`.
     - `resolve/1` accepts that shape and passes `handle` to `:re.run`.
     - A forged map with a bad handle can crash or produce runtime errors.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_regex.ex

     Required change:
     - Use an opaque runtime type or validate the handle.
     - Static Cure code should not be able to forge internal regex records.
     - Runtime should return `Result` on invalid handles.

239. CRDT operations use process dictionary state.
     Problem:
     - ORSet `or_add/3` generates tags using `Process.get/put`.
     - That makes `or_add` stateful and process-local.
     - `Std.CRDT` presents CRDT operations as ordinary pure functions.

     Files:
     - lib/std/crdt.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_crdt.ex

     Required change:
     - Thread a replica/tag generator state explicitly, or mark `or_add` effectful.
     - If deterministic pure CRDTs are required, tags must be supplied as arguments.
     - Do not reduce/process-dictionary CRDT operations in type-level contexts.

240. CRDT signatures do not enforce non-negative increments.
     Problem:
     - Runtime `g_increment`, `pn_increment`, and `pn_decrement` require `by >= 0` in guards.
     - Surface signatures use `by: Int`.
     - Negative values cause function-clause failure rather than a typed error/result.

     Files:
     - lib/std/crdt.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_crdt.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `Nat`, `NonNegativeInt`, or refinement-backed smart constructors.
     - Or return `Result(Counter, Error)` for negative input.
     - Do not expose guarded runtime failure as total `Int -> ...`.

241. CRDT merge/value functions rely on Erlang term ordering for generic values.
     Problem:
     - `or_value/1` sorts arbitrary elements with `Enum.sort()`.
     - `lww_merge/2` breaks ties on atom node ordering.
     - `mv_merge/2` breaks ties using `va >= vb` for arbitrary values.
     - Generic `T` may not have a meaningful or stable total order.

     Files:
     - lib/std/crdt.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_crdt.ex

     Required change:
     - Require `Ord(T)` / `Ord(Node)` evidence where ordering is needed.
     - Or avoid sorting/tie-breaking on arbitrary user values.
     - Make merge laws explicit and test them under the chosen ordering assumptions.

242. CRDT record types hide their actual representation with unconstrained `T`.
     Problem:
     - `GCounter.counts: T`, `ORSet.entries: T`, `MVRegister.writes: T`.
     - These fields are really maps/sets with specific shapes.
     - The surface type lets callers instantiate internal representation fields as arbitrary `T`.

     File:
     - lib/std/crdt.cure

     Required change:
     - Make CRDT records opaque.
     - Or give fields precise parameterized types:
       `Map(Node, Nat)`, `Map(T, Set(Tag))`, etc.
     - Static code should not construct invalid CRDT internals.

243. App/resource files convert arbitrary config into atoms.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.App.Resource` converts app names, registered names, env keys, and values with `String.to_atom/1`.
     - Config-derived atoms are created during build/release resource generation.

     File:
     - lib/cure/app/resource.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse app config as strings first.
     - Validate against declared app/env schema.
     - Use controlled symbol table or existing atoms only.
     - Avoid arbitrary atom creation from project configuration.

244. Project dependency writer reads dependency maps using `String.to_atom/1`.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.write_lockfile` / dependency rendering path fetches map values by converting string keys to atoms.
     - This conflates string-keyed and atom-keyed dependency metadata and interns unknown keys.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex

     Required change:
     - Normalize dependency metadata to a single schema at parse time.
     - Do not convert arbitrary keys to atoms during rendering.
     - Reject unknown dependency keys rather than preserving them vaguely.

245. Signing trusted-key file silently drops malformed lines and malformed public keys.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.Signing.parse_trusted/1` ignores any line that does not match the regex.
     - It also ignores invalid hex entries.
     - A corrupted trust file can partially load without a hard warning/error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/signing.ex

     Required change:
     - Trust file parsing should return `{:ok, keys}` or `{:error, diagnostics}`.
     - Invalid lines or malformed keys should fail in install/release verification mode.
     - Include line numbers in diagnostics.

246. Signing writes trust files non-atomically.
     Problem:
     - `write_trusted/1` writes directly to `trusted.toml`.
     - A crash or concurrent write can leave a partially written trust file.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/signing.ex

     Required change:
     - Write to a temp file and atomic rename.
     - Consider file locking.
     - Preserve permissions.

247. Signing key handles are not sanitized before path construction.
     Problem:
     - `generate_keypair/1`, `load_private/1`, and `load_public/1` use:
       `Path.join(keys_dir(), handle <> ".sec/.pub")`
     - A malicious handle containing path separators or `..` could escape the intended key directory.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/signing.ex

     Required change:
     - Restrict handles to a safe grammar.
     - Reject path separators, `..`, NUL, empty strings, and overly long handles.
     - Use normalized path checks before writing.

248. Transparency verification can degrade to unverified success.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.Transparency.verify/3` returns `{:ok, :unverified}` when the log is unreachable unless `:strict_transparency` is set.
     - That may be acceptable for dev, but not for release/publish/package-trust mode.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/transparency.ex

     Required change:
     - Make strict transparency mandatory for release/publish/verified installs.
     - Dev mode may allow unverified installs with explicit warning.
     - Lockfile should record whether transparency was verified.

249. Transparency chain verification does not require contiguous indices or genesis policy.
     Problem:
     - `verify_chain/1` sorts entries by `"index"` and checks hash links.
     - It does not require indices to be contiguous.
     - It does not validate that the first entry has the expected genesis previous hash.
     - Missing entries can go unnoticed if the remaining chain links internally.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/transparency.ex

     Required change:
     - Require contiguous indices from a trusted checkpoint or genesis.
     - Verify first previous hash policy.
     - Pin a log checkpoint/root in the lockfile or registry metadata.

250. Transparency lookup verifies the entire supplied log but not inclusion against a signed checkpoint.
     Problem:
     - `do_verify/4` checks that a matching entry exists and that the supplied list chains internally.
     - If the registry supplies a truncated but internally consistent log containing the target entry, the client may accept it.
     - There is no signed tree head/checkpoint at this layer.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/transparency.ex

     Required change:
     - Verify inclusion against a signed log checkpoint/tree head.
     - Persist and compare checkpoints to detect rollback.
     - Record verified log index/hash in the lockfile.

251. Resolver treats invalid transitive dependency constraints as generic conflicts.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Project.Resolver.extend_active/4` turns parse failure for dependency constraints into `:conflict`.
     - The user gets a version conflict rather than “package metadata has invalid constraint”.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/resolver.ex

     Required change:
     - Return structured error:
       `{:invalid_dependency_constraint, package, dep, constraint, reason}`
     - Do not backtrack away metadata corruption as if it were ordinary version incompatibility.

252. Resolver loses conflict explanations during backtracking.
     Problem:
     - When solving a candidate fails, `try_candidates/6` discards the nested reason and tries the next candidate.
     - If all candidates fail, the final error is only `{:version_conflict, name, constraints}`.
     - This makes dependency failure diagnosis weak.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/resolver.ex

     Required change:
     - Accumulate conflict causes.
     - Report the dependency chain that caused failure.
     - Differentiate:
       - no matching version,
       - invalid metadata,
       - transitive conflict,
       - missing package.

253. Resolver accepts registry entries without validating their shape.
     Problem:
     - `solve/3` expects entries shaped like `%{version: v, deps: deps}`.
     - Bad registry entries can crash or behave unpredictably.
     - There is no schema validation before resolution.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/resolver.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate registry entry schema before solving.
     - Reject missing/invalid `version` or `deps`.
     - Do not let malformed registry data raise inside resolver.

254. Pattern exhaustiveness treats pin patterns as wildcards.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.PatternChecker.classify_pattern/1` maps `{:pin, _, _}` to `:wildcard`.
     - A pinned value pattern does not cover all values; it covers one runtime value.
     - This can make a match look exhaustive when it is not.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/pattern_checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Classify pins as singleton/equality-constrained patterns where possible.
     - If the pinned value is not statically enumerable, treat it as non-exhaustive unless a true wildcard exists.
     - Do not let pins satisfy coverage of infinite or finite types.

255. Pattern exhaustiveness treats unknown constructor calls as wildcards.
     Problem:
     - A function-call pattern whose name is not PascalCase constructor-like becomes `:wildcard`.
     - Malformed pattern calls can therefore make a match exhaustive.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/pattern_checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown pattern call should be an error in static/final mode.
     - Constructor recognition must use checked ADT metadata, not naming convention.
     - Advisory mode may recover, but final compilation must fail.

256. Pattern redundancy calculation is broken and discards redundant indices.
     Problem:
     - `check_redundancy/2` tries to collect redundant patterns after a wildcard.
     - The reduce branch for `seen_wildcard == true` does not append the current index.
     - It then returns `:exhaustive` even when `all_redundant` is non-empty.
     - Redundancy information is lost.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/pattern_checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Fix redundant-index collection.
     - Return `{:redundant, indices}` or include redundancy alongside exhaustiveness.
     - Static mode should optionally error on unreachable branches.

257. Pattern checker only hardcodes Option/Result/List/Bool.
     Problem:
     - `required_shapes/1` knows only:
       `Bool`, `Result`, `Option`, `List`.
     - User ADTs and indexed data are not enumerated.
     - Unknown ADT-like types fall back to `nil`, which means “needs wildcard”.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/pattern_checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Coverage should use checked ADT/constructor metadata from the environment.
     - User-defined ADTs must be included.
     - Indexed data coverage belongs in the Core/dependent pattern checker, not this hardcoded legacy path.

258. Typed-hole synthesizer is string-based and can suggest ill-typed code.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Synth` compares type strings with shallow regex matching.
     - `Option(T)` matches `Option(Int)` merely by head name.
     - It has a hand-maintained stdlib catalogue that may not match actual checked stdlib signatures.
     - It emits expression strings, not checked AST/Core candidates.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/synth.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat this as an editor hint engine only.
     - Do not use synthesized candidates as proof/checker output.
     - For trusted filling, synthesize typed AST/Core and re-run the checker.
     - Catalogue entries should be generated from checked stdlib metadata.

259. Typed-hole synthesizer effect filtering depends on hand-written catalogue effects.
     Problem:
     - Synth candidates carry effects from a static list.
     - If stdlib effect metadata changes, suggestions become stale.
     - It can suggest effectful code as pure or omit required effects.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/synth.ex

     Required change:
     - Pull effects from checked function metadata.
     - Recheck candidate effects before applying.
     - In static mode, never trust catalogue effects.

260. Legacy equality rewrite is a blind structural substitution over surface AST.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Equality.rewrite/2` replaces any subtree definitionally equal to `a_ast` with `b_ast`.
     - It does not account for binders, variable capture, context, motive typing, or direction/equality proof erasure in Core.
     - It is separate from canonical builtin `Eq`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/equality.ex

     Required change:
     - Remove this from the trusted path.
     - Surface rewrite should elaborate to canonical Eq eliminator/transport.
     - Any remaining surface-AST rewrite helper should be advisory/migration-only.

261. Legacy equality runtime value `:cure_refl` is still exposed as proof machinery.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Equality.refl_runtime_value/0` returns `:cure_refl`.
     - This continues the token-proof model that conflicts with canonical checked Eq.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/equality.ex

     Required change:
     - Remove token proof runtime representation from trusted equality.
     - Erased proofs should not need a globally meaningful atom.
     - If a unit value is needed after erasure, it must not be accepted as evidence.

262. Refinement construction resolves malformed base types through the legacy resolver.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Refinement.from_type_annotation/2` calls `Cure.Types.Type.resolve(base_ast)`.
     - The legacy resolver can fall back to `:any`.
     - A malformed refinement base can therefore become `{x : Any | P}`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/refinement.ex

     Required change:
     - Static mode must parse/resolve refinement bases strictly.
     - Unknown base type should reject.
     - Refinements should lower to Core obligations or checked smart constructors.

263. Refinement satisfiability result type cannot represent unknown.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Refinement.satisfiable?/1` is spec’d as `boolean()`.
     - SMT satisfiability can be `unknown`, timeout, unavailable, or malformed.
     - Collapsing this to boolean encourages unsafe defaults.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/refinement.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `:sat | :unsat | :unknown | {:error, reason}` or equivalent.
     - Static/final mode must reject unknown/error.
     - Diagnostics should explain solver status.

264. `Std.Http` response record representation is assumed by runtime, not enforced.
     Problem:
     - Runtime returns a map:
       `%{__struct__: :response, status: ..., headers: ..., body: ...}`
     - Surface `Response` record must compile to exactly that shape.
     - If record representation changes or namespacing is added, HTTP silently diverges.

     Files:
     - lib/std/http.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_http.ex

     Required change:
     - Generated record representation should be centralized.
     - Runtime helper should construct through the same representation API or generated constructor.
     - Add ABI tests for every runtime stdlib record bridge.

265. Runtime stdlib ADT tags are inconsistent across modules.
     Problem:
     - JSON uses tags like `{:ok, value}`, `{:error, reason}`, `:none`, `{:some, value}`.
     - Regex uses `{:ok, regex}`, `{:error, ...}`, `:none`, `{:some, match}`.
     - Core/dependent constructors use `{:ctor, name, args}` internally and emission may use atoms/tuples.
     - The runtime ABI for `Option`/`Result` is implicit and duplicated.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_regex.ex
     - lib/cure/elab/emit.ex
     - lib/std/option.cure
     - lib/std/result.cure

     Required change:
     - Define one canonical runtime ABI for ADTs.
     - Runtime stdlib helpers must construct via that ABI.
     - Add ABI conformance tests for Option, Result, records, and user ADTs.

266. MCP `compile_cure` compiles and loads arbitrary source into the MCP server VM.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.MCP.Server.call_tool("compile_cure", ...)` calls:
       `Cure.Compiler.compile_and_load(source, emit_events: false)`
     - This loads generated BEAM code into the long-running MCP process.
     - If the MCP server is exposed to an AI tool or editor integration, tool input can mutate the server VM by loading modules.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - MCP compile should default to parse/check only.
     - Loading/running code should require an explicit unsafe/dev capability.
     - Prefer compiling in an isolated short-lived process or sandbox.
     - Static/final MCP workflows should never call `compile_and_load`.

267. MCP `type_check_cure` uses only the legacy checker.
     Problem:
     - `type_check_cure` lexes/parses and calls:
       `Cure.Types.Checker.check_module/2`
     - It does not route dependent-looking programs through the Core elaborator/kernel path.
     - It can therefore report “Type check passed” for code that would fail dependent final-Core checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - MCP type checking should use the same mode-sensitive compiler/check pipeline as `cure check`.
     - For dependent modules, call the Core elaborator/kernel checker.
     - Report which checker/mode was used.

268. MCP `validate_syntax` can be mistaken for real validation.
     Problem:
     - `validate_syntax` performs lex+parse only and returns:
       `Syntax is valid`
     - It does not type-check, effect-check, verify holes, verify refinements, or check final-Core readiness.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Rename/tool-describe this as parse-only validation.
     - Provide separate tools:
       - `parse_cure`
       - `check_cure_static`
       - `check_cure_dependent`
       - `check_cure_release`
     - Avoid ambiguous “valid” wording.

269. MCP invalid JSON requests disappear without JSON-RPC error responses.
     Problem:
     - `safe_decode/1` rescues all JSON errors and returns `:error`.
     - The stdio loop drops invalid requests instead of returning JSON-RPC parse errors.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Return a JSON-RPC `Parse error` response where possible.
     - Log malformed request diagnostics in dev mode.
     - Do not silently ignore malformed tool requests.

270. MCP has no input size limits.
     Problem:
     - `compile_cure`, `parse_cure`, `type_check_cure`, and `analyze_fsm` accept arbitrary source strings.
     - A tool client can send huge source payloads and force lex/parse/typecheck work inside the MCP server.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Add maximum request size.
     - Add per-tool source-size limits.
     - Add timeouts for compile/check/analyze tools.
     - Return structured timeout/resource-limit errors.

271. LSP diagnostics are parse-only.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.LSP.Server.compute_diagnostics/2` lexes and parses, then returns `[]` on parse success.
     - It does not run type checking, hole goal discovery, effect checking, refinement checking, FSM verification, or dependent Core checking.
     - Users can see a clean LSP buffer while the compiler would reject the file.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - LSP diagnostics should have tiers:
       - syntax diagnostics,
       - legacy type diagnostics,
       - dependent Core diagnostics,
       - hole goals,
       - effect/refinement diagnostics.
     - The UI should clearly label which diagnostics are currently enabled.

272. LSP AST cache can skip diagnostics based only on client version.
     Problem:
     - `didChange` skips diagnostics when cached version equals the incoming version.
     - If a buggy client reuses a version with different text, diagnostics become stale.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Cache by `{uri, version, content_hash}`.
     - If text changes, recompute regardless of version.
     - Keep version as a fast path only when paired with content hash.

273. LSP rename is raw textual replacement.
     Problem:
     - `compute_rename/5` finds word occurrences with a regex and rewrites every match in the document.
     - It can rename:
       - comments,
       - strings,
       - unrelated scopes,
       - imported names,
       - field names,
       - constructor names,
       - protocol methods.
     - It does not use binding/resolution information.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Rename must use scoped name resolution.
     - If scope-aware rename is unavailable, disable rename or make it preview-only.
     - Never advertise a semantic rename provider backed by regex replacement.

274. LSP prepare-rename range calculation is wrong at cursor positions.
     Problem:
     - `prepare_rename/3` computes:
       `start = char - String.length(word)`
       `end = char + String.length(word)`
     - If the cursor is in the middle of the word, this range is too wide.
     - It can edit surrounding text.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Return the actual word occurrence span from `extract_word_at/2`.
     - Track start/end offsets during extraction.
     - Add tests for cursor at start/middle/end of identifier.

275. LSP quickfix for non-exhaustive match inserts a throwing wildcard.
     Problem:
     - Code action for “not exhaustive” inserts:
       `_ -> throw "unhandled case"`
     - This converts a totality/coverage problem into an exception path.
     - In static/dependent Cure, this is the wrong default.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - In static/dependent mode, suggest missing constructors/cases, not a throwing wildcard.
     - Offer throwing wildcard only in partial/runtime mode.
     - Mark it explicitly as partial/unsafe.

276. LSP hover/signature/completion use syntax symbol tables, not checked symbols.
     Problem:
     - `build_symbol_table/1` extracts function names and params from the AST.
     - It does not know resolved types, overloads, protocol impls, imports, effects, totality, or dependent elaboration results.
     - Hover/completion can present invalid or stale signatures.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Drive semantic LSP features from checked module metadata.
     - Fall back to syntax-only with explicit “untyped” detail.
     - Do not display syntax signatures as if they were checked types.

277. LSP type formatting falls back to `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `format_type/1` returns `"Any"` for unknown/unhandled type AST.
     - This mirrors the broader `Any` fallback problem in user-facing tooling.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Render unknown type as:
       - `?T`
       - `<unknown>`
       - `<unresolved>`
     - Reserve `Any` only for explicit dynamic type.

278. LSP reader can crash on malformed `Content-Length`.
     Problem:
     - `read_lsp_message/0` does:
       `String.to_integer()` on the `content-length` header.
     - Malformed headers can raise and terminate the server.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse content length safely.
     - Return protocol error for invalid headers.
     - Keep the server alive after malformed client input.

279. LSP reader has no message size limit.
     Problem:
     - `read_lsp_message/0` reads exactly `Content-Length` bytes with no cap.
     - A client can request very large reads and consume memory.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Add a maximum LSP message size.
     - Reject oversized payloads with a protocol error.
     - Add defensive limits for diagnostics/completion requests too.

280. LSP `exit` notification halts the entire VM unconditionally.
     Problem:
     - `do_handle("exit", ...)` calls:
       `System.halt(0)`
     - A client can terminate the hosting VM directly.
     - This is standard-ish for a standalone LSP process, but unsafe if embedded.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Only halt if running as a dedicated LSP process.
     - If embedded, stop the server process instead.
     - Require a prior `shutdown` request before accepting `exit`.

281. Temporal parser accepts arbitrary state atoms without validating against the model.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Temporal.Parser` parses any identifier into `Formula.atom(name)`.
     - Formula atoms are not checked against FSM states at parse time.
     - Later checker paths must validate them, but parser/tooling may present invalid formulae as parsed successfully.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/temporal/parser.ex
     - lib/cure/temporal/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Add a model-aware validation pass:
       - every atom must reference a declared state/proposition.
     - Parse success should not be confused with temporal-property validity.

282. Temporal formula smart constructors accept arbitrary child values.
     Problem:
     - `Formula.always/1`, `Formula.eventually/1`, `Formula.next_/1`, `Formula.until/2`, etc. accept any term.
     - Invalid formula trees can be constructed programmatically and later pretty-printed or checked.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/formula.ex

     Required change:
     - Add `Formula.valid?/1` / `validate/1`.
     - Temporal checker should validate formula shape before checking.
     - Constructors may remain permissive internally, but public checking must reject malformed formula trees.

283. Temporal formula type specs for binary connectives are wrong.
     Problem:
     - Type spec says:
       `{:and_, [t()]}`
       `{:or_, [t()]}`
       `{:implies, [t()]}`
       `{:until, [t()]}`
     - Runtime constructors store two children.
     - This weakens Dialyzer/spec-based checks.

     File:
     - lib/cure/temporal/formula.ex

     Required change:
     - Fix specs to:
       `{:and_, [t(), t()]}`
       `{:or_, [t(), t()]}`
       `{:implies, [t(), t()]}`
       `{:until, [t(), t()]}`
     - Add shape validation tests.

284. SMT S-expression parser ignores trailing tokens.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.SMT.Parser.parse_sexp/1` returns `{:ok, result}` even when `parse_tokens/1` leaves `_rest`.
     - Malformed output like two adjacent top-level forms can be accepted as the first form.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - If tokens remain after parsing one S-expression, return an error.
     - Do not accept partial parses in solver/model tooling.

285. SMT S-expression parser returns `nil` for empty input.
     Problem:
     - `parse_tokens([])` returns `{nil, []}`.
     - Therefore empty input can become `{:ok, nil}`.
     - This makes malformed solver output look like a parsed value.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Empty input should return `{:error, :empty}`.
     - Callers must handle missing solver/model output explicitly.

286. SMT model parser silently overwrites duplicate definitions.
     Problem:
     - `parse_model/1` uses `Map.new/1`.
     - Duplicate `define-fun` entries keep the last one and discard earlier conflicting values.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Detect duplicate model bindings.
     - Return a structured error on duplicate or conflicting entries.
     - Include the duplicated name in diagnostics.

287. SMT model parser ignores unsupported sorts.
     Problem:
     - Regex captures the sort with `\w+` but discards it.
     - `parse_value/1` parses ints/floats/bools/strings without checking the declared sort.
     - A malformed or unsupported model can be accepted with wrong value types.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse and validate sorts.
     - Reject unsupported sorts unless explicitly represented as uninterpreted values.
     - Preserve enough model metadata for diagnostics.

288. Legacy dependent compatibility treats missing constraints as compatible both ways.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Dependent.compatible?/2` has:
       `{[], _} -> true`
       `{_, []} -> true`
     - This means constrained and unconstrained dependent types can be compatible in both directions.
     - That is too broad for subtyping/compatibility.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

     Required change:
     - Compatibility/subtyping must distinguish:
       - exact constructor/indices,
       - constraint implication,
       - unconstrained supertype vs constrained subtype.
     - Do not treat both directions as true merely because one side has no constraints.

289. Legacy dependent compatibility does not actually prove implication.
     Problem:
     - Comments say SMT implication should be used, but implementation falls back to:
       `c1 == c2`
     - This rejects some valid cases and accepts some invalid cases due to the broad empty-constraint rules.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

     Required change:
     - Either remove this from trusted checking or implement actual obligation generation.
     - Constraint implication should become a Core/SMT obligation with explicit unknown/error handling.
     - Do not present structural equality as dependent compatibility.

290. Dependent VC generation truncates parameters and arguments.
     Problem:
     - `generate_vc/2` uses `Enum.zip(vps, args)`.
     - If there are too few or too many args, zip silently truncates.
     - Some value parameters may remain unsubstituted, and extra args are ignored.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

     Required change:
     - Check arity exactly before substitution.
     - Return `{:error, {:arity_mismatch, expected, got}}` on mismatch.
     - Do not generate partial VCs.

291. Dependent substitution does not substitute value parameters themselves.
     Problem:
     - `substitute/2` only substitutes inside constraints.
     - The dependent type’s value parameter list `vps` remains unchanged.
     - Return types such as `Vector(T, m + n)` need substitution in indices/value params too.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

     Required change:
     - Substitute throughout:
       - type params,
       - value params/index expressions,
       - constraints.
     - Or remove this legacy representation from trusted checking and lower to Core.

292. Hole recognizer treats `_` in value position as a hole.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Holes.recognise/1` maps:
       `{:variable, _, "_"}` to `{:hole, "_"}`
     - `_` is also conventionally a wildcard/ignored binding.
     - This can confuse value holes with pattern wildcards or ignored variables.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/holes.ex

     Required change:
     - Separate:
       - wildcard `_`,
       - type hole `_` in annotation position,
       - anonymous body/proof hole `??`,
       - named hole `?name`.
     - Do not infer hole kind from variable spelling alone without syntactic context.

293. Hole numbering only walks 3-tuple AST nodes.
     Problem:
     - `number_anonymous/1` only recursively walks `{tag, meta, children}` where `children` is a list.
     - Other AST forms containing child terms can be skipped.
     - Anonymous holes inside metadata, params, guards, clauses, or nonstandard tuples may not be numbered.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/holes.ex

     Required change:
     - Use the central AST traversal/walker.
     - Cover metadata fields that contain expressions/types.
     - Add tests for holes in:
       - guards,
       - lambdas,
       - match arms,
       - record fields,
       - type annotations,
       - proof positions.

294. Derive silently ignores unknown derive classes.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Derive.derive/3` returns `[]` for unknown typeclasses.
     - A misspelled `@derive` can silently generate nothing.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown derive class should be a compile error in static/release mode.
     - Dev mode may warn and continue only if explicitly requested.
     - Diagnostics should include valid derive names.

295. Derived `Show` calls unqualified `show`.
     Problem:
     - Generated `show` implementation calls `show(x.field)` without dictionary/protocol resolution.
     - It may recursively call itself, call the wrong visible function, or fail depending on scope/imports.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Derived Show must require checked `Show(field_type)` evidence.
     - Generate dictionary/protocol calls after resolution, not raw unqualified names.
     - Reject derive if any field lacks Show.

296. Derived `Functor` maps every field regardless of type parameter occurrence.
     Problem:
     - Docs admit generated `fmap` applies `f` to every field.
     - For records with fields not containing the functor parameter, this is not a lawful or well-typed functor.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Track type parameter occurrence per field.
     - Apply `f` only where the parameter occurs positively.
     - Reject negative/mixed/invariant occurrences unless explicitly supported.
     - Require full type annotations in generated code.

297. Derived `Monoid` uses `<>` for every field.
     Problem:
     - `derive_monoid/3` combines all fields with the string/list concat operator `<>`.
     - It does not require `Monoid(field_type)` evidence.
     - Primitive fields are documented as requiring override, but the generator still emits code.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Require checked Monoid evidence per field.
     - Generate calls to `combine` through the protocol/dictionary.
     - Reject fields without Monoid implementations.

298. Derived functions are not namespace-qualified.
     Problem:
     - Derive emits functions named `show`, `eq`, `compare`, `fmap`, `combine`, `to_json`.
     - Without an enclosing impl/dictionary representation, these can collide with user functions or other derived implementations.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/derive.ex

     Required change:
     - Derive should generate protocol impl containers/dictionaries, not bare top-level functions.
     - Names should be hygienic and module-qualified.
     - Collision detection should run before insertion.

299. Documentation extraction resolves types through the legacy fallback resolver.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Doc.Extractor.extract_function/1` uses:
       `Type.display(Type.resolve(type_ast))`
     - Unknown/malformed types can appear in docs as `Any`.
     - Docs can therefore misrepresent actual checked APIs.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/extractor.ex

     Required change:
     - Generate docs from checked metadata where available.
     - Render unresolved types as unresolved, not `Any`.
     - Document whether a signature is checked or syntax-derived.

300. Documentation extraction silently returns `Unknown` modules.
     Problem:
     - Non-module or malformed AST returns:
       `%{module: "Unknown", ...}`
     - CLI docs generation then filters out `Unknown` modules.
     - Parse/shape failures can disappear from generated docs.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/doc/extractor.ex
     - lib/cure/cli.ex

     Required change:
     - For documentation builds used in release/publish, malformed modules should be errors.
     - Best-effort docs mode may skip, but must report skipped files.

301. CLI doc generation skips parse failures.
     Problem:
     - `cmd_doc/2` reads each file, lexes/parses, and on any error returns `[]`.
     - Documentation can be generated successfully while some source files failed parsing.

     File:
     - lib/cure/cli.ex

     Required change:
     - Add strict docs mode for release/publish.
     - Report every skipped file with reason.
     - Do not claim complete docs when source files failed parse.

302. CLI test preloads project libs but does not fail immediately on lib compile errors.
     Problem:
     - `load_project_lib/0` compiles every `lib/**/*.cure`.
     - Compile failures are warnings rather than aborting test setup.
     - Tests may then fail later with less precise `:undef`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/cli.ex

     Required change:
     - `cure test` should fail fast when project lib compilation fails, at least by default.
     - Keep warning-only mode behind `--best-effort`.
     - Test output should distinguish setup failure from test assertion failure.

303. CLI doctests wrap examples in imprecise checking path.
     Problem:
     - CLI doctests call `Cure.Doc.Doctests.run_one/2`, and the doctest module previously wraps expressions as `Any`.
     - This makes docs examples too permissive for static/dependent examples.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/cli.ex
     - lib/cure/doc/doctests.ex

     Required change:
     - Add checked doctest mode with inferred/declared result types.
     - Static docs should reject examples that only pass via `Any`.

304. CLI `draw`/watch/template options intern arbitrary atoms.
     Problem:
     - CLI paths use `String.to_atom/1` for:
       - draw filters,
       - watch actions,
       - templates,
       - MFA function names.
     - User command-line input can create atoms.

     File:
     - lib/cure/cli.ex

     Required change:
     - Use closed enums for known CLI options.
     - Reject unknown action/template/filter names as strings.
     - Use `String.to_existing_atom/1` only for already-loaded MFA references, or avoid atoms entirely.

305. CLI signing command rescues broadly.
     Problem:
     - Around signing/key commands, CLI uses broad `rescue`.
     - This can collapse distinct security errors into generic user-facing failures.

     File:
     - lib/cure/cli.ex

     Required change:
     - Return structured signing errors from signing modules.
     - CLI should report exact failure class:
       - invalid handle,
       - unreadable key,
       - corrupt key,
       - permission error,
       - verification failure.

306. BeamWriter writes BEAM files non-atomically.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Compiler.BeamWriter.write_beam/4` writes directly to final path.
     - A crash or concurrent build can leave a truncated `.beam`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/beam_writer.ex

     Required change:
     - Write to temp file and atomic rename.
     - Optionally fsync in release mode.
     - Avoid partially-written artifacts in release/publish builds.

307. BeamWriter allows custom compile options from caller.
     Problem:
     - `compile_forms/2` appends:
       `Keyword.get(opts, :compile_opts, [])`
     - If external callers can pass options, they may change debug info, warnings, or compilation behavior unexpectedly.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/beam_writer.ex

     Required change:
     - Define allowed compile options per mode.
     - Release mode should use a fixed vetted option set.
     - Reject unsupported compile options in static/release/publish.

308. `compile_and_load` ignores BEAM warnings.
     Problem:
     - `BeamWriter.compile_and_load/1` discards warnings from `compile_forms/1`.
     - Warnings from generated Erlang forms can indicate unreachable clauses, bad guards, or dubious generated code.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/beam_writer.ex

     Required change:
     - Return warnings from `compile_and_load`.
     - Static/release mode should decide whether BEAM warnings are fatal.
     - Container compilers should surface these warnings too.

309. Antigen corpus fault decoder interns arbitrary atoms.
     Problem:
     - `Antigen.Corpus.decode_fault/1` uses:
       `String.to_atom(k)`
       `String.to_atom(tok)`
       `Enum.map(elems, &String.to_atom/1)`
     - Corpus files can create atoms on replay.

     File:
     - lib/antigen/corpus.ex

     Required change:
     - Use `String.to_existing_atom/1` against `Challenge.__known_atoms__/0`, or a local symbol table.
     - Reject unknown fault keys/atoms.
     - Keep corpus replay safe against arbitrary files.

310. Antigen corpus record decoding rescues all exceptions into `{:error, e}`.
     Problem:
     - `decode_record/1` catches all exceptions.
     - That is convenient, but it can hide bugs in decoders/generators as ordinary bad corpus records.

     File:
     - lib/antigen/corpus.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - malformed external corpus record,
       - unsupported old corpus version,
       - internal decoder crash.
     - Internal decoder crashes should fail Antigen infrastructure tests.

311. Antigen corpus append is not concurrency-safe.
     Problem:
     - `append/3` checks `seen?/2` by scanning the file, then appends.
     - Concurrent writers can both miss the key and append duplicates.
     - File writes are single append calls, but dedup is not atomic.

     File:
     - lib/antigen/corpus.ex

     Required change:
     - Use file locking or a single corpus writer process.
     - Or accept duplicates and dedup at read time explicitly.
     - Do not claim append-time dedup under concurrency.

312. Antigen failure report filenames race.
     Problem:
     - `Antigen.Report.next_index/3` counts existing files and chooses `length + 1`.
     - Concurrent failures with same seed/assay can pick the same index.

     File:
     - lib/antigen/report.ex

     Required change:
     - Use unique monotonic IDs or atomic exclusive file creation.
     - Include timestamp/process unique integer if needed.
     - Avoid overwriting failure reports.

313. Protocol registration silently overwrites protocol definitions.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Types.Protocol.register_protocol/3` stores protocols with:
       `Map.put(ctx.protocols, name, proto_def)`
     - Duplicate protocol declarations replace earlier ones.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject duplicate protocol definitions in the same scope/module.
     - Include original and duplicate source spans.

314. Protocol implementation registration does not reject duplicate impls.
     Problem:
     - `register_impl/3` prepends every impl to the implementation list.
     - Duplicate implementations for the same protocol/type can coexist.
     - Dispatch ordering then determines behavior.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject duplicate `impl Protocol for Type`.
     - Or require explicit overlapping-instance policy, with deterministic priority.
     - Static mode should not allow ambiguous impls.

315. Protocol method signatures are collected but not validated in registration.
     Problem:
     - `register_impl/3` stores body directly.
     - This module does not verify that the impl provides exactly the protocol methods with matching signatures.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

     Required change:
     - Check:
       - all required methods present,
       - no unknown methods unless extension is allowed,
       - parameter and return types match protocol declaration after substitution,
       - effects/totality constraints are respected.
     - Store checked impl metadata, not raw bodies.

316. Protocol implementation name mangling can collide.
     Problem:
     - `mangle_impl_name/2` uses lowercase method/type strings:
       `"#{String.downcase(method_name)}__for__#{String.downcase(for_type)}"`
     - Names differing only by case or module qualification can collide.
     - Module path is not included.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

     Required change:
     - Use qualified stable symbols for protocol impl methods.
     - Include module/protocol/type identity in the generated symbol.
     - Detect collisions before codegen.

317. Protocol user-defined record guard is unqualified.
     Problem:
     - `type_guard/3` maps user type name to:
       `Macro.underscore(type_name) |> String.to_atom()`
     - This loses module namespace and can collide across records.
     - It also assumes record runtime `__struct__` atom layout.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/protocol.ex

     Required change:
     - Runtime record tags must be qualified and centrally defined.
     - Protocol guards should use checked type metadata, not string-derived atoms.
     - Static protocol dispatch should not rely on runtime map shape alone.

318. CoreBridge/source tool paths still create globals for unresolved names.
     Problem:
     - Legacy tooling that translates expressions into Core often treats names as globals too early.
     - This interacts badly with typed primitive globals, universe-polymorphic globals, and local binders.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex
     - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex

     Required change:
     - Any surface-to-Core bridge must carry a checked context.
     - Free local variables, module globals, primitive globals, and constructors must be distinguished before translation.

319. `Cure.Compiler.compile_string/2` optimizes after checking and pattern-matches optimizer success.
     Problem:
     - `maybe_optimize/3` does:
       `{:ok, optimized, _stats} = Optimizer.optimize(ast, opts)`
     - An optimizer error becomes a `MatchError`, not a structured compiler error.
     - This is separate from the previously noted “post-optimization not rechecked” issue.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Handle optimizer errors explicitly.
     - Return `{:error, {:optimizer_error, reason}}`.
     - Recheck optimized AST/Core in static/release mode.

320. Cure compiler dependent path is selected by AST heuristic at codegen time.
     Problem:
     - `codegen/5` checks:
       `if Cure.Elab.Program.dependent?(ast) do ... else legacy codegen ... end`
     - This routes a whole module late in the pipeline.
     - Any dependent feature missed by the heuristic can fall into legacy codegen/checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler.ex
     - lib/cure/elab/program.ex

     Required change:
     - Make compile mode explicit:
       - legacy/dynamic,
       - static,
       - dependent.
     - Do not rely on AST feature sniffing for trust-boundary selection.
     - In static/dependent mode, route all modules through the same final-Core readiness gate.

321. Documentation and LSP “Unknown” sentinels can hide malformed AST shape.
     Problem:
     - Several tools use sentinel names like `"Unknown"` / `"unknown"` / `"unnamed"`.
     - These are useful for display, but dangerous if later used as real identifiers or silently filtered.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/doc/extractor.ex
     - lib/cure/lsp/symbols.ex
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex
     - lib/cure/types/stdlib.ex

     Required change:
     - Use structured missing-name diagnostics.
     - Do not feed `"Unknown"` into checked metadata, symbol extraction, or generated code.
     - Display sentinels should remain UI-only.

322. `Cure.SMT.Parser.parse_model/1` is regex-based and not a real S-expression model parser.
     Problem:
     - Model parsing uses one regex over `(define-fun ...)`.
     - Nested values, arrays, datatypes, uninterpreted values, functions, and complex negative/decimal forms can be misparsed.
     - Counterexamples can therefore be wrong or incomplete.

     File:
     - lib/cure/smt/parser.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse the model as S-expressions first.
     - Then validate each `define-fun` form structurally.
     - Preserve unsupported model values as structured terms or reject with clear diagnostics.

323. `Cure.Types.Holes.render/3` uses legacy `Type.display/1`.
     Problem:
     - Hole goal rendering displays goals through the legacy type display function.
     - For Core/dependent holes, this may misrender Pi/Sigma/indexed/Eq/universe-polymorphic goals.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/holes.ex

     Required change:
     - Use a Core-aware pretty-printer for dependent hole goals.
     - Keep legacy rendering only for legacy checker holes.
     - Include context quantities/effects/proof erasure status in dependent hole displays.

324. `Cure.Doc.Extractor` omits effects when no explicit effect annotation exists.
     Problem:
     - `effect_list` is `[]` if metadata lacks `:effects`.
     - This can mean either “pure” or “unknown/not checked”.
     - Docs currently cannot distinguish those cases.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/extractor.ex

     Required change:
     - Docs should distinguish:
       - checked pure,
       - checked effectful,
       - unchecked/unknown effects,
       - extern/unsafe effects.
     - Generate docs from checked effect metadata rather than syntax metadata.

325. `Cure.MCP.Server` exposes examples and stdlib docs by raw requested names.
     Problem:
     - `get_examples` / `get_stdlib_docs` are driven by user-provided strings.
     - The implementation should be checked for path traversal or uncontrolled file lookup.

     File:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex

     Required change:
     - Whitelist example names and stdlib module names.
     - Never convert arbitrary tool arguments into file paths without normalization and root checks.
     - Return structured “not found” errors.

326. REPL/editor/tooling paths should be explicitly outside the trusted build story.
     Problem:
     - MCP, LSP, REPL, docs, synth, bless, fix, and formatter all contain best-effort fallbacks.
     - Individually that is fine, but the repository does not enforce a hard distinction between:
       - advisory/editor tooling,
       - checked compiler pipeline,
       - release/publish pipeline.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/mcp/server.ex
     - lib/cure/lsp/server.ex
     - lib/cure/repl/*.ex
     - lib/cure/doc/*.ex
     - lib/cure/types/synth.ex
     - lib/cure/bless/advisor.ex
     - lib/cure/fix.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/formatter.ex

     Required change:
     - Introduce a formal trust-tier policy:
       - editor/advisory tools may recover and approximate,
       - compiler static mode must reject approximation,
       - release/publish mode must require checked artifacts.
     - Add tests that advisory fallbacks cannot enter final artifacts.

327. Registry dependency version constraints appear to be ignored.
     Problem:
     - `pick_version(name, _constraint, _root, false)` receives a constraint but ignores it.
     - It calls `Registry.list_versions(name)` and chooses `[top | _]`.
     - This can install the newest version even if it violates the declared constraint.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `pick_version/4`

     Required change:
     - Parse and enforce the declared constraint before selecting a version.
     - Reject if no version satisfies the constraint.
     - Record the selected version and constraint in the lockfile.
     - Add tests:
       - dependency requires `< 2.0.0`, registry latest is `2.1.0`
       - dependency requires exact `1.2.3`
       - transitive dependency has incompatible constraint.

328. Unknown dependency map shapes resolve successfully.
     Problem:
     - `resolve_one(_, _root, _reuse_lock?), do: :ok`
     - A malformed dependency entry silently succeeds instead of rejecting.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `resolve_one/3`

     Required change:
     - Unknown dependency shape should be:
       `{:error, {:invalid_dependency, dep}}`
     - Reject missing `name`, empty source, or unsupported dependency kind.
     - Do not silently skip malformed dependencies.

329. Path dependencies can escape the project root.
     Problem:
     - Path dependencies use:
       `Path.expand(rel_path, root)`
     - There is no policy preventing `../../somewhere` or absolute paths.
     - This may be desirable for dev, but not for release/publish.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - path dependency branch in `resolve_one/3`

     Required change:
     - Dev mode may allow arbitrary path deps.
     - Release/publish mode should require:
       - path under project root or workspace root,
       - explicit allowlist,
       - or vendored/locked dependency metadata.
     - Lockfile should record canonical path/hash for path deps.

330. Installed tarballs are extracted without path validation.
     Problem:
     - `install_tarball/4` calls:
       `:erl_tar.extract(..., cwd: target)`
     - The extraction result is ignored.
     - There is no explicit validation that tar entries stay inside `target`.
     - Tar entries containing `../` or absolute paths should never be trusted.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `install_tarball/4`

     Required change:
     - Inspect tar entries before extraction.
     - Reject absolute paths, `..`, drive prefixes, symlinks escaping target, and duplicate normalized paths.
     - Check and propagate `:erl_tar.extract/2` errors.
     - Extract into a temp directory and atomically move into place.

331. Dependency install ignores dependency compile failures.
     Problem:
     - After extraction, dependency `.cure` files are compiled with:
       `_ = Cure.Compiler.compile_file(...)`
     - Failures are ignored.
     - The dependency ebin path is still added to the code path.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `install_tarball/4`
       - path dependency branch in `resolve_one/3`

     Required change:
     - Dependency compile failures must abort resolution.
     - Do not add dependency ebin path unless every required source file compiled successfully.
     - Use checked/static mode for release/publish dependency compilation.

332. Dependency ebin paths are added globally.
     Problem:
     - Dependency resolution calls:
       `:code.add_patha(...)`
     - This mutates the VM global code path.
     - Stale or malicious modules in dependency ebin directories can affect later compilation/loading.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex

     Required change:
     - Static builds should resolve dependencies through checked artifact metadata, not global code-path mutation.
     - If BEAM loading is required, load exact modules by expected name/hash.
     - Avoid global `:code.add_patha` in release/publish verification.

333. Project compiler config interns arbitrary option names as atoms.
     Problem:
     - `[compiler]` TOML keys are converted with:
       `String.to_atom(key)`
     - Unknown compiler options create atoms and are accepted as booleans.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `apply_kv({:table, "compiler"}, ...)`

     Required change:
     - Use a closed set of known compiler options.
     - Unknown compiler option = structured config error.
     - Do not intern arbitrary TOML keys.

334. Compiler config coerces all unknown values to booleans.
     Problem:
     - Non-`stdlib_path` compiler config values are interpreted as:
       `strip_quotes(val) == "true"`
     - Invalid values like `"maybe"` silently become `false`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse each compiler option by declared type.
     - Reject invalid booleans instead of coercing to false.
     - Preserve exact line/key in diagnostics.

335. Stdlib path overrides can load arbitrary sources/beams.
     Problem:
     - `CURE_LIB`, `CURE_HOME`, `:stdlib_source_dir`, and `:stdlib_beam_dir` influence stdlib source/BEAM lookup.
     - That is convenient for development, but in release/static mode it can replace the trusted stdlib.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/paths.ex
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Dev/REPL may allow stdlib path overrides.
     - Static/release/publish mode should use bundled checked stdlib only, or require signed/hash-pinned stdlib override.
     - Record stdlib source/BEAM hash in build metadata.

336. Stdlib BEAM discovery loads modules before trusting them.
     Problem:
     - `discover_from_beams/1` scans `Cure.Std.*.beam`, converts filename to module atom, loads the BEAM, then calls `module.__group__/0`.
     - A malicious BEAM in a candidate stdlib directory can execute code at load time or inside `__group__/0`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex
       - `discover_from_beams/1`

     Required change:
     - Do not execute BEAM code to discover stdlib metadata in trusted builds.
     - Use signed metadata generated at stdlib build time.
     - Verify module hash before loading.
     - Treat BEAM introspection as dev/REPL fallback only.

337. Stdlib source-JIT compiles unchecked.
     Problem:
     - `jit_compile_module/2` calls:
       `Cure.Compiler.compile_and_load(..., check_types: false)`
     - Missing stdlib BEAMs can be recovered by compiling unchecked stdlib source into the VM.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Trusted prelude JIT must type-check.
     - Better: remove source-JIT from static/release mode entirely.
     - If source-JIT remains in REPL, mark it as dev-only.

338. Stdlib preload swallows BEAM load failures.
     Problem:
     - `load_if_present/2` returns `:ok` even when:
       - file read fails,
       - BEAM load fails,
       - module name mismatch happens,
       - BEAM is corrupt.
     - Callers cannot distinguish “loaded” from “failed”.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex
       - `load_if_present/2`

     Required change:
     - Return structured errors.
     - Static/release mode should fail when a required stdlib module cannot be loaded.
     - Dev mode may degrade, but should report exact failures.

339. Stdlib module discovery interns module names from BEAM filenames.
     Problem:
     - `Path.basename(".beam") |> String.to_atom()`
     - Candidate stdlib directories can create arbitrary atoms through filenames.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Use expected stdlib module table.
     - Use `String.to_existing_atom/1` only after verifying against known module names.
     - Do not intern arbitrary discovered filenames.

340. Project publisher packages Elixir source files.
     Problem:
     - `collect_files/1` includes:
       `lib/**/*.ex`
     - Cure packages can ship arbitrary Elixir source.
     - If later tooling compiles/loads those files, this becomes an untyped code path.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex
       - `collect_files/1`

     Required change:
     - Decide whether Cure packages may contain Elixir source.
     - If yes, require explicit native/unsafe package declaration.
     - If no, remove `lib/**/*.ex` from package contents.
     - Publish should report native files distinctly.

341. Publisher tarball construction uses bang I/O and non-atomic temp files.
     Problem:
     - `build_tarball/2` and `build_hex_tarball/1` use `File.read!`, `File.write!`-style assumptions, and temp filenames based on `unique_integer`.
     - Failures raise rather than returning structured publish errors.
     - Temp files may remain on failures.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex

     Required change:
     - Use structured I/O errors.
     - Use `try/after` cleanup for temp files.
     - Use exclusive temp file creation.
     - Release/publish tooling should not crash on ordinary filesystem errors.

342. Hex metadata interns package name as an atom.
     Problem:
     - `hex_metadata/2` does:
       `{:app, String.to_atom(project.name)}`
     - Package names are user-controlled.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/publisher.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate package/app names against a strict grammar before interning.
     - Or avoid atom conversion until a trusted final packaging step.
     - Reject names that cannot safely become OTP app atoms.

343. Proof certificate collection uses public ETS.
     Problem:
     - `.cureproof` collection uses:
       `:ets.new(@ets_table, [:named_table, :public, :bag])`
     - Any process can insert forged certificates during proof collection.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof.ex

     Required change:
     - Use protected/private ETS owned by the compiler/proof collector.
     - Or pass certificates explicitly through compiler results.
     - Never accept public ETS contents as proof artifacts.

344. Proof collection skips compile/type errors.
     Problem:
     - `collect/1` compiles every file with:
       `_ = Cure.Compiler.compile_file(...)`
     - Errors are ignored.
     - It then serializes whatever certificates were deposited.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof.ex

     Required change:
     - Proof collection must fail if any source file fails to compile/check.
     - Certificates should be tied to the checked build graph.
     - Do not collect proofs from a partially failed build.

345. Proof artifact deserialization validates no schema.
     Problem:
     - `deserialize/1` uses `binary_to_term([:safe])`, then returns `{:ok, certs}`.
     - It does not validate list shape, certificate keys, certificate kinds, module names, statement shape, or witness shape.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof.ex

     Required change:
     - Add strict schema validation after decoding.
     - Reject unknown keys/kinds.
     - Include artifact version and checked Core hash.

346. Proof verifier accepts malformed refinement certificates.
     Problem:
     - `verify_refinement(_statement, _witness), do: :ok`
     - Any refinement certificate that does not match the expected shape is accepted.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Malformed refinement certificate = error.
     - Refinement verification must replay the obligation or validate checked evidence.
     - Bounds-only consistency is not enough.

347. Proof verifier accepts malformed SMT certificates.
     Problem:
     - `verify_smt(_statement, _witness), do: :ok`
     - Even the main SMT path accepts any well-formed model and also accepts no solver.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof/verifier.ex
