     Required change:
     - Malformed SMT certificate = error.
     - Solver unavailable = error in verified mode.
     - SMT certificate should be independently replayed or checked through SMTCoq-style certificates.

348. Proof verifier accepts malformed totality certificates.
     Problem:
     - `verify_totality(_statement, _witness), do: :ok`
     - `scc_total?(_, _), do: true`
     - Unknown SCC shapes are accepted.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/proof/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown totality certificate shape = error.
     - Totality certificate must reference checked Core definitions.
     - Malformed SCC data should never prove totality.

349. `exception_handling` infers as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - Type checker has:
       `defp do_infer(env, {:exception_handling, _meta, _}), do: {:ok, :any, env}`
     - Try/catch/finally expressions are not actually typed.
     - Exception effects are not tracked.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex

     Required change:
     - Type try/catch result by joining branch result types.
     - Type `finally` as effect-only or `Unit`.
     - Add exception effect tracking.
     - Static/total mode should reject unhandled exceptions unless explicitly effectful/partial.

350. Codegen `try` with malformed children returns `:ok`.
     Problem:
     - `compile_try/3` returns atom `:ok` when children do not match `[try_body | rest]`.
     - Malformed exception AST becomes a successful value.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_try/3`

     Required change:
     - Malformed try/catch/finally AST should be a codegen error.
     - Backend should assert AST invariants produced by parser/checker.

351. Catch clauses ignore exception class.
     Problem:
     - Generated Erlang catch clause pattern is:
       `{Class, Pattern, Stacktrace}` but class and stacktrace are `_`.
     - Cure catch patterns match only the reason.
     - Throw/error/exit classes are collapsed.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_catch_and_finally/3`

     Required change:
     - Cure exception model should distinguish throw/error/exit or intentionally erase them.
     - If erased, document it and type it.
     - Pattern matching on exceptions should not pretend to be more precise than runtime behavior.

352. Send expression accepts `Atom` / `Any` as pid-like.
     Problem:
     - Type checker comments say `:atom`, `:any`, and type variables preserve old semantics.
     - Any non-pid can pass through send typing.
     - Return type is the message type, not a send-result/effect type.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `do_infer({:send, ...})`

     Required change:
     - Static mode: send target must be `Pid(MessageType)` or a checked actor/FSM endpoint.
     - Sending should carry an effect.
     - Return type should be delivery result or sent message only if explicitly specified.

353. Comprehension generators fall back to `Any`.
     Problem:
     - For `x <- collection`, if collection does not infer as `List(t)`, element type becomes `:any`.
     - Bad generators can typecheck as dynamic comprehensions.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `bind_qualifier_vars/2`

     Required change:
     - Static mode: generator source must be a known iterable type.
     - Unknown/non-list generator source = type error.
     - Dynamic comprehensions require explicit dynamic boundary.

354. Comprehension filters are not type-checked as Bool.
     Problem:
     - `bind_qualifier_vars/2` ignores unknown qualifiers, including filters.
     - The comprehension body is typed, but filters are not required to be Bool at this point.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Each filter qualifier must infer/check as Bool.
     - Effects inside filters must be tracked.
     - Unsupported qualifier forms should error in static mode.

355. Expression-position declarations infer as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - These infer as `Any`:
       - container
       - function_def
       - import
       - property
     - Misplaced declarations can survive expression typing.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Declarations in expression position should be parse/check errors in static mode.
     - Editor recovery may keep placeholders but final compilation must reject.

356. Std.Io `print_int` and `print_float` drop the `Io` effect.
     Problem:
     - `println` is typed `! Io`.
     - `print_int` and `print_float` call `println(...)` but are declared:
       `fn print_int(n: Int) -> Atom`
       `fn print_float(f: Float) -> Atom`
     - Their effects are missing.

     File:
     - lib/std/io.cure

     Required change:
     - Change to:
       `fn print_int(n: Int) -> Atom ! Io`
       `fn print_float(f: Float) -> Atom ! Io`
     - Add stdlib effect propagation tests.

357. Std.Functor signature is not a lawful higher-kinded functor.
     Problem:
     - Protocol says:
       `proto Functor(F)`
       `fn fmap(container: F, f: A -> B) -> F`
     - This cannot express `F A -> (A -> B) -> F B`.
     - `List` impl returns raw `List`, losing element type.

     File:
     - lib/std/functor.cure

     Required change:
     - Defer Functor until Cure has a clear higher-kinded/type-constructor story.
     - Or model specific functors directly:
       `List.map : List(A) -> (A -> B) -> List(B)`
     - Do not keep current `Functor(F)` in checked/static stdlib as if it were principled.

358. Std.Show for String does not escape.
     Problem:
     - Docs say String `show` wraps the value in quotes and “does not escape.”
     - That output is not valid/readable source for strings containing quotes, backslashes, or control characters.

     File:
     - lib/std/show.cure

     Required change:
     - Either rename to `display`/`debug_string`, or implement escaping.
     - If `show` is meant to be source-like, it must be round-trippable or documented as not source syntax.

359. Std.Show `show_line` is unconstrained.
     Problem:
     - `fn show_line(x: T) -> String = show(x) <> "\n"`
     - It does not explicitly require `Show(T)` evidence in the surface type.
     - Depending on protocol resolution, this can become another unconstrained generic call.

     File:
     - lib/std/show.cure

     Required change:
     - Require explicit protocol/dictionary evidence:
       `show_line : Show(T) => T -> String`
       or equivalent.
     - Static protocol calls must be type-directed.

360. Std.Match tuple accessors return arbitrary `T`.
     Problem:
     - `fst(t: Tuple) -> T`
     - `snd(t: Tuple) -> T`
     - These recover caller-chosen arbitrary types from an unparameterized runtime tuple.
     - They are also non-exhaustive for some tuple shapes.

     File:
     - lib/std/match.cure

     Required change:
     - Replace with typed tuple projections where tuple arity/type is known.
     - Dynamic tuple access should return `Option(Dynamic)` / `Result`.
     - Do not expose arbitrary `T` from `Tuple`.

361. REPL snapshot decode has no size/depth limits.
     Problem:
     - `.cure-snap` uses gzip + `binary_to_term([:safe])`.
     - `[:safe]` helps, but a crafted compressed file can still be very large after gunzip.
     - No shape/size/depth validation is applied after decoding.

     File:
     - lib/cure/repl/snap.ex

     Required change:
     - Limit compressed and decompressed size.
     - Validate snapshot schema strictly.
     - Reject unknown keys and oversized lists.
     - Treat shared snapshots as untrusted.

362. REPL snapshots can merge arbitrary existing loaded paths.
     Problem:
     - `apply_snap/2` keeps `loaded_paths` that exist on the current machine.
     - A shared snapshot can cause the REPL session to remember paths outside the project.

     File:
     - lib/cure/repl/snap.ex

     Required change:
     - On load, ask before importing external loaded paths.
     - Restrict loaded paths to project root unless explicitly allowed.
     - Store path hashes if snapshots are meant to resume trusted sessions.

363. LSP transport treats invalid JSON as incomplete.
     Problem:
     - `safe_json_decode/1` returns `:error`.
     - `parse_one/1` maps that to `:incomplete`.
     - A complete frame containing invalid JSON can wedge the buffer instead of producing a parse error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/transport.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - incomplete frame,
       - invalid JSON frame.
     - Invalid JSON should consume the frame and return a JSON-RPC parse error.

364. LSP transport can crash on malformed Content-Length.
     Problem:
     - `parse_content_length/1` calls:
       `String.to_integer(...)`
     - Malformed length raises.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/transport.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse content length safely.
     - Reject negative or too-large lengths.
     - Return structured protocol error, not an exception.

365. LSP transport has no maximum frame size.
     Problem:
     - `parse_one/1` accepts whatever Content-Length says.
     - Large frames can accumulate in memory.

     File:
     - lib/cure/lsp/transport.ex

     Required change:
     - Add max frame size.
     - Reject oversized frames before buffering the full payload.

366. Documentation extras can read files outside the project root.
     Problem:
     - `collect_extras/2` does:
       `Path.expand(extra, project_root)`
       then reads the file if regular.
     - It does not require the expanded path to stay under `project_root`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/html_generator.ex

     Required change:
     - For release/publish docs, require extras to stay inside project root.
     - Or require explicit allowlist for external docs.
     - Report skipped/forbidden extras as diagnostics.

367. Documentation slugs can collide and overwrite pages.
     Problem:
     - Extra and module page filenames are derived by slugifying names.
     - Two extras/modules can map to the same slug.
     - Later writes overwrite earlier pages.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/html_generator.ex

     Required change:
     - Detect duplicate slugs before writing.
     - Include source path/module name in the collision error.
     - Use deterministic disambiguation only if explicitly requested.

368. Printer fallback emits `inspect/1` output as Cure source.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Compiler.Printer.to_string/3` falls back to `inspect(other)`.
     - Unknown AST nodes can become Elixir tuple syntax or other non-Cure source.
     - Formatter/fix tools relying on the printer can produce invalid or misleading code.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/printer.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown AST in printer should return a structured error in strict mode.
     - Formatter recovery may keep original source, but should report the unprintable node.
     - Do not treat `inspect/1` output as valid Cure.

369. Printer unknown operators become atom names.
     Problem:
     - `operator_to_string(other), do: Atom.to_string(other)`
     - Unknown operator atoms can print as arbitrary text.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/printer.ex

     Required change:
     - Only known operators should print.
     - Unknown operator = printer/formatter error in strict mode.

370. Coverage instrumentation ignores compile/analyse failures.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Cover.start/1` ignores `:cover.compile_beam` failures.
     - `collect/0` reports zero coverage for failed analysis.
     - CI coverage can silently degrade.

     File:
     - lib/cure/cover.ex

     Required change:
     - Report failed instrumentation separately.
     - In CI/coverage-enforced mode, fail on instrumentation/analysis errors.
     - Do not conflate “0% covered” with “coverage failed.”

371. Watch `check` uses only the legacy checker.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Watch.run_action(:check, ...)` lexes/parses and calls:
       `Cure.Types.Checker.check_module`
     - It does not use dependent Core elaboration/final-Core checks.
     - Watch mode can say “OK” for code the real dependent pipeline rejects.

     File:
     - lib/cure/watch.ex

     Required change:
     - Watch check should call the same mode-sensitive check pipeline as the CLI.
     - Display checker mode in output.
     - Dependent/static watch mode should run final-Core readiness checks.

372. Watch compile/check actions do not return aggregate failure.
     Problem:
     - Watch prints errors per file but the action itself does not return a failure status.
     - This is fine for an interactive loop, but not for scripting.

     File:
     - lib/cure/watch.ex

     Required change:
     - Return structured summary:
       `{:ok, count}` or `{:error, failures}`
     - Keep printing as UI behavior, but expose machine-readable result.

373. Profiler defaults to unchecked compilation.
     Problem:
     - `profile_string/2` passes:
       `check_types: Keyword.get(opts, :check_types, false)`
     - Profiling a file by default measures unchecked compilation.

     File:
     - lib/cure/profiler.ex

     Required change:
     - Default profiling should match normal checked build mode.
     - Add explicit `check_types: false` only for dev benchmarking.
     - Report compile mode in the profile output.

374. Profiler returns `{:ok, report}` even when compilation fails.
     Problem:
     - `profile_string/2` always returns `{:ok, report}`.
     - Compile failure is embedded as a string inside the report.

     File:
     - lib/cure/profiler.ex

     Required change:
     - For tool/CI use, return `{:error, report}` when compilation fails.
     - Or expose both:
       - `profile_string/2` always reports,
       - `profile_checked/2` fails on compiler failure.

375. OTel context extraction accepts arbitrary binary sizes.
     Problem:
     - `extract_ctx({:cure_otel, trace_id, span_id})` accepts any binaries.
     - It does not enforce trace/span ID length or format.
     - Bad context can propagate through process dictionaries.

     File:
     - lib/cure/otel.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate trace/span ID lengths.
     - Reject malformed context tokens.
     - Keep invalid context as no-op only for display/runtime, not trace continuity.

376. OTel sample ratio is not validated at startup.
     Problem:
     - `sample_ratio` is taken from opts.
     - Non-number values can cause function-clause crashes in `sample?/1`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/otel.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate `sample_ratio` is a number in `[0.0, 1.0]`.
     - Return `{:error, {:invalid_sample_ratio, value}}` on bad config.

377. OTel MemoryExporter uses public ETS.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.OTel.MemoryExporter` stores spans in:
       `:ets.new(..., [:ordered_set, :public, :named_table, ...])`
     - Any process can forge or delete spans.

     File:
     - lib/cure/otel/memory_exporter.ex

     Required change:
     - Use protected/private ETS or a GenServer.
     - Treat memory exporter as test/dev only.
     - Never use memory exporter data as release/build evidence.

378. Pipeline events can leak full compiler payloads to arbitrary subscribers.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.Pipeline.Events.subscribe/1` lets any process subscribe to compiler events.
     - Events can include tokens, ASTs, types, errors, source paths, and possibly user code fragments.
     - This is fine inside one trusted VM, but should not be assumed private.

     File:
     - lib/cure/pipeline/events.ex

     Required change:
     - Treat pipeline events as diagnostic/public within the VM.
     - Do not emit secrets into event payloads.
     - For language-server/MCP/server modes, consider subscriber authorization or event redaction.

379. Oracle fixture reader uses raising JSON decode.
     Problem:
     - `read_fixture/1` calls:
       `JSON.decode!(body)`
     - Corrupt fixture JSON crashes the oracle instead of returning structured failure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/oracle.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `{:error, {:bad_fixture, path, reason}}`.
     - Make replay tests fail with a clear fixture diagnostic.

380. Oracle environment timeout parsing can crash.
     Problem:
     - `env_int/2` calls:
       `String.to_integer(s)`
     - Invalid `ORACLE_CURE_TIMEOUT_MS` or `ORACLE_IDRIS_TIMEOUT_MS` crashes.

     File:
     - lib/cure/oracle.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse env ints safely.
     - Reject negative/zero timeouts.
     - Fall back with a warning only in dev/test mode.

381. Project scaffold templates default to unchecked compilation.
     Problem:
     - New projects are generated with:
       `[compiler] type_check = false`
     - This means `cure new` bakes the unsafe default into every new project.
     - Earlier we noted the compiler/project default; this is the scaffold/template source of that policy.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `default_toml/1`
       - `default_toml(:app)`

     Required change:
     - Generated projects should default to:
       `type_check = true`
     - Fast unchecked compilation should require explicit dev-mode config.
     - App/release templates should be strict by default.

382. Project metadata silently fabricates missing identity.
     Problem:
     - Missing project name becomes `"unnamed"`.
     - Missing version becomes `"0.1.0"`.
     - Release/publish can proceed with fabricated package identity.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `parse_toml/1`

     Required change:
     - Missing/empty `project.name` = config error.
     - Missing/invalid `project.version` = config error.
     - Validate version as SemVer or the project’s declared version grammar.
     - Dev-only scaffolding may supply defaults, but parsed package config should not.

383. App detection swallows file read/lex/parse errors.
     Problem:
     - `detect_app/2` returns `[]` when source read/lex/parse fails.
     - A malformed file can make app detection conclude “no app” rather than “project is invalid.”

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `detect_app/2`

     Required change:
     - In release/static mode, read/lex/parse failure during app detection must be an error.
     - Editor/dev discovery may be best-effort, but must report skipped files.

384. Missing app name is accepted too broadly.
     Problem:
     - `verify_app_name(nil, _project), do: :ok`
     - This is fine for library projects, but not for application/release projects.
     - A release-oriented project can pass name verification with no app name.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `verify_app_name/2`

     Required change:
     - Mode-gate this behavior:
       - library project: app may be absent
       - app/release project: app and app name required
     - Missing app should fail before release assembly.

385. Malformed or missing release phases disable app phase verification.
     Problem:
     - `declared_phases(_)` returns `nil`.
     - Passing `nil` to app verification can skip bidirectional phase checks.
     - A malformed `[application.start_phases]` table can therefore weaken verification.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
     - lib/cure/app/verifier.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - no phases declared intentionally
       - malformed phase config
       - phase config unavailable due parse issue
     - If app code has `on_phase`, missing/malformed phase declarations should be an error.

386. Cure.toml parser silently ignores unknown sections, unknown keys, and malformed lines.
     Problem:
     - Unknown sections/keys fall through to returning the accumulator unchanged.
     - Malformed key/value lines can become empty entries or be ignored.
     - Malformed dependency lines can return `nil` and disappear.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex
       - `parse_toml/1`
       - `apply_kv/3`
       - `parse_kv/1`
       - `parse_dep_line/1`

     Required change:
     - Add strict config mode for static/release/publish.
     - Unknown section/key = structured config error.
     - Malformed line = structured config error with line number.
     - Best-effort config parsing should be editor/dev-only.

387. Cure.toml scalar parser accepts unsupported values as raw strings.
     Problem:
     - `parse_scalar/1` falls back to returning the trimmed string.
     - Arrays parse only quoted string items and can silently drop unsupported forms.
     - Invalid config types therefore survive as strings instead of errors.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse config by schema.
     - Each key should declare its allowed value type.
     - Invalid scalar/array/object shape should be a config error.

388. Release overwrite can delete arbitrary output directories.
     Problem:
     - `prepare_output/2` calls `File.rm_rf(release_dir)` when overwrite is enabled.
     - `output_dir` can be absolute or point outside the project.
     - A mistaken path can recursively delete unrelated files.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `prepare_output/2`

     Required change:
     - Normalize and validate release output path.
     - Refuse dangerous paths:
       - `/`
       - home directory
       - project root
       - parent of project root
       - empty path
     - Prefer `_build`/release-local output by default.
     - Require explicit force flag for external output dirs.

389. Release runner script interpolates name/version without shell escaping.
     Problem:
     - `write_runner/3` embeds release name and version directly into shell variables.
     - Quotes/newlines/shell metacharacters in project config can corrupt or inject shell script content.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `write_runner/3`

     Required change:
     - Validate release name/version with a strict grammar.
     - Or shell-escape every interpolated value.
     - Reject newline/control characters.

390. Release sys_config and vm_args can read files outside the project root.
     Problem:
     - `sys_config` and `vm_args` are expanded relative to project root.
     - There is no containment check.
     - A project config can pull arbitrary local files into a release.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `write_sys_config/3`
       - `write_vm_args/3`

     Required change:
     - Require these paths to stay under the project root by default.
     - External config files should require explicit allowlist/unsafe release option.
     - Release metadata should record copied config file hashes.

391. Release app version fallback fabricates `"0.0.0"`.
     Problem:
     - `read_app_vsn/1` returns `"0.0.0"` when app metadata is malformed or lacks `:vsn`.
     - Release artifacts can be assembled with fake dependency/app versions.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `read_app_vsn/1`

     Required change:
     - Missing or malformed `.app` version should be a release error.
     - Do not fabricate versions for release metadata.

392. Release copies whole ebin directories indiscriminately.
     Problem:
     - `copy_application/5` lists every file in source ebin and copies it.
     - Unexpected files, stale BEAMs, symlinks, or junk can enter the release.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `copy_application/5`

     Required change:
     - Copy only verified expected artifacts:
       - `.beam`
       - `.app`
       - optionally `.appup`
     - Reject symlinks and unexpected files in release mode.
     - Prefer a checked manifest of modules/artifacts.

393. `include_erts: true` silently succeeds when ERTS source is missing.
     Problem:
     - `maybe_copy_erts/2` returns `:ok` when ERTS directory is missing.
     - The user explicitly requested ERTS inclusion, but the release may omit it.

     File:
     - lib/cure/release.ex
       - `maybe_copy_erts/2`

     Required change:
     - If `include_erts` is true and ERTS cannot be found/copied, fail release build.
     - Only `include_erts: false` should skip ERTS.

394. Lexer can crash on malformed exponent floats.
     Problem:
     - Exponent parsing allows `1.0e`, `1e+`, etc. to reach `String.to_float/1`.
     - That can raise instead of returning a lex error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
       - `lex_exponent/1`
       - `lex_decimal/2`

     Required change:
     - Require at least one digit after `e/E` and optional sign.
     - Return `{:error, {:invalid_float_literal, span}}`, not a crash.

395. Lexer accepts overly permissive numeric underscores.
     Problem:
     - Numeric lexing removes underscores without validating placement.
     - Examples like `1_`, `1__2`, `0x_FF`, or `0b_101` can collapse to valid numbers.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex

     Required change:
     - Allow underscores only between digits.
     - Reject leading, trailing, and consecutive underscores.
     - Apply consistently to decimal, hex, and binary forms.

396. Lexer does not reliably detect unterminated regex literals.
     Problem:
     - Regex scanning consumes until `/`, then advances as if a closing slash exists.
     - `~r/foo` can be accepted or produce confusing state.
     - Escaped slash support also appears absent.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
       - `lex_regex/1`

     Required change:
     - Return an explicit unterminated-regex error if no closing `/` is found.
     - Support escaped delimiters or document/reject them clearly.
     - Add tests for `~r/a\/b/` and missing closing slash.

397. Lexer drops interpolation lexing errors.
     Problem:
     - Interpolation lexing converts inner lex errors to an empty token list and continues.
     - A string with invalid interpolation can be accepted as if the interpolation were empty.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex
       - `lex_interpolation_expr/2`
       - `lex_string_body/3`

     Required change:
     - Propagate interpolation lex errors to the outer tokenizer.
     - Include interpolation span in diagnostics.

398. Lexer column tracking is byte-based.
     Problem:
     - `advance/2` increments columns by byte count.
     - Multi-byte Unicode characters skew diagnostic columns.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/lexer.ex

     Required change:
     - Track byte offset and display column separately.
     - Display columns should be grapheme/codepoint-aware.

399. Project JSON decoder overwrites duplicate object keys.
     Problem:
     - Object parsing uses `Map.put/3`.
     - Duplicate keys silently keep the last value.
     - This is risky for registry/signing/lock metadata.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject duplicate keys in security/package metadata.
     - Include duplicated key in diagnostics.
     - If permissive JSON is needed for dev tooling, make it explicit.

400. Project JSON unicode escape decoder can crash or accept invalid scalar values.
     Problem:
     - `\uXXXX` decoding builds `<<cp::utf8>>` directly.
     - Lone surrogate halves are invalid Unicode scalar values.
     - Surrogate pairs are not handled as pairs.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject lone surrogate halves.
     - Implement surrogate-pair decoding if needed.
     - Return structured JSON parse errors instead of raising.

401. Project JSON encoder can emit invalid JSON for non-finite floats.
     Problem:
     - Float encoding uses `Float.to_string/1`.
     - JSON has no representation for NaN or Infinity.

     File:
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject non-finite floats.
     - Or encode them only through an explicit nonstandard policy.

402. Type checker unknown unary operators infer as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - Unknown unary operators return `{:ok, :any, env}`.
     - This lets malformed or future operators pass through static checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `do_infer({:unary_op, ...})`

     Required change:
     - Unknown unary operator = type error in static/final mode.
     - Dynamic/editor mode may recover with a diagnostic placeholder.

403. Record construction path does not fully validate record schema.
     Problem:
     - Record construction via function-call metadata returns `{:named, name}` after inferring args.
     - It does not itself prove:
       - record type exists,
       - every field exists,
       - required fields are present,
       - duplicate fields are rejected,
       - field values match declared field types.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - function-call branch with `record: true`

     Required change:
     - Record construction must check against checked record metadata.
     - Unknown/missing/duplicate/wrongly typed fields should be errors.
     - Do not rely on later codegen or runtime map construction.

404. Multi-clause function arity mismatch falls back to `Any`.
     Problem:
     - In multi-clause checking, if clause pattern arity differs from declared params, param types become `Any`.
     - Parser usually prevents this, but the checker should not rely on that.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `check_multi_clause/5`

     Required change:
     - Clause arity mismatch = type error.
     - Include function name, expected arity, actual arity, and clause span.

405. Malformed multi-clause bodies become `Any`.
     Problem:
     - A clause body shape other than exactly one expression returns:
       `{:ok, :any, []}`
     - Malformed or multi-expression bodies can pass.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `check_multi_clause/5`

     Required change:
     - Type-check block bodies properly, or reject malformed bodies.
     - Never use `Any` as a clause-body recovery in static/final mode.

406. Generic argument inference swallows errors as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `infer_args/2` maps argument inference errors to `{:any, env}`.
     - This feeds calls, records, collections, and other constructs.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `infer_args/2`

     Required change:
     - In static/final mode, propagate argument inference errors.
     - Use `Any` fallback only behind explicit dynamic mode.

407. Function parameter matching accepts `Any` on either side.
     Problem:
     - `params_match?/2` accepts a call when either the parameter or argument is `Any`.
     - This is the generic call-compatibility escape hatch.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex
       - `params_match?/2`

     Required change:
     - Static/final mode should not treat `Any` as universal compatibility.
     - Dynamic calls should require explicit dynamic boundary.

408. Core case evaluation crashes on malformed branch sets.
     Problem:
     - Case evaluation destructures the result of `Enum.find/2`.
     - If no branch matches the constructor, evaluation crashes.
     - Kernel coverage should prevent this, but artifact tools/evaluators should fail structurally.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex

     Required change:
     - Final-Core validation must guarantee branch coverage.
     - Evaluator/normalizer used on artifacts should return structured malformed-Core errors rather than crashing.

409. Normalizer fuel is stored in the process dictionary and is not nest-safe.
     Problem:
     - `with_fuel/2` writes a process dictionary key and deletes it after.
     - It does not restore a previous fuel value.
     - Nested normalization can erase the outer fuel configuration.

     File:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex
       - `with_fuel/2`

     Required change:
     - Save previous value and restore it in `after`.
     - Prefer explicit fuel threaded through normalization state.

410. Normalizer option validation raises.
     Problem:
     - Invalid normalization options raise `ArgumentError`.
     - Tooling/artifact validation callers should get structured errors.

     File:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex
       - `normalize_opts/1`

     Required change:
     - Add a public safe API returning:
       `{:ok, opts}` or `{:error, reason}`
     - Keep raising form only for internal assertions if needed.

411. Totality call graph counts globals in non-computational positions.
     Problem:
     - `called_globals/1` generically traverses all tuples/lists.
     - Globals appearing in types, indices, motives, or erased proof positions can count as runtime calls.
     - This muddles computational recursion with type/proof references.

     File:
     - lib/cure/core/certificate.ex

     Required change:
     - Build the termination call graph over computationally relevant positions after erasure/relevance.
     - Track type/proof references separately if needed.
     - Avoid false recursion edges from erased/type-only terms.

412. Codegen regex flags silently ignore unknown flags.
     Problem:
     - Unknown regex flags map to `nil` and are rejected by filtering, effectively disappearing.
     - A misspelled flag changes regex behavior without an error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_regex_flags/2`

     Required change:
     - Unknown regex flag = compile error.
     - Lexer/parser should preserve source span for the bad flag.

413. Malformed regex literal compiles to `:undefined`.
     Problem:
     - Fallback regex literal compilation returns the atom `:undefined`.
     - This is another codegen recovery path that creates a normal value.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_regex_literal/2`

     Required change:
     - Malformed regex AST = codegen error.
     - Codegen should assert parser/checker invariants.

414. String interpolation uses unconstrained Erlang formatting.
     Problem:
     - Non-string/non-number interpolation values are rendered with:
       `io_lib:format("~p", [value])`
     - This is effectively unconstrained `Show` for arbitrary values.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `wrap_to_iodata/2`

     Required change:
     - Static mode should require `Show(T)` or explicit `to_string`.
     - Dynamic mode may keep fallback formatting.
     - Interpolation should track effects and partiality of conversion.

415. Comprehension codegen assumes non-empty children and known qualifier shapes.
     Problem:
     - `compile_comprehension/3` destructures `[body | qualifiers]`.
     - Malformed empty children crash.
     - Unknown qualifier forms can crash or be mishandled.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_comprehension/3`

     Required change:
     - Parser/checker should validate comprehension shape.
     - Codegen should return structured errors for malformed comprehension AST.

416. Binary segment size expressions can inherit generic codegen fallback behavior.
     Problem:
     - Segment size expressions are compiled through general expression codegen.
     - If that path recovers to `:undefined` or another placeholder, binary construction becomes malformed/surprising.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - binary segment helpers

     Required change:
     - Binary segment size must type-check as a non-negative integer.
     - Invalid/malformed size expression = compile error.

417. Binary segment type spec silently drops malformed `unit`.
     Problem:
     - Segment `unit` is included only when it is a literal integer.
     - Malformed unit metadata is ignored and defaults apply.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - binary segment type-spec helpers

     Required change:
     - Invalid unit/type/signedness/endianness must be a checked error.
     - Do not silently fall back to Erlang binary defaults.

418. Attribute access lowers to partial `maps:get/2`.
     Problem:
     - Attribute access compiles to `:maps.get(field, object)`.
     - Missing field raises.
     - This is unsafe for dynamic maps and should not be the representation of checked record projection.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_attribute_access/3`

     Required change:
     - Split checked record projection from dynamic map lookup.
     - Checked record projection should only target known fields.
     - Dynamic field lookup should return `Option`/`Result` or require unsafe.

419. Catch/finally codegen silently drops malformed trailing parts.
     Problem:
     - Catch/finally compilation splits match arms from non-match parts.
     - It accepts exactly one finally body; other malformed trailing parts can be ignored.

     File:
     - lib/cure/compiler/codegen.ex
       - `compile_catch_and_finally/3`

     Required change:
     - Parser should produce a precise try/catch/finally AST.
     - Codegen should reject more than one finally body or unknown trailing parts.

420. Type export skips read/parse failures.
     Problem:
     - `ExportTypes.export/2` maps failed files to `[]`.
     - A generated schema can omit types from files that failed to parse.

     File:
     - lib/cure/export_types.ex

     Required change:
     - Strict export mode should fail on any read/lex/parse error.
     - Best-effort export should report skipped files explicitly.

421. Type export output filenames can collide by basename.
     Problem:
     - Output path is based on `Path.basename(source_file, ".cure")`.
     - `lib/foo/types.cure` and `lib/bar/types.cure` can both emit `types.proto`.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/export_types.ex
     - lib/cure/export_types/protobuf.ex

     Required change:
     - Use module name or relative path in output filename.
     - Detect collisions before writing.
     - Include source paths in collision diagnostics.

422. HTML docs generation uses bang I/O and crashes on ordinary filesystem errors.
     Problem:
     - Docs generation uses `File.mkdir_p!`, `File.write!`, `File.read!`, etc.
     - Publish/doc builds should return structured errors.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/html_generator.ex

     Required change:
     - Replace bang I/O with `{:ok, _} | {:error, reason}` flow.
     - Return a doc build failure summary.

423. HTML docs sidebar contains duplicated navigation markup.
     Problem:
     - Sidebar renderer emits nested/duplicated `<nav class="cure-doc-nav">`.
     - Not type-soundness, but generated HTML is structurally wrong.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/html_generator.ex
       - `render_sidebar/1`

     Required change:
     - Remove duplicate `<nav>`.
     - Add HTML snapshot/golden test.

424. HTML docs logo path is inserted without asset/root policy.
     Problem:
     - Logo path is escaped, but not copied, rooted, or validated.
     - Docs can point to external URLs or files outside the package.

     File:
     - lib/cure/doc/html_generator.ex

     Required change:
     - For publish docs, copy assets into docs output and reference local copies.
     - External logos should require explicit config.
     - Reject path traversal.

425. Telemetry bridge swallows subscription failures.
     Problem:
     - Telemetry init rescues failures from pipeline event subscription and returns `:ok`.
     - Observability may silently be disabled.

     File:
     - lib/cure/telemetry.ex

     Required change:
     - Return/report subscription failures.
     - CI tests for telemetry should fail if subscription fails.
     - Runtime should expose degraded telemetry status.

426. Telemetry re-emits full compiler payloads.
     Problem:
     - Telemetry events include `payload: payload`.
     - Payloads can include ASTs, tokens, source snippets, paths, types, or diagnostics.
     - This may leak source into external telemetry subscribers.

     File:
     - lib/cure/telemetry.ex

     Required change:
     - Redact/summarize payloads by default.
     - Full payload export should require explicit opt-in.
     - Avoid sending source text/tokens to telemetry by default.

427. Diagnostic bundle collects sensitive environment and project data.
     Problem:
     - `Cure.John` collects environment variables, project metadata, dependency data, registry data, logs, and crash dump excerpts.
     - Useful locally, but risky for shareable bug reports.

     File:
     - lib/cure/john.ex

     Required change:
     - Default to redacted/shareable output.
     - Require `--include-sensitive` or equivalent for full local diagnostics.
     - Mark sensitive sections clearly.

428. Diagnostic bundle reads logs/crash dumps without enough sharing policy.
     Problem:
     - `.cure/logs`, `_build/cure/logs`, and `erl_crash.dump` can contain secrets, source, env values, or paths.
     - Bundling/tailing these by default can leak data.

     File:
     - lib/cure/john.ex

     Required change:
     - Cap reads.
     - Redact common secrets/tokens.
     - Require explicit `--include-logs` for shareable reports.

429. REPL history persists commands without a visible redaction/permissions policy.
     Problem:
     - REPL history can store source snippets, tokens, secrets, or local paths.
     - The history layer should be treated as sensitive user data.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/repl/history.ex
     - lib/cure/repl.ex

     Required change:
     - Ensure history file mode is `0600`.
     - Provide easy history disablement.
     - Consider redacting obvious secrets.
     - Document storage location and retention.

430. REPL docs can read from arbitrary user-loaded paths.
     Problem:
     - REPL docs search includes paths loaded during the session.
     - That is useful interactively, but those docs should not be confused with trusted stdlib/project docs.

     Files:
     - lib/cure/repl/docs.ex
     - lib/cure/repl.ex

     Required change:
     - Mark loaded-path docs as untrusted/session-local.
     - Do not include them in project/release docs unless explicitly configured.
     - Apply root/path policy if exporting docs.
431. `mix cure.verify` extracts package tarballs fully into memory.
     Problem:
     - `verify_tarball/2` uses:
       `:erl_tar.extract(path, [:compressed, :memory])`
     - A large or malicious tarball can consume significant memory before any proof artifact is inspected.
     - There is no entry count, total size, or per-entry size limit.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.verify.ex

     Required change:
     - Stream tar entries or preflight tar metadata.
     - Add maximum tarball size, maximum entry count, and maximum `.cureproof` size.
     - Reject oversized verification inputs with structured errors.

432. `mix cure.verify` directory mode uses `File.read!/1`.
     Problem:
     - `verify_directory/2` reads every `.cureproof` file with `File.read!`.
     - Permission errors, truncation, or ordinary filesystem failures crash the Mix task rather than producing a structured verification failure.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.verify.ex

     Required change:
     - Replace `File.read!/1` with structured read errors.
     - Continue or fail according to verification policy, but report the exact file/reason.

433. `mix cure.verify` verifies proof artifacts without checking package/source binding.
     Problem:
     - The task locates any `*.cureproof` entries and verifies their internal certificate list.
     - It does not prove that those certificates correspond to the Cure sources/modules in the tarball or directory being verified.
     - A tarball could contain stale or unrelated proof artifacts.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.verify.ex

     Required change:
     - Proof artifacts must include:
       - package name/version,
       - source/final-Core hashes,
       - compiler/core version,
       - module list,
       - dependency/prelude hashes.
     - `cure.verify` must check that the artifact matches the package contents.

434. `mix cure.verify` non-strict mode exits successfully with no proofs.
     Problem:
     - Missing `.cureproof` artifacts are only informational unless `--strict` is passed.
     - This is acceptable for dev, but dangerous if CI/release tooling accidentally uses the default.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.verify.ex

     Required change:
     - Make verified/release install mode strict by default.
     - Keep non-strict behavior only for explicit advisory/dev verification.
     - Print mode clearly.

435. Stdlib BEAM bundling ignores the final error count.
     Problem:
     - `Mix.Tasks.Cure.BundleStdlibBeams.run/1` calls:
       `_ = bundle(...)`
       then returns `:ok`.
     - `bundle/2` accumulates `%{errors: n}`, but `run/1` does not fail when `n > 0`.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib_beams.ex

     Required change:
     - If any stdlib source fails to compile, the task should fail in normal/release builds.
     - Best-effort staging should require an explicit flag.

436. Stdlib BEAM bundling silently no-ops when the compiler is unavailable.
     Problem:
     - `bundle/2` returns success with zero work when `compiler_available?/0` is false.
     - The moduledoc frames this as first-compile tolerance, but it can leave packaged stdlib BEAMs stale/missing.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib_beams.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - bootstrap mode: no-op permitted
       - packaging/release mode: compiler unavailable = error.
     - Emit a structured reason, not a success count.

437. Stdlib BEAM bundling uses mtime-only freshness.
     Problem:
     - `should_compile?/2` recompiles only if source mtime is newer than BEAM mtime.
     - It ignores:
       - compiler version,
       - stdlib dependency changes,
       - build flags,
       - prelude/builtin registry changes,
       - dependency BEAM changes.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib_beams.ex

     Required change:
     - Track source hash + compiler version + build flags.
     - Recompile when any build-relevant input changes.
     - Release bundles should be content-addressed or manifest-driven.

438. Stdlib module detection by regex is too weak.
     Problem:
     - `module_name_from_source/1` uses a regex over raw source:
       `^\s*(?:mod|proof|actor|fsm|sup|app)\s+...`
     - It does not use the lexer/parser.
     - It may match inside comments/strings or miss valid syntax.
     - It treats proof/actor/fsm/sup/app containers as stdlib module names for BEAM placement.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib_beams.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse the source and read the real top-level container declaration.
     - Reject ambiguous/missing module names in release bundling.
     - Do not infer trusted module names from regex.

439. Stdlib source bundling ignores copy failures by using bang I/O.
     Problem:
     - `Mix.Tasks.Cure.BundleStdlib.bundle/2` uses `File.mkdir_p!` and `File.cp!`.
     - Ordinary filesystem errors crash rather than returning structured build failures.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib.ex

     Required change:
     - Use structured file errors.
     - Release packaging should produce a precise failure report.

440. Stdlib source bundling also uses mtime-only freshness.
     Problem:
     - `should_copy?/2` copies only when source mtime is newer.
     - It can leave stale staged sources if clocks are skewed or files are restored with old mtimes.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.bundle_stdlib.ex

     Required change:
     - Use content hashes for staged stdlib sources.
     - Record a stdlib source manifest.

441. Example regression task runs examples through unchecked/default compiler mode.
     Problem:
     - `mix cure.check.examples` calls `Cure.Compiler.compile_file/2` and `compile_and_load/2` without forcing checked/static mode.
     - Given the project/compiler defaults elsewhere, examples may pass without the static/dependent checks intended for the language.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.check.examples.ex

     Required change:
     - Force `check_types: true` and the intended checker mode.
     - Split examples into:
       - dynamic examples,
       - static examples,
       - dependent examples.
     - Report checker mode for every example.

442. Example expected outputs still depend on `:cure_refl`.
     Problem:
     - `@expected` includes:
       - `"equality_laws" => ":cure_refl"`
       - `"equality_proofs" => ":cure_refl"`
     - This is direct regression coverage for the proof-token equality model that is supposed to be removed.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.check.examples.ex

     Required change:
     - Rewrite equality examples around canonical builtin `Eq`.
     - Since proofs erase, expected runtime output should not be the proof token.
     - Remove `:cure_refl` from examples and tests.

443. Example runner detects `main` with a regex.
     Problem:
     - `main_fn?/1` uses:
       `String.match?(src, ~r/\bfn\s+main\b/)`
     - This can match comments, strings, or nested examples.
     - It can also miss valid syntax.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.check.examples.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse the file and inspect top-level declarations.
     - Do not decide execution behavior from regex.

444. Example runner loads compiled example modules into the current VM.
     Problem:
     - `compile_and_load/2` loads example modules into the Mix task VM.
     - Examples can leave modules/processes/global state behind and affect later examples.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.check.examples.ex

     Required change:
     - Run executable examples in isolated processes or fresh VM contexts.
     - At minimum, compile to unique module names and clean up runtime state where possible.

445. `mix cure.synth` parses effects by `String.to_atom/1`.
     Problem:
     - `parse_effects/1` converts every CLI effect name into an atom.
     - User CLI input can create arbitrary atoms.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.synth.ex

     Required change:
     - Use a closed effect enum.
     - Unknown effect name = CLI error.
     - Do not atomize arbitrary strings.

446. `mix cure.synth` silently drops malformed context bindings.
     Problem:
     - `parse_ctx/1` ignores entries that are not `name=Type`.
     - A typo in the context can remove a binding and produce misleading candidates.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.synth.ex

     Required change:
     - Malformed context binding should be an error.
     - Validate names and type strings.
     - Report exact bad binding.

447. `mix cure.synth` accepts unbounded `--max` and `--depth`.
     Problem:
     - `max_results` and `depth` come directly from CLI options.
     - Very large values can force expensive synthesis work.

     File:
     - lib/mix/tasks/cure.synth.ex

     Required change:
     - Add sane maximums.
     - Reject negative or excessive values.
     - Report synthesis budget in output.

448. `Cure.Types.Checker.check_module/2` accepts non-module ASTs as typechecked.
     Problem:
     - `check_module_dispatch/4` returns `{:ok, ast}` for:
       - unknown container types,
       - blocks with no module/proof container,
       - arbitrary AST shapes.
     - This is useful for REPL snippets, but unsafe for file/module checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Split APIs:
       - `check_module_file/2`: requires a module/container and rejects unknown top-level shapes.
       - `check_expression/2`: for REPL snippets.
     - Static/release file checking must not accept arbitrary non-module AST as a checked module.

449. Unknown container types are accepted.
     Problem:
     - If a container has a `:container_type` other than `:module` or `:proof`, the legacy checker returns success.
     - Actor/FSM/app/sup containers may therefore avoid normal type checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Every container kind must have an explicit checker or explicit runtime/dynamic classification.
     - Unknown container type = checker error.

450. Empty or malformed blocks are accepted as modules.
     Problem:
     - A `{:block, _, children}` with no recognized module/proof container returns `{:ok, ast}`.
     - A malformed file can therefore be accepted by the checker if parsing recovered into a block.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - File-level checking should require a valid top-level declaration.
     - LSP/editor parse recovery may keep partial blocks, but final checking must fail.

451. Record field default checking falls back to `Any` for unknown fields.
     Problem:
     - During record schema registration:
       `declared = Map.get(field_map, field_name, :any)`
     - A default for an unknown/misspelled field can be checked against `Any`.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown record field in defaults = error.
     - Duplicate/misspelled field names should be caught during record declaration registration.

452. Record type identities are lowercase atoms.
     Problem:
     - Record registration stores:
       `{:record, String.to_atom(String.downcase(name)), field_map}`
     - This loses module qualification and case distinctions.
     - It can collide with other records or ADTs.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Use qualified record type identities.
     - Avoid `String.to_atom/1`.
     - Align record identity with final Core/global symbol representation.

453. ADT constructor parameter types are deliberately erased to `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `register_adt_container/4` explicitly creates:
       `param_types = List.duplicate(:any, length(raw_params))`
     - Constructor calls are therefore checked only by arity, not by declared field types.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Parse and store real constructor parameter types.
     - Instantiate type parameters properly.
     - Reject constructor calls with wrong argument types.

454. Multi-clause inferred parameter types fall back to `Any`.
     Problem:
     - If a function clause’s pattern count does not align with declared params, the checker falls back to:
       `Enum.map(params, &{&1, :any})`
     - This hides clause arity/shape bugs.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Clause arity mismatch should be an error.
     - Do not produce `Any` parameter types for malformed clauses.

455. Multi-clause malformed body returns `Any`.
     Problem:
     - A malformed clause body shape can return:
       `{:ok, :any, []}`
     - That lets bad function clauses pass until codegen/runtime.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject malformed clause bodies.
     - Or type-check block bodies explicitly if multi-expression clauses are supported.

456. Literal type fallback is `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer/2` for literals falls back to `:any` for unknown literal subtypes.
     - Unknown/new literal classes can enter the checker as dynamic.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown literal subtype = error in static/final mode.
     - Parser recovery literals should carry an invalid marker that final checking rejects.

457. `_` variable in expression position infers as `Any`.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer(env, {:variable, _, "_"})` returns `:any`.
     - `_` should be a pattern wildcard/ignored binding, not a value expression.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Reject `_` in expression position.
     - Treat `_` as wildcard only in syntactic binding/pattern positions.
     - Separate wildcard, anonymous hole, and ignored binding syntax.

458. Binary operation inference has a final `Any` fallback.
     Problem:
     - If a binary operator does not match the known operator cases, inference returns:
       `{:ok, :any, env}`
     - Unknown operator syntax can therefore pass legacy checking.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Unknown binary operator = type error in static/final mode.
     - Dev/editor mode may recover with diagnostics only.

459. String interpolation infers `String` without checking interpolated parts.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer({:string_interpolation, ...})` returns `:string`.
     - It does not require `Show(T)`/`ToString(T)` evidence for interpolated expressions.
     - It also does not track effects from interpolated expressions.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Infer/check each interpolation expression.
     - Require explicit conversion or `Show(T)`.
     - Track effects.

460. `early_return` and `yield` are typed as their expression value without control effects.
     Problem:
     - `early_return` and `yield` infer the inner expression type.
     - They are control-flow effects, not ordinary expressions.
     - Treating them as plain values can make surrounding code appear total/pure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Add control-effect typing:
       - return effect,
       - generator/yield effect.
     - Static/total code should either reject or explicitly type these constructs.

461. Empty maps infer as `Map(Any, Any)`.
     Problem:
     - `do_infer({:map, _, pairs})` returns `{:map, :any, :any}` for empty maps.
     - Empty collections need expected-type checking or annotations in static code.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - In checking mode, infer empty map from expected type.
     - In inference-only static mode, require annotation.
     - Do not default to `Any`.

462. Lambda parameters without annotations become `Any`.
     Problem:
     - Lambda inference uses parameter metadata and falls back to `Any` for missing/unknown parameter types.
     - This is too permissive for static/dependent mode.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Either:
       - infer lambda parameter types from expected function type in checking mode,
       - or require annotations when no expected type exists.
     - Do not default unannotated static lambdas to `Any`.

463. `Std.List.contains` uses unconstrained structural equality.
     Problem:
     - `contains(list, elem)` uses:
       `h == elem`
     - It is polymorphic over `T` without requiring `Eq(T)`/`DecEq(T)`.
     - This repeats the generic runtime equality problem in collection APIs.

     File:
     - lib/std/list.cure

     Required change:
     - Require runtime equality evidence:
       `contains : Eq(T) => List(T) -> T -> Bool`
     - Or provide concrete primitive-specialized versions only.
     - Do not use universal structural equality in checked polymorphic stdlib.

464. `Std.List.filter/find/any/all` predicate effects are not modeled.
     Problem:
     - Predicate type is written as:
       `T -> Bool`
     - A predicate can be effectful/partial in the current language unless effects are tracked in function types.
     - The list helper signatures claim pure transformations.

     File:
     - lib/std/list.cure

     Required change:
     - Function types must carry effects.
     - List combinators should either require pure predicates or propagate predicate effects:
       `filter : (T -> Bool ! e) -> List(T) -> List(T) ! e`

465. `Std.List.nth` accepts negative indices and returns the default by recursion.
     Problem:
     - Negative `idx` is not rejected or represented in the type.
     - It eventually hits `[]` and returns `default`, conflating out-of-range and negative index.

     File:
     - lib/std/list.cure

     Required change:
     - Use `Nat`/`NonNegativeInt` for indices.
     - Or return `Option(T)` / `Result(T, IndexError)`.
     - Keep defaulting helpers explicitly named, e.g. `nth_or`.

466. `Std.Map.from_list` accepts untyped tuple entries.
     Problem:
     - `from_list : List(Tuple) -> Map`
     - It does not specify that each tuple must be a 2-tuple of `{K,V}`.
     - Bad tuple shapes can raise or create unexpected maps through Erlang `:maps.from_list/1`.

     File:
     - lib/std/map.cure

     Required change:
     - Use a typed pair/list representation:
       `List(Pair(K,V)) -> Map(K,V)`
     - Or return `Result(Map(K,V), Error)` for dynamic input.
     - Do not expose raw `List(Tuple)` in checked API.

467. `Std.Map.get_or` still calls partial `get` after a separate membership check.
     Problem:
     - `get_or` uses `has_key(key, map) -> get(key, map)`.
     - This is fine in a pure immutable map, but if maps/dynamic values cross FFI or effects, the separated check/get pattern is less robust.
     - More importantly, the checked API still depends on the partial `get` existing as a normal function.

     File:
     - lib/std/map.cure

     Required change:
     - Provide one primitive/runtime helper that implements safe lookup atomically:
       `lookup : Map(K,V) -> K -> Option(V)`
     - Keep raw `get` in unsafe/partial tier.

468. `Std.Gen.int_in` hides invalid ranges by returning `lo`.
     Problem:
     - `int_in(lo, hi)` returns `lo` when `hi <= lo`.
     - An invalid range is treated as a valid deterministic generator.
     - This can mask bad property-test inputs.

     File:
     - lib/std/gen.cure

     Required change:
     - Use:
       - `int_in : (lo : Int) -> (hi : Int) -> Proof(lo <= hi) -> Gen(Int)`
       - or `Result(Int, GenError)`
       - or rename to `int_in_or_lo`.
     - Do not silently repair invalid generator ranges in checked API.

469. `Std.Gen.shrink(v : T) -> List(T)` is a runtime dynamic cast.
     Problem:
     - Runtime shrink dispatches on runtime shape.
     - For unknown types it returns `[]`.
     - The signature claims it can shrink any `T` and return `List(T)`.

     Files:
     - lib/std/gen.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_gen.ex

     Required change:
     - Use a `Shrink(T)` protocol/dictionary.
     - Keep runtime shape-based shrink only in dynamic test tier.
     - Do not claim universal shrinkability.

470. `Std.Test.forall_shrunk` return type is unconstrained.
     Problem:
     - Signature:
       `forall_shrunk(gen: Atom -> T, property: T -> Bool, runs: Int) -> T`
     - Runtime returns `:ok` on success and raises on failure.
     - The return type `T` is misleading and can be instantiated as anything.

     Files:
     - lib/std/test.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_test.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `Result(Unit, PropertyFailure(T))` or `Atom`/`Unit` for success.
     - Mark raising variants as test-only/partial.
     - Do not return arbitrary `T`.

471. `cure_std_test` swallows shrinker exceptions.
     Problem:
     - `shrinks/1` rescues any shrinker error and returns `[]`.
     - A broken shrinker is treated as “no shrinks available.”

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_test.ex

     Required change:
     - Distinguish:
       - no shrinker,
       - shrinker returned empty list,
       - shrinker crashed.
     - Property-test output should report shrinker crashes.

472. `cure_std_test` treats property exceptions as boolean failure.
     Problem:
     - `holds?/2` rescues/catches exceptions and returns `false`.
     - A property crash is reported like an ordinary failed property, losing error class/stack.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_test.ex

     Required change:
     - Return structured property outcomes:
       - passed,
       - falsified,
       - crashed.
     - Include crash reason/stack in test output.

473. `Std.Option.zip` returns an untyped `Tuple`.
     Problem:
     - `zip(a: Option(T), b: Option(U)) -> Option(Tuple)`
     - The successful tuple shape is known but not represented.

     File:
     - lib/std/option.cure

     Required change:
     - Return a typed pair:
       `Option(Pair(T, U))`
     - Or introduce a proper product type in the stdlib.
     - Avoid raw `Tuple` in checked APIs.

474. `Std.Result` and `Std.Core` duplicate Result helpers.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Core` defines Result helpers and `Std.Result` defines another set.
     - Divergence in semantics, docs, effects, or ABI can creep in.

     Files:
     - lib/std/core.cure
     - lib/std/result.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep Result helpers in one authoritative module.
     - Re-export from `Std.Core` if necessary.
     - Add ABI tests proving both names lower to the same constructors/functions if re-exported.

475. `Std.Time.zone` also uses raising `DateTime.from_unix!/2`.
     Problem:
     - Earlier format issues apply to zone conversion too:
       `DateTime.from_unix!(micros + offset_seconds * 1_000_000, :microsecond)`
     - Out-of-range adjusted timestamps raise, but signature returns `Result(Zoned, ParseError)` only for unknown zone parsing.

     Files:
     - lib/std/time.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_time.ex

     Required change:
     - Catch/avoid `from_unix!`.
     - Return `OutOfRange` when adjusted timestamp cannot be represented.
     - Add tests for extreme instants plus offsets.

476. `Std.Time.add` can overflow the runtime integer/date range later.
     Problem:
     - `add` constructs an `Instant` by raw integer addition.
     - It does not validate whether the resulting instant can be formatted/zoned later.
     - This pushes failure into later operations.

     Files:
     - lib/std/time.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_time.ex

     Required change:
     - Either allow arbitrary mathematical instants and make formatting return `Result`,
       or validate instants at construction.
     - Be explicit about the representable time range.

477. `Stdlib.Paths` chooses the first existing override silently.
     Problem:
     - Source/beam dirs are selected by order and `File.dir?/1`.
     - If multiple candidates exist, the first wins silently.
     - This can hide an unexpected `CURE_LIB`/`CURE_HOME`/configured path shadowing the bundled stdlib.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/paths.ex

     Required change:
     - In dev mode, print/debug which stdlib path is selected.
     - In static/release mode, require exactly the trusted bundled path unless explicitly configured and hash-pinned.
     - Detect multiple candidates and report precedence.

478. `Stdlib.Paths` ignores unreadable configured directories by filtering them out.
     Problem:
     - Candidate dirs are filtered by `File.dir?/1`.
     - A configured-but-unreadable/missing stdlib path can silently fall back to another stdlib location.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/paths.ex

     Required change:
     - If the user explicitly configures a stdlib path, failure to use it should be an error.
     - Only implicit fallback candidates should be silently skipped.

479. `Stdlib.Preload.preload/1` promises success even when nothing is loaded.
     Problem:
     - The module docs and spec say preload always returns `:ok`.
     - That makes it impossible for callers to know whether required stdlib modules are actually available.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Return a structured result:
       `{:ok, loaded}` or `{:error, missing_or_failed}`
     - Keep best-effort preload only for REPL/dev with explicit option.

480. `Stdlib.Preload.compile_missing_from_sources/1` swallows source compile failures.
     Problem:
     - JIT compile from source catches/rescues and returns `:ok` on failure.
     - Missing BEAMs can remain missing without a hard preload failure.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - In trusted mode, source compile failure = error.
     - In dev mode, report which module failed and why.

481. `Stdlib.Preload.load_if_present/2` treats corrupt BEAM as success.
     Problem:
     - If `:code.load_binary/3` returns `{:error, reason}`, the function returns `:ok`.
     - Corrupt/mismatched BEAMs are indistinguishable from successful loads.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Return `{:error, {:beam_load_failed, module, reason}}`.
     - Static/release preload must fail on corrupt required BEAMs.

482. `Stdlib.Preload.discover_from_beams/1` trusts `__group__/0` execution.
     Problem:
     - Discovery loads a BEAM and calls `module.__group__/0`.
     - `__group__/0` is arbitrary code from that module, not inert metadata.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Use a signed/generated metadata file for stdlib module groups.
     - Do not execute candidate stdlib BEAM code for discovery in trusted builds.

483. `Stdlib.Preload` silently classifies failed `__group__/0` calls as `:core`.
     Problem:
     - When `__group__/0` raises/catches, discovery falls back to `:core`.
     - A broken stdlib module can be imported as a core module.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/preload.ex

     Required change:
     - Failure to read group metadata should be an error in trusted mode.
     - Do not default broken modules into `:core`.

484. `Cure.Types.Checker` still supports proof containers on the legacy path.
     Problem:
     - `check_module_dispatch/4` routes `:proof` containers to `check_proof_body/4`.
     - This is separate from final Core proof/equality checking.
     - Earlier recommendation was to remove permissive proof containers; this is the exact remaining checker seam.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Delete proof container checking from the trusted checker.
     - Migrate proofs to ordinary erased checked definitions.
     - Reject legacy `proof` containers in static/final mode.

485. Proof-container shape check only inspects declared return syntax.
     Problem:
     - `check_proof_body/4` requires each function’s declared return type to be `Eq(...)` or refinement-like.
     - It does not make the proof body a kernel-checked equality witness.
     - A function can satisfy the syntactic shape while still relying on token proofs or legacy refinement behavior.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Remove this path or lower proof functions to Core and check them there.
     - Syntactic return-shape checks are not proof verification.

486. `Cure.Types.Checker` installs stdlib signatures before knowing module mode.
     Problem:
     - `check_module/2` always starts with:
       `Stdlib.install_qualified()`
       `Stdlib.install_qualified_types()`
     - This pulls legacy stdlib signature metadata into both legacy and dependent-looking checks before the final mode decision.
     - If stdlib metadata contains `Any`/unsafe extern signatures, they are available unless carefully quarantined later.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Choose checker mode first.
     - Install only the stdlib tier appropriate for that mode.
     - Dependent/static mode should import checked Core/prelude metadata, not legacy stdlib signatures.

487. Dependent checker result is converted back to the original AST.
     Problem:
     - `check_dependent_module/1` returns `{:ok, ast}` after `Cure.Elab.Program.check_ast(ast)` succeeds.
     - The checked Core environment is discarded.
     - Downstream legacy code can still operate on the original unchecked-ish AST.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Return checked elaboration artifacts:
       - Core env,
       - final-Core readiness result,
       - diagnostics,
       - checked module metadata.
     - Do not collapse dependent success back into the raw surface AST.

488. Dependent type errors lose source spans.
     Problem:
     - `check_dependent_module/1` reports all dependent type errors with:
       `[line: 0]`
     - This prevents tooling from locating the actual failing source construct.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Preserve elaborator/kernel source spans.
     - At minimum, attach declaration/function name and source line.

489. `Map.get(field_map, field_name, :any)` appears in checked record default path.
     Problem:
     - This is a concrete `Any` leak in record declaration checking, distinct from later field access.
     - A mistyped default field name is not guaranteed to fail.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Replace with explicit unknown-field error.
     - Record declaration registration should validate all field/default pairs.

490. `Std.Io.print_int` and `print_float` are missing the `Io` effect.
     Problem:
     - They call `println`, which is effectful:
       `println : String -> Atom ! Io`
     - But their signatures omit `! Io`.

     File:
     - lib/std/io.cure

     Required change:
     - Change:
       `fn print_int(n: Int) -> Atom`
       `fn print_float(f: Float) -> Atom`
     - To:
       `fn print_int(n: Int) -> Atom ! Io`
       `fn print_float(f: Float) -> Atom ! Io`
     - Add effect propagation tests for stdlib wrappers.

491. `Std.Math.log`, `log2`, `log10`, and `sqrt` are domain-partial.
     Problem:
     - Math wrappers expose:
       `sqrt : Float -> Float`
       `log : Float -> Float`
       etc.
     - Runtime math may return NaN or raise/behave specially depending on input and BEAM math semantics.
     - The type does not express domain restrictions or NaN.

     File:
     - lib/std/math.cure

     Required change:
     - Use domain-refined inputs where mathematically partial:
       - `NonNegativeFloat` for sqrt
       - `PositiveFloat` for log
     - Or return `Result(Float, MathError)`.
     - Keep raw BEAM math wrappers in runtime/unsafe tier.

492. `Std.Math.pow` is not total over all Float pairs in a proof-friendly sense.
     Problem:
     - `pow(base, exp)` exposes raw floating behavior.
     - Negative bases with fractional exponents, infinities, NaNs, and overflow make this unsuitable for type-level or proof-relevant computation.

     File:
     - lib/std/math.cure

     Required change:
     - Classify as runtime float primitive only.
     - Do not reduce in typechecking.
     - Consider `Result` or documented IEEE-like semantics if retained.

493. `Std.Show` exposes raw `atom_to_binary/1`.
     Problem:
     - `atom_to_binary` can expose internal atoms and module/generated names.
     - It is pure runtime conversion, but for checked formatting it should be part of a deliberate `Show(Atom)` policy.

     File:
     - lib/std/show.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep only behind `Show(Atom)` implementation.
     - Ensure it does not get used as unconstrained generic display.

494. `Std.String.to_int` and `to_float` are partial externs.
     Problem:
     - `binary_to_integer/1` and `binary_to_float/1` raise on invalid input.
     - Signatures claim:
       `String -> Int`
       `String -> Float`

     File:
     - lib/std/string.cure

     Required change:
     - Replace with:
       `parse_int : String -> Result(Int, ParseError)`
       `parse_float : String -> Result(Float, ParseError)`
     - Move raising versions to `parse_int_bang` / unsafe tier if needed.

495. `Std.String.split` wraps non-global `:binary.split/2`.
     Problem:
     - Docs/examples imply ordinary split, but `:binary.split/2` splits once by default.
     - This is a contract bug that can mislead users.

     File:
     - lib/std/string.cure

     Required change:
     - Rename to `split_once`, or use `:binary.split/3` with `[:global]`.
     - Add tests for multiple separators.

496. `Std.String.repeat` accepts arbitrary `Int` count.
     Problem:
     - `:binary.copy/2` raises for negative counts.
     - Signature is:
       `String -> Int -> String`

     File:
     - lib/std/string.cure

     Required change:
     - Use `Nat`/`NonNegativeInt`.
     - Or return `Result(String, Error)`.

497. `Std.Pair.from_list` invents values for wrong lengths.
     Problem:
     - Docs say:
       - single-element lists are duplicated,
       - empty or longer lists fall back to `%[nil, nil]`.
     - That hides shape errors and introduces `nil` into an unconstrained tuple type.

     File:
     - lib/std/pair.cure

     Required change:
     - Return `Option(Pair(T,T))` or `Result`.
     - Do not synthesize `%[nil, nil]` in checked code.
     - Use explicit `pair_or_default` if desired.

498. `Std.Pair.to_list` claims homogeneous `List(T)` from an untyped tuple.
     Problem:
     - `to_list(pair: Tuple) -> List(T)` assumes both tuple elements have the same caller-chosen type `T`.
     - Runtime tuple may be heterogeneous.

     File:
     - lib/std/pair.cure

     Required change:
     - Use typed pair:
       `Pair(A, B)`.
     - `to_list` should only exist for homogeneous pairs or return `List(Dynamic)`.

499. `Std.Set` represents sets as raw maps.
     Problem:
     - `Set` operations use `Map` directly and `to_list(set) -> List(T)`.
     - There is no type-level guarantee that map keys are `T` or values are the expected sentinel.

     File:
     - lib/std/set.cure

     Required change:
     - Define `Set(T)` as an abstract/opaque type.
     - Keep raw-map set helpers in dynamic tier only.

500. `Std.Access` tuple element access uses 0-based API over 1-based BEAM access without type-level bounds.
     Problem:
     - `elem_at(i)` later calls `Std.Pair.element(i + 1, container)`.
     - Negative indices or out-of-range indices can raise through `:erlang.element/2`.

     File:
     - lib/std/access.cure

     Required change:
     - Use `Nat`/bounded indices for checked tuple access.
     - Dynamic access should return `Option`/`Result`.
     - Raising accessors should live in unsafe/partial tier.

501. `Std.Access` update callbacks use `Any -> Any`.
     Problem:
     - Deep update functions erase field/container types and accept arbitrary dynamic callbacks.
     - This is useful for Elixir-style dynamic access, but not a checked static API.

     File:
     - lib/std/access.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep `Std.Access` in dynamic stdlib tier.
     - Provide typed lenses/optics separately if Cure wants checked deep updates.

502. `Std.Refine.PositiveFloat` and `Probability` use Float predicates as refinements.
     Problem:
     - Float comparison in refinements inherits NaN/ordering corner cases.
     - SMT/logic treatment of floats is much harder than integer refinements.

     File:
     - lib/std/refine.cure

     Required change:
     - Treat float refinements as runtime smart constructors unless Cure has a deliberate real/float logic.
     - Do not feed ordinary BEAM float predicates directly into trusted proof obligations.

503. `Std.Core.min/max/clamp` are unconstrained generic comparisons.
     Problem:
     - They use `<`, `>`, and related comparisons over arbitrary `T`.
     - This relies on universal runtime ordering rather than `Ord(T)` evidence.

     File:
     - lib/std/core.cure

     Required change:
     - Require `Ord(T)` evidence.
     - Or restrict to concrete numeric/string/atom types.
     - Return `Ordering`-based APIs after `Ord` is fixed.

504. `Std.Core.ne` uses universal structural inequality.
     Problem:
     - `ne(x: T, y: T) -> Bool = x != y`
     - This bypasses `Eq(T)` evidence and repeats the runtime/propositional equality confusion.

     File:
     - lib/std/core.cure

     Required change:
     - Require runtime equality evidence:
       `Eq(T) => T -> T -> Bool`
     - Keep primitive equality only for concrete primitive types.

505. `Std.Ord` Float implementation documents NaN as BEAM term-order.
     Problem:
     - `Ord for Float` uses ordinary `<`/`>` and says NaNs follow BEAM term-order.
     - This is not a lawful total numeric order in the usual sense.

     File:
     - lib/std/ord.cure

     Required change:
     - Either remove `Ord Float`,
       or expose a deliberate `Float.total_order` with documented NaN semantics.
