     - Do not use it for proof-relevant ordering.

506. `Std.Ord` Atom implementation uses runtime atom ordering.
     Problem:
     - Ordering atoms by BEAM term/order/name is runtime-specific and not semantic ordering.
     - It may be okay for maps/sorting, but should not imply logical order.

     File:
     - lib/std/ord.cure

     Required change:
     - Keep as runtime ordering only.
     - Separate `RuntimeOrd` from proof-relevant order.

507. `Std.Vector.append` depends on `plus(m,n)` normalization and should be an Antigen target.
     Problem:
     - This is good dependent code, but it is exactly the kind of definition that can expose bugs in:
       - index substitution,
       - dependent pattern matching,
       - recursive totality,
       - universe handling,
       - erased implicit arguments.
     - Current broader tests still carry legacy equality/token examples.

     File:
     - lib/std/vector.cure

     Required change:
     - Add focused Core/Antigen tests for `Vector.append`.
     - Ensure final Core contains no holes, primitive equality, or unsolved metas.
     - Prove/verify termination and index preservation.

508. `Std.Nat` and legacy `Nat == Int` coexist.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Nat` defines inductive `Nat`.
     - Legacy type resolver still maps `Nat` to `Int` in some paths.
     - This creates a real stdlib/checker semantic split.

     Files:
     - lib/std/nat.cure
     - lib/cure/types/type.ex
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Pick one meaning per mode.
     - Static/dependent mode: `Nat` must be the inductive type.
     - Machine naturals should get a different name.

509. `Std.Result` group is marked `:option`.
     Problem:
     - `Std.Result.__group__() -> Atom = :option`
     - This may be deliberate grouping, but it can confuse selective prelude/import grouping.

     File:
     - lib/std/result.cure

     Required change:
     - Consider a separate `:result` group or document that `:option` includes sum types.
     - Ensure group-based stdlib loading imports exactly intended modules.

510. `Std.CRDT` group is `:system` despite mostly pure-looking data APIs.
     Problem:
     - The module has process-dictionary/stateful internals for ORSet tags but is grouped as `:system`.
     - The grouping does not express effect/purity boundaries precisely.

     File:
     - lib/std/crdt.cure

     Required change:
     - Move stateful CRDT tag generation into effectful/runtime tier.
     - Keep pure CRDT merge/value operations in a pure data tier only if representation and ordering requirements are typed.

511. `Cure.Stdlib.Paths.configured_source_dir/0` reads application env without validating type.
     Problem:
     - If `:stdlib_source_dir` is set to a non-binary value, the function silently returns `nil`.
     - A bad explicit config falls back to another path.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/paths.ex

     Required change:
     - Explicit misconfiguration should be an error.
     - Only absent config should fall back.

512. `Cure.Stdlib.Paths.cure_home/0` and `cure_lib/0` treat empty env vars as absent.
     Problem:
     - Empty env vars become `nil`, allowing fallback.
     - This is fine for dev ergonomics, but strict builds should report that an explicitly provided env var is invalid/empty.

     File:
     - lib/cure/stdlib/paths.ex

     Required change:
     - Strict mode: empty override env var = configuration error.
     - Dev mode: may ignore with warning.

513. `Cure.Types.Checker` dependent-mode routing occurs after installing legacy stdlib.
     Problem:
     - Even though dependent modules are sent to `check_dependent_module/1`, the function first creates a legacy env with legacy stdlib installed.
     - This is wasted at best and confusing at worst.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Test `Cure.Elab.Program.dependent?(ast)` before building/installing legacy stdlib env.
     - Better: choose mode before any checker-specific environment setup.

514. `Cure.Types.Checker` error metadata uses generic line `0` for dependent failures.
     Problem:
     - Dependent checker failures are reported as:
       `[line: 0]`
     - LSP/MCP/CLI users cannot locate the error.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Preserve source spans from elaborator/parser.
     - If unavailable, report declaration name and AST path.

515. `Cure.Types.Checker` still treats proof containers as normal modules plus shape gate.
     Problem:
     - This is separate from `.cureproof` artifacts and `Std.Proof`.
     - The surface `proof` container itself remains accepted by the legacy checker.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Remove `:proof` container support from final/static mode.
     - Replace with ordinary erased checked definitions.

516. `Cure.Types.Checker` block mode checks only the first container.
     Problem:
     - In a block, it finds the first container and checks based on that.
     - Additional containers in the same block can be ignored by the checker path.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - File/block checking must process all top-level declarations.
     - Multiple top-level containers should be either supported explicitly or rejected.

517. `Cure.Types.Checker` `install_imports/2` imports every `use` before declaration checking.
     Problem:
     - Imports are installed globally before checking all declarations.
     - This can make import order irrelevant in the checker while codegen/runtime import order may differ.
     - It also means unused/mistyped imports can affect name resolution before their own validation is complete.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Define import scoping/order precisely.
     - Validate imports before installing them.
     - Keep checker and codegen resolution semantics identical.

518. `Cure.Types.Checker` record schemas are registered before default values are fully checked.
     Problem:
     - The record type enters the environment while default checking is still advisory/legacy.
     - If defaults are malformed but non-fatal, the record type remains available.

     File:
     - lib/cure/types/checker.ex

     Required change:
     - Validate the full record declaration first.
     - Install the record type only if all fields/defaults check.

519. `Std.Map.keys/values` and `Std.Set.to_list` depend on BEAM map iteration order.
     Problem:
     - Docs say map/set list order is `:maps.keys/1`-defined.
     - Any tests or APIs that assume deterministic order across runtimes/builds can be brittle.

     Files:
     - lib/std/map.cure
     - lib/std/set.cure

     Required change:
     - Document as unordered explicitly.
     - Provide `keys_sorted`/`to_sorted_list` requiring `Ord(K)` where deterministic order is needed.

520. `Std.Json` object encoding order depends on input list order, but decoded objects from maps may have arbitrary order.
     Problem:
     - If object values ever flow through maps or CRDT-like structures, encoded JSON can be nondeterministic.
     - This matters for hashing/signing/package metadata if reused.

     Files:
     - lib/std/json.cure
     - lib/cure/stdlib/cure_std_json.ex
     - lib/cure/project/json.ex

     Required change:
     - Package/signing JSON must use deterministic key ordering.
     - Runtime JSON may preserve list order, but must be documented.
FOCUSED PASS: KERNEL / NORMALISER / ELABORATOR / DEPENDENT TYPE MACHINERY

Reference baseline used for this pass:
- Lean:
  - Core expressions are ordinary `Expr` forms:
    `bvar`, `fvar`, `mvar`, `sort`, `const`, `app`, `lam`, `forallE`, `letE`, `lit`, `mdata`, `proj`.
  - Constants carry universe arguments.
  - Primitive operations are mostly constants/applications with trusted reduction/folding keyed by names.
  - Equality is ordinary inductive/proposition machinery, not a primitive rewrite Core node.
  - Metavariables are explicit, typed, contextual, and solved by the elaborator/meta layer.
- Agda:
  - Primitives are typed `PrimitiveImpl = PrimImpl Type PrimFun`.
  - Primitive implementations carry a name, arity, argument occurrences, and reducer.
  - Conversion/reduction/metas/levels live in dedicated type-checking machinery.
  - Pattern unification and coverage are sophisticated elaborator/checker subsystems, not arbitrary final-Core syntax extensions.
- Idris 2:
  - Core terms have explicit `Meta`, `Bind`, `App`, `PrimVal`, `Erased`, `TType`, etc.
  - Primitive values/constants are represented distinctly from ordinary applications.
  - Holes/metas and erasure are first-class tracked entities.
  - Normalisation/conversion/unification are integrated around a typed core and checked context.

521. Cure still has a primitive propositional equality layer that none of the references use as final Core.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex
       - `infer({:eq, ty, a, b})`
       - `infer({:refl, a})`
       - `infer({:rewrite, proof, motive, body})`
       - `check({:refl, a}, {:veq, ...})`
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex
       - evaluates `{:eq}` to `:veq`
       - evaluates `{:refl}` to `:vrefl`
       - erases `{:rewrite}` by evaluating only the body
     - lib/cure/core/quote.ex
       - reifies `:veq` back to `{:eq}`
       - reifies `:vrefl` back to `{:refl}`
     - lib/cure/core/conv.ex / normalise.ex / value.ex also know `:veq` and `:vrefl`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean represents equality as applications of the constant `Eq` and constructor/recursor machinery.
     - Agda/Idris similarly treat equality as an inductive family / built-in typed primitive, not as a separate `eq/refl/rewrite` AST family in the kernel.
     - They may have rewrite tactics/elaborator features, but not a final-Core primitive rewrite node like Cure’s `{:rewrite, ...}`.

     Fix:
     - Remove `{:eq}`, `{:refl}`, `{:rewrite}`, `:veq`, and `:vrefl` from final Core.
     - Keep only canonical builtin-inductive `Eq`:
       `{:data, :Eq, [ty], [lhs, rhs]}`
       and canonical constructor:
       `{:ctor, :refl, [value]}`
     - Rebuild rewrite as ordinary Eq eliminator/transport.

522. Cure’s surface rewrite elaborator emits primitive rewrite nodes instead of Eq eliminator applications.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
       - `rewrite_plan/6` returns builders that emit `{:rewrite, ...}`.
       - `symmetry_proof/4` emits `{:rewrite, proof, motive, mk_refl(a)}`.
       - `bridge_step/7` builds nested primitive rewrite terms.
       - The comments explicitly say the internal transport machinery still emits `{:rewrite}`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean’s rewriting elaborates through equality recursors, congruence, simplification, and proof terms.
     - Agda’s rewrite/with mechanisms elaborate through equality and pattern/refinement machinery, not a new primitive Core rewrite node.
     - Idris elaborates rewrite through equality eliminators in TT, not an untyped transport special form.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A bespoke final/Core-ish `{:rewrite, proof, motive, body}` eliminator that is neither a normal application nor an inductive recursor.
     - A rewrite planner that generates internal primitive proof nodes and then relies on the kernel’s special `infer({:rewrite, ...})`.

     Fix:
     - Introduce canonical `Eq.rec` / `Eq.ndrec` / `transport` as typed builtin globals or ordinary recursor encodings.
     - Lower `rewrite p in body` to applications of that eliminator.
     - Delete primitive rewrite from kernel, eval, quote, normalise, substitution, unifier, and elaborator.

523. Cure’s rewrite “bridge step” is an ad hoc equality proof generator not present in the reference designs.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex
       - `find_bridge/3` searches reducible subterms syntactically.
       - `bridge_step/7` fabricates an inline bridge proof:
         `bridge_proof = {:rewrite, {:refl, s_nf}, const_motive, mk_refl(s_nf)}`
       - This is explicitly a workaround for the normalizer preserving stuck cases.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris do not add a special “bridge proof by primitive rewrite around primitive refl” mechanism in final terms.
     - They rely on definitional equality, canonical recursors, congruence/simplification, and properly typed proof terms.
     - If conversion cannot see an equality, a proof/tactic/elaborator obligation is produced, not a kernel-only primitive rewrite bridge.

     Why this is dangerous:
     - It couples rewrite elaboration to normalizer quirks.
     - It uses the very primitive equality/rewrite representation we want to remove.
     - It is explicitly incomplete: comments admit it handles one reducible-inner-occurrence pattern, not general up-to-conversion occurrence matching.

     Fix:
     - Delete bridge-step primitive rewrite construction.
     - Implement occurrence abstraction using a typed conversion-aware abstraction pass or require explicit user lemma/proof.
     - Use canonical transport only.

524. Cure’s kernel accepts holes as terms.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `def check(_ctx, {:hole, _name}, _expected), do: :ok`
     - lib/cure/elab/declarations.ex registers pending definitions as:
       `{:hole, "__pending__"}`
     - Hole rejection is deferred to codegen/emit paths.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have holes/metas in elaboration, but unsolved holes do not become accepted final kernel terms.
     - Idris has explicit `Meta`/`Erased`/hole machinery and final unresolved metas are not valid executable checked definitions.
     - Lean mvars are meta-level; kernel final declarations do not accept arbitrary unsolved mvars as proof of any type.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - Treating a source/elaboration hole as a kernel-checkable term at every expected type.
     - Reusing hole terms as pending-definition bodies.

     Fix:
     - Holes belong only to the elaborator/metavariable layer.
     - Final kernel `check/3` must reject `{:hole, _}`.
     - Pending definitions need a non-Core pending marker.
     - Final-Core readiness must reject all ordinary unsolved holes before erasure/codegen.

525. Cure’s universe machinery falls far short of Lean/Agda/Idris.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/universe.ex:
       `@ceiling 2`
       `Type 0`, `Type 1`, `Type 2`
     - lib/cure/core/value.ex rejects universe values above the fixed ceiling.
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex hardcodes:
       `Type` as `{:type, 0}` with type `{:vtype, 1}`.
     - Globals do not carry universe parameters.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean has `Sort u`, `Type u`, level parameters, level metavariables, and constants with level arguments.
     - Agda has level constraints and level solving.
     - Idris has universe levels and meta-level checking machinery.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A fixed low universe ceiling as the trusted universe policy.
     - No level expressions, no level variables, no level metas, no universe-polymorphic globals.

     Fix:
     - Replace integer-only levels with:
       `zero`, `succ(level)`, `max(level, level)`, `param(name)`, `meta(id)`.
     - Add level constraints and cumulativity solving.
     - Change globals to carry level arguments.
     - Make builtin Eq universe-polymorphic:
       `Eq.{u} : (A : Type u) -> A -> A -> Type u`.

526. Cure globals are bare atoms, unlike Lean constants or Idris/Agda qualified names.
     Cure:
     - Core term:
       `{:global, name}`
     - Quote:
       `{:nglobal, name}` reifies to `{:global, name}`
     - Env maps definitions by bare atom.
     - Elaborator frequently uses `String.to_atom(name)`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean constants are `Expr.const name universe_args`.
     - Idris names are structured/qualified.
     - Agda qualified names carry module/scope identity.
     - Reference systems do not use bare host-language atoms as final global identity.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - Host atom = semantic global identity.
     - No universe args on globals.
     - No stable symbol table / namespace identity in Core.

     Fix:
     - Replace bare atom globals with qualified symbol IDs plus level arguments:
       `{:global, qname, level_args}`
     - Use module-qualified constructor/family/global identities.
     - Intern only via compiler symbol table; do not create BEAM atoms during parsing/elaboration.

527. Cure primitive operations are a separate Core node, unlike Lean/Agda’s typed primitive constants.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `infer(ctx, {:prim, op, args})`
       `infer_prim/3`
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `eval({:prim, op, args}, env)`
       folds arithmetic/comparison directly.
     - lib/cure/core/quote.ex:
       reifies neutral primitives as `{:prim, op, args}`.
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex and conv handle `:nprim`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean primitive arithmetic is represented as constants/applications with trusted reductions keyed by declaration names.
     - Agda primitives are named implementations with explicit types.
     - Idris has primitive constants/literals, but primitive behavior is integrated into typed Core primitives, not an open `op` atom + arg list special form.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - `{:prim, op, args}` as an untyped/open-ended operation language inside Core.
     - Kernel typing by Elixir clause rather than primitive declarations with checked types.

     Fix:
     - Keep primitive literals/types if desired:
       `{:int_type}`, `{:int_lit, n}`, `{:float_type}`, `{:float_lit, f}`.
     - Replace primitive ops with typed primitive globals:
       `Int.add : Int -> Int -> Int`
       represented as ordinary global application.
     - Primitive registry should store:
       type, arity, purity, totality, reducer, lowering rule.

528. Cure folds partial arithmetic into definitional equality policy too loosely.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       - `div` by zero becomes `:stuck`.
       - `rem` by zero becomes `:stuck`.
       - float operations fold in Core evaluation.
       - float equality/comparison folds to Bool constructors.
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex types division as ordinary numeric op.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda marks primitive functions through typed primitive declarations and is careful about partiality; some partial integer div/mod examples are explicitly commented as partial in the reference snapshot.
     - Lean’s Nat arithmetic is total and definitional; machine/float behavior is not treated as arbitrary proof-friendly definitional equality in the same way.
     - Idris distinguishes primitive values/types and has normalisation policies around primitives.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A generic numeric primitive `:div` whose zero-divisor case silently becomes a stuck neutral rather than a typed partial/error/result/precondition.
     - Float computation inside Core definitional equality without a stated proof-theoretic policy.

     Fix:
     - Classify primitives:
       `:logic_total`, `:runtime_total`, `:partial`, `:effectful`, `:unsafe`.
     - Only `:logic_total` primitives reduce during typechecking.
     - Division/remainder need `NonZeroInt`, proof argument, or `Option/Result`.
     - Float ops should be runtime-only unless Cure deliberately specifies exact semantics.

529. Cure’s normalizer intentionally freezes stuck case expansion; this is a bespoke workaround rather than a standard reference strategy.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex:
       - `unfold_certified_head/3` unfolds certified globals.
       - If unfolding exposes a stuck eliminator, it keeps the global folded.
       - Comments explicitly justify freezing to avoid non-canonical normal forms and elaborator occurrence-matching problems.
     - This directly motivates the bridge rewrite heuristic.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris do use transparency/reducibility/fuel/WHNF controls, but they do not make rewrite planning depend on a bespoke “preserve stuck cases so global calls remain folded” invariant.
     - Lean’s definitional equality works over expressions with whnf/transparency modes; normal forms are not chosen to suit a later syntactic rewrite occurrence search.
     - Agda/Idris reduction/conversion integrate stuck eliminators with their normal forms and unification machinery.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A normalizer policy chosen partly to make elaborator syntactic occurrence matching work.
     - A custom `stuck_cases: :preserve` mode with no alternative and no reference-equivalent final invariant.

     Fix:
     - Separate:
       - kernel conversion/WHNF policy,
       - display normalisation,
       - elaborator occurrence search.
     - Do not let rewrite elaboration depend on preserved stuck-case shape.
     - Implement conversion-aware abstraction or typed proof obligations instead.

530. Cure normalizer fuel is process-dictionary state.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex:
       - `with_fuel/2` uses `Process.put(@fuel_key, fuel)`.
       - `spend_fuel/1` reads/writes that process key.
       - Existing value is deleted, not restored.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris normalisation/checking state is threaded through monads/contexts/options, not hidden mutable process dictionary state.
     - Fuel, reduction depth, transparency, and interruptibility are explicit checker state.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - Hidden global-per-process mutable fuel controlling trusted normalisation.

     Fix:
     - Thread fuel explicitly through normalizer state.
     - At minimum restore previous process-dictionary fuel in `after`.
     - Make fuel exhaustion a structured result, not a thrown process-local exception.

531. Cure’s quoted data value representation is lossy unless a signature is supplied.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/quote.ex:
       - `{:vdata, name, args}` flattens params ++ indices.
       - Without signature, read-back puts all args in params and no indices.
       - Comments document that callers needing the split must pass `sig`.
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex has special value-recursive code to avoid false bad-motive due to this collapse.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean inductive applications are ordinary constant applications; parameter/index structure is recovered from declaration metadata, but expression representation is not ambiguously flattened in a way that changes arity.
     - Agda/Idris store enough internal term/declaration information that quoting/read-back is not dependent on accidentally passing an optional signature at every sensitive use.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A read-back function whose output can be structurally wrong for indexed family terms unless the caller remembers to pass `sig`.
     - Kernel helper workarounds specifically because quote loses parameter/index split.

     Fix:
     - Store data applications in Core/value with explicit param/index split:
       `{:vdata, name, params, indices}`
     - Or make signature mandatory for all value read-back.
     - Remove flat read-back from trusted paths.

532. Cure’s kernel performs first-order index unification and skips branch checking for “impossible” branches.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `check_case_branches/7`
       - calls `unify_indices/4`.
       - when result is `:impossible`, it does:
         `{:cont, :ok}` and does not check the branch body.
     - The unifier is hand-written first-order rigid/index unification.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean represents dependent elimination through recursors/case compilation where impossible branches are handled through elaboration/recursor construction, not by a kernel ad hoc branch-body skip over arbitrary Core branches.
     - Agda has a sophisticated coverage/unification checker for clauses and absurd patterns.
     - Idris has coverage/case-tree construction and impossible-case handling as part of elaboration/checking machinery.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - Kernel-level branch skipping based on a bespoke first-order index unifier over the raw Core branch list.
     - A public `branch_unify/4` query in the kernel for the elaborator.

     Why this is risky:
     - If `unify_indices/4` incorrectly returns `:impossible`, an ill-typed branch body is accepted.
     - The correctness of the trusted kernel now depends on this custom unifier being sound.

     Fix:
     - Prefer representing impossible branches explicitly as an absurd eliminator whose type is checked.
     - Or move impossible-branch pruning to elaboration and require kernel case terms to be recursor-valid.
     - If kernel keeps it, the branch unifier needs a precise formal invariant and Antigen assays proving no false `:impossible`.

533. Cure’s index unifier silently ignores arity mismatch in some internal paths.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `unify_indices/4` zips `result_indices` and `scrut_indices`.
       - `unify_spine(_, _, _arity, subst), do: {:ok, subst}` for mismatched sub-spines.
     - The caller usually has separate arity checks for declared index count, but the inner unifier can still silently accept mismatched nested spines.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Lean/Idris unification treats arity/spine mismatch as a real unification failure or a stuck constraint, not success.
     - A mismatch is not a solved substitution.

     Fix:
     - Any spine length mismatch inside index unification should be `:undecided` or `:impossible` depending on rigid head context, never `{:ok, subst}`.
     - Add tests where nested constructor/data index spines have mismatched arity.

534. Cure’s index unifier ignores definitional equality except through syntactic/reified forms in limited places.
     Cure:
     - Kernel branch unifier works mostly syntactically on reified values.
     - It has rigid-head and occurs checks, but no full conversion-aware constraint solving for open terms.
     - Elab unifier has `delta_convertible?/4`, but only for closed, meta-free terms and only depth 0.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean’s `isDefEq` and Agda/Idris unification are conversion-aware under contexts and binders.
     - They maintain constraints/metas rather than simply dropping `:undecided` pairs.

     What Cure has done:
     - A hybrid: some closed conversion checks, some syntactic first-order unification, and many conservative “ignore undecided” paths.

     Fix:
     - Introduce a proper constraint queue:
       - solved,
       - stuck,
       - impossible,
       - deferred.
     - Do not erase undecided constraints if they are required for branch type correctness.
     - Re-run constraints after metas/globals reduce.

535. Cure’s elaborator metavariables are not contextual enough.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       - metavariable is just `{:meta, id}`.
       - MetaCtx stores `types: %{id => type_term}` and `solutions`.
       - No local context is stored with the meta.
       - Miller solving tries to reconstruct abstraction from a type term and binder depth.
     - `whnf_pre/4` is disabled under binders (`depth != 0`) due shifting complexity.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean mvars carry local context and type.
     - Idris metas are applied to their environment/spine.
     - Agda metavariables are contextual constraints with rich occurs/escaping checks.
     - They do not use a bare global id plus ad hoc depth strengthening as the main representation.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - Ambient-frame metas with no stored local context.
     - A partial Miller-pattern solver that only reduces at depth 0.

     Fix:
     - Store each meta with:
       - local context,
       - type,
       - universe context,
       - allowed dependencies,
       - source span/kind.
     - Represent meta occurrences as applied metas over explicit spines.
     - Replace depth hacks with context-aware strengthening/zonking.

536. Cure’s Miller-pattern solver can generate solutions without checking them immediately.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       - `miller_solve/5` builds `wrap_lams(doms, body)` and puts it as solution.
       - It relies on the final kernel re-check to catch wrong solves.
       - `peel_pi_domains/2` peels syntactic Pi domains without normalizing or checking binder dependencies.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris unification checks meta assignments against meta type/context constraints.
     - They maintain well-typed substitutions as an invariant, not only “kernel will catch it later.”

     Fix:
     - After solving a meta, check the solution against the meta’s recorded type in its recorded context.
     - Normalize the meta type before peeling Pi domains.
     - Reject or defer if domain extraction is not justified.

537. Cure final-Core meta rejection is not centralized.
     Cure:
     - MetaCtx docs say unsolved metas must not reach kernel.
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex has `zonk/2`, `meta_free?/1`.
     - Kernel itself has no explicit `{:meta, _}` rejection path.
     - Eval has no `{:meta, _}` clause, so a leaked meta crashes rather than cleanly rejects.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have explicit unsolved meta/hole reporting and do not let raw metas hit kernel evaluation as unknown tuples.

     Fix:
     - Add final-Core invariant checker:
       - no metas,
       - no holes,
       - no unresolved level metas,
       - no primitive equality,
       - no primitive op nodes once removed.
     - Kernel/eval should return structured `{:error, {:meta_in_core, id}}` or reject before evaluation.

538. Cure’s elaborator has multiple fallback elaboration strategies that are not reference-style constraint solving.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       - tries inference path.
       - if constructor/implicit application inference fails, retries bidirectional variants.
       - comments repeatedly say original errors are surfaced only if fallback fails.
     - This is a sequence of hand-written retries rather than a principled constraint/elaboration engine.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris elaboration is constraint-driven:
       - elaborate term,
       - create metavariables,
       - collect constraints,
       - synthesize implicits,
       - solve or report unsolved obligations.
     - They do not rely primarily on “try this path, if it fails try a special bidirectional constructor path.”

     What Cure has done:
     - Bespoke retry ladders for nested constructors, implicit apps, lambda args, bidirectional app, etc.

     Fix:
     - Move to a single bidirectional elaboration algorithm with:
       - expected type propagation,
       - metavariable insertion,
       - constraint collection,
       - deterministic solve/final zonk.
     - Fallback retries should become specific rules in that system, not independent alternate paths.

539. Cure dependent match motives are still a limited hand-built subset.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       - `elaborate_match/6` comments say result varying with matched indices was a follow-up, then later code manually generalizes indices and scrutinee terms.
       - `detect_carried_index/7` handles exactly one non-invertible computed index.
       - It requires closed index type.
       - It transports siblings using Eq-arrow/rewrite machinery.
     - The implementation has special cases for tuple scrutinees, nested arms, guards, carried index siblings, rematch arms, etc.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean match elaboration abstracts discriminants/results systematically (`kabstract`, match alternatives, recursor/casesOn).
     - Agda/Idris coverage/matching compilers manage pattern matrices, inaccessible patterns, with-abstraction, and index refinement as core elaboration features.
     - They do not rely on one-computed-index detection plus Eq-arrow transport as the main dependent match model.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - `detect_carried_index` restricted to one computed index and siblings.
     - Eq-arrow sibling transport built through primitive rewrite.
     - Sentinel-variable replacement to simulate abstraction of computed index occurrences.

     Fix:
     - Implement a real dependent match elaborator:
       - abstract all discriminants with a principled abstraction operation,
       - build explicit motives,
       - elaborate branches against recursor/motive expectations,
       - carry equalities systematically when needed,
       - produce canonical Eq eliminator applications, not primitive rewrite.
     - Keep the current subset as experimental until it is rechecked through canonical eliminators.

540. Cure’s dependent match uses sentinel de Bruijn variables to abstract computed terms.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       - `build_motive/6` chooses `sentinel_base = 1 + max_free_ref(...)`.
       - Replaces computed index/scrutinee occurrences with sentinel vars.
       - Later `generalize/4` maps sentinels to motive binders.
     - This is explicitly compared in comments to Lean’s `kabstract`, but implemented by ad hoc sentinel indices.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean has explicit expression abstraction machinery over locally nameless expressions.
     - Agda/Idris do not fake binders by inventing de Bruijn indices above max free index in raw terms.
     - They use abstraction/substitution operations with context-aware invariants.

     Why this is risky:
     - Sentinels are indistinguishable from real variables after replacement except by side map.
     - Any missed traversal shape or binder-depth bug can capture or misbind.
     - Adding new Core forms requires updating every abstraction/generalization function.

     Fix:
     - Implement a real `abstract(term, target)` primitive returning a binder-safe abstraction.
     - Use a central traversal library for binder-aware operations.
     - Antigen-test every Core form for abstraction/substitution round trips.

541. Cure has too many duplicated binder-aware traversals.
     Cure:
     - Binder-aware traversals exist separately in:
       - Term.shift/subst
       - Kernel.replace_branch_vars
       - Elaborator.abstract_term
       - Elaborator.replace_term
       - Elaborator.generalize
       - Elaborator.free_indices
       - Elab.Subst
       - Elab.Unify.mabs/escapes
       - Relevance.walk
       - Erase.has_hole
       - Resolution.rekey_term
       - TotalityClosure.collect
     - Many still mention retired primitive equality forms.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have central term traversal/substitution infrastructure.
     - Adding a term constructor updates shared operations or compiler-enforced pattern matches, not a dozen bespoke walkers.

     What Cure has done:
     - A fragile “parallel visitors” architecture.

     Fix:
     - Create one Core fold/traverse/rename/subst framework with binder information.
     - Define term constructors once with traversal behavior.
     - Make missing case handling fail tests.
     - Remove duplicate ad hoc traversals.

542. Cure’s value-level type classification is ad hoc.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `infer_type_value_sort/2` classifies values as types.
       - Special cases:
         `:vtype`, `:vneutral`, `:vint_type`, `:vfloat_type`, `:vdata`, `:vpi`, `:vsigma`, `:veq`.
       - It sometimes reifies endpoints and rechecks them.
     - This exists partly because quote/read-back loses param/index split.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris infer types from terms under contexts and use whnf/conversion rather than a separate value-classifier patchwork.
     - If values represent types, their classification is part of the normal typed evaluation/checking discipline.

     Fix:
     - Remove the need for value-level type classification by:
       - preserving param/index split,
       - checking motives from terms/recursor types,
       - using ordinary kernel inference on well-reified terms.
     - If value classification remains, make it total over all type-value forms and backed by signature-aware read-back.

543. Cure’s `check_coverage` only checks constructor-name set inclusion.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - declared constructors set must be subset of covered constructors.
       - It does not reject duplicate branches.
       - It does not reject extra covered names until branch checking reaches them.
       - It does not account for indexed impossible coverage except later branch unification.
     - Branch order/duplicates are not treated as a first-class coverage result.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris coverage checkers track clauses, redundancy, absurdity, reachability, and missing cases.
     - Lean match compilation builds alternatives through recursor/casesOn; duplicated/invalid alternatives are elaborator errors.

     Fix:
     - Reject duplicate branches.
     - Coverage should return structured:
       - missing constructors,
       - duplicate constructors,
       - impossible constructors,
       - unreachable branches.
     - Keep branch typing separate from coverage.

544. Cure’s constructor/family representation flattens parameters and indices in values.
     Cure:
     - Eval:
       `{:data, name, params, indices}` becomes `{:vdata, name, params ++ indices}`.
     - Quote optionally splits by signature.
     - Kernel code frequently splits by `Inductive.param_count`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris distinguish parameters/indices in declaration metadata and applications enough that elaboration/reduction does not require guessing split from a flat list.
     - Lean constants/apps are uniform, but inductive metadata drives recursor generation without flattening ambiguity.

     Fix:
     - Use explicit value representation:
       `{:vdata, name, params, indices}`
     - Constructors should carry explicit implicit params separately if needed.
     - Remove split/re-split logic from quote/kernel/elaborator.

545. Cure constructors with parameters cannot infer; they require checking mode.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `infer({:ctor, name, args})` rejects parameter-bearing constructors:
         `{:ctor_requires_checking_mode, family_name}`
       - Elaborator then has special inference/bidirectional retries to infer parameters from args.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris handle implicit parameters with metavariables and constraints uniformly.
     - Constructors can be elaborated with implicit arguments inserted and solved; the core expression is ordinary application to a constructor constant.

     Fix:
     - Represent constructors as typed globals/constants with implicit parameters.
     - Elaborator inserts metas for implicit params and solves them.
     - Kernel inference should infer constructor constants/applications normally once all explicit/implicit args are present.

546. Cure’s primitive Bool handling is split between hardcoded constructor atoms and builtin registry.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `bool_type_value/1` consults builtin registry for family id.
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `vbool(true) -> {:vctor, :True, []}`
       `vbool(false) -> {:vctor, :False, []}`
       hardcoded constructor atoms.
     - If Bool constructors are namespaced/qualified later, evaluator must change.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris use declared constructor constants/qualified names.
     - Bool constructors are not just ad hoc host atoms in the evaluator.

     Fix:
     - Bool primitive folds should use builtin registry constructor IDs, or primitive reducer should be signature-aware.
     - Better: primitive comparison returns application of canonical Bool constructors by stable symbol ID.

547. Cure’s normalizer/quote path can produce terms the kernel no longer wants.
     Cure:
     - Quote can reify `:veq` and `:vrefl` to retired primitive terms.
     - Normalise keeps `:veq`, `:vrefl`, `:nprim`.
     - This means normalization can output non-final constructs.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems’ read-back produces their own valid internal term language.
     - They do not normalize into a language that the final kernel policy later says should not exist.

     Fix:
     - First define final Core syntax.
     - Ensure Eval/Normalise/Quote are closed over exactly that syntax.
     - Normalizing a valid final Core term must produce a valid final Core term.

548. Cure’s legacy dependent type system is not comparable to Agda/Lean/Idris dependent types.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/dependent.ex:
       `{:dependent, name, type_params, value_params, constraints}`
       compatibility:
       `{[], _} -> true`
       `{_, []} -> true`
       otherwise structural equality of constraints.
       VCs generated with `Enum.zip(vps,args)`.
       substitution changes only constraints, not value params.
     - This is parallel to Core and SMT-backed.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Lean/Idris dependent types are not an external tuple with value params and constraints checked by approximate SMT compatibility.
     - Indices live in the core language; equality/conversion/unification operates over terms.

     What Cure has done that does not appear in the references:
     - A second “dependent type” representation outside the real Core.
     - Compatibility rules where absence of constraints on either side makes types compatible both ways.
     - SMT/refinement logic separate from definitional equality.

     Fix:
     - Remove `Cure.Types.Dependent` from trusted checking.
     - Lower all dependent types to Core Pi/Sigma/indexed data/refinement obligations.
     - SMT may solve obligations only by producing checked/trusted evidence.

549. Cure’s legacy Pi/Sigma machinery is a second dependent calculus.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/pi.ex
     - lib/cure/types/sigma.ex
     - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex
     - Pi/Sigma resolve through legacy `Type.resolve`, surface AST substitution, and `Reduce.normalize`.
     - Sigma returns `:any` for missing second component AST.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have one core dependent function/pair calculus.
     - Pi/Sigma are core binders or inductives, not parallel legacy modules with separate subtyping.

     What Cure has done:
     - Pi/Sigma exist both in Core and in legacy type modules.

     Fix:
     - Delete/quarantine legacy Pi/Sigma from static/final mode.
     - Parse Pi/Sigma syntax directly into Core binders.
     - Use Core conversion/substitution only.

550. Cure.Types.Reduce is a parallel normalizer.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex:
       - substitutes surface AST variables,
       - tries CoreBridge,
       - folds boolean connectives itself because Bool primitives were retired,
       - structural fallback if CoreBridge fails.
     - It compares normalized surface AST equality directly.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris do not have a trusted second normalizer over surface syntax for dependent types.
     - Definitional equality is defined by the core conversion engine.

     What Cure has done:
     - A “kernel-backed-ish” surface reducer that falls back to structural congruence and hand-folds booleans.

     Fix:
     - Do not use `Cure.Types.Reduce` as trusted definitional equality.
     - Trusted normalization must be only Core Normalise/Conv.
     - Surface reducers may be editor/advisory only.

551. Cure.Types.CoreBridge maps surface variables to globals.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex:
       `{:variable, ..., name}` becomes `{:global, String.to_atom(name)}`.
     - That is wrong for local value parameters, binders, and dependent indices.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris distinguish local bound variables, free variables, globals, constructors, and metas.
     - They never translate every unresolved variable into a global constant.

     Fix:
     - CoreBridge must carry a context mapping source variables to de Bruijn indices.
     - Unknown names must be errors or unresolved constraints, not globals.
     - Better: remove CoreBridge from trusted checking.

552. Cure’s elaborator hardcodes `"Eq"` and `"refl"` name behavior.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       - `name == "refl"`
       - `mk_eq` uses `:Eq`
       - `eq_parts({:vdata, :Eq, ...})`
     - lib/cure/elab/program.ex detects dependent modules by `Keyword.get(meta,:name) in ["Eq","refl"]`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris use resolved names/builtin mappings, not raw strings in elaboration/control-flow.
     - Builtin identity type may have special status, but through environment/builtin declarations, not string equality throughout.

     Fix:
     - Resolve `Eq` and `refl` through builtin registry symbols.
     - Do not use raw string names for semantic decisions.
     - Dependent-mode selection must not sniff raw strings.

553. Cure’s dependent pipeline is selected by heuristic AST sniffing.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/program.ex:
       `dependent?/1` checks for indexed_type, sigma_type, rewrite_expr, `Eq`, `refl`, tuple projections, implicit params, etc.
     - lib/cure/compiler.ex / checker path route modules based on this predicate.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris do not choose between “real dependent checker” and “legacy checker” by AST heuristic.
     - They have one core typing pipeline.

     What Cure has done:
     - A trust boundary controlled by a best-effort syntactic recognizer.

     Fix:
     - Static/dependent mode must be explicit.
     - All static code should route through one final-Core readiness gate.
     - Legacy checker should be dynamic/advisory only.

554. Cure’s elaborator uses host `System.get_env` for tracing inside elaboration.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       `CURE_REWRITE_LOG`
       `IO.puts(:stderr, ...)`
     - This is not type-soundness critical, but it is a side-effect inside elaboration.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference checkers have logging/tracing frameworks, but they do not wire arbitrary env reads/IO directly into proof-term planning logic.
     - Debug logging is controlled by checker options/monads.

     Fix:
     - Pass tracing/debug config explicitly through elaborator options.
     - Keep elaboration function pure with respect to source/checking result.

555. Cure’s kernel `check_def/2` can type-check definitions before final-readiness validation.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `check_def/2` checks declared type and body.
       It does not reject:
       - holes,
       - primitive equality,
       - metas,
       - non-final primitive ops,
       - pending bodies,
       - unresolved universe constraints.
     - Some of these crash later or are accepted by special rules.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have clear phases:
       elaborated declaration -> no unsolved holes/metas -> kernel/environment add.
     - Final environment additions require fully elaborated terms.

     Fix:
     - Add `Core.Final.validate_def/2`.
     - `check_def/2` should call it before evaluation/checking or as part of validation.
     - Certification and serialization must require final readiness.

556. Cure’s `Env.certify/2` and certificate pipeline are too weak for Lean/Agda/Idris-style trusted reduction.
     Cure:
     - Certification marks definitions reducible for δ.
     - Normalizer trusts `Env.certified?/2` to unfold.
     - Earlier audit found certification is public and mostly checks closedness if called directly.
     - Certificate.terminating? is a size-change approximation over Core terms.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean unfolds definitions according to trusted environment declarations/reducibility and termination checking for recursive definitions.
     - Agda/Idris termination/coverage checking is deeply integrated with definitions before they become trusted normalizing definitions.
     - They do not have a public “mark certified” helper equivalent that can bypass the checker.

     Fix:
     - Certification should be an opaque token produced only by kernel validation.
     - δ-reducible definitions must be:
       typed,
       total,
       closed,
       meta-free,
       hole-free,
       final-Core clean,
       effect-free.
     - Add certificate hash over the body/type.

557. Cure’s normalizer reifies WHNF/NF without signature-aware quote in public paths.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex:
       - `whnf/3` and `nf/3` call `Quote.reify(Context.length(ctx))`, without passing signature.
     - Quote defaults to flat data read-back without `sig`.
     - Kernel callers sometimes work around by passing `sig` manually elsewhere.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference read-back for internal terms preserves enough structure to be useful across checker operations.
     - They do not have public normal forms that silently lose index/parameter split.

     Fix:
     - Normalise should pass `Context.signature(ctx)` to Quote.reify.
     - Or value representation should preserve param/index split so this is unnecessary.
     - Add tests normalizing indexed family types and then re-checking them.

558. Cure’s conversion has η for functions but no type-directed η discipline for records/Σ beyond pairs.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/conv.ex:
       - η compares lambdas against neutral functions by applying a fresh neutral.
       - Σ pairs compare structurally only when both are `:vpair`.
       - projections reduce only when actual pair is known.
     - No record/projection η policy except maybe pairs.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean has projection/structure η rules for certain structures.
     - Agda/Idris have definitional equality rules chosen carefully per record/eta settings.
     - Cure’s Σ/record eta story is incomplete.

     Fix:
     - Decide:
       - no η for Σ/records, or
       - η for non-recursive records/Σ projections.
     - Make this explicit and test conversion behavior.
     - Do not accidentally rely on η that conversion does not implement.

559. Cure’s erased/relevance discipline is not integrated into kernel typing.
     Cure:
     - Erasure/relevance is a separate elaborator pass:
       lib/cure/elab/relevance.ex
       lib/cure/elab/erase.ex
     - Kernel Pi types do not carry multiplicity/quantity.
     - Env defs carry separate `quantities`.
     - Relevance checker silently accepts malformed/missing quantities in some cases from earlier audit.

     Reference contrast:
     - Idris has quantity/erasure in core binders.
     - Lean has proof irrelevance/Prop and irreducible proof erasure at extraction/codegen, not a separate quantity list detached from binder structure.
     - Agda has relevance/quantity annotations integrated into syntax/checking.

     What Cure has done:
     - Binder relevance stored outside Core term structure as a parallel vector.

     Fix:
     - Put quantity/relevance on binders:
       `{:pi, quantity, dom, cod}`
       `{:lam, quantity, dom, body}`
     - Kernel should check erased arguments according to binder relevance.
     - Erasure should consume Core binder annotations, not external vectors.

560. Cure’s final dependent story currently has three competing equalities.
     Cure:
     - Primitive propositional equality:
       `{:eq}`, `{:refl}`, `{:rewrite}`, `:veq`, `:vrefl`
     - Canonical builtin-inductive Eq:
       `{:data, :Eq, ...}`, `{:ctor, :refl, ...}`
     - Legacy token equality:
       `Cure.Types.Equality`, `:cure_refl`
     - Plus runtime Eq protocol in stdlib.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris may distinguish propositional equality from boolean equality/typeclass equality, but they do not let multiple trusted propositional-equality encodings coexist.
     - Runtime boolean equality is separate from proof equality.

     Fix:
     - Trusted propositional equality: one canonical builtin-inductive Eq.
     - Runtime equality protocol: rename or namespace clearly.
     - Remove primitive equality and token equality.

561. Cure’s final dependent story has two normalizers and two unifiers.
     Cure:
     - Core:
       - Normalise/Eval/Conv
       - Elab.Unify
       - Kernel branch unifier
     - Legacy:
       - Types.Reduce
       - Types.Unify
       - Types.Dependent compatibility
       - SMT/refinement paths

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris may have many algorithms, but the trusted definitional equality and unification are unified around one internal core.
     - They do not have a separate legacy dependent checker with different semantics for the same language constructs.

     Fix:
     - Trusted path:
       - one Core normalizer,
       - one conversion checker,
       - one elaborator unifier/constraint solver,
       - one final-Core validator.
     - Legacy paths must be advisory/dynamic only.

562. Cure uses “kernel re-check catches it” as a substitute for maintaining elaborator invariants.
     Cure examples:
     - Elab.Unify comments say wrong Miller solves are caught downstream.
     - Rewrite bridge comments say kernel checks conversions after synthetic rewrites.
     - Constructor implicit inference uses retries and then kernel check catches issues.
     - Carried sibling transport says ill-typed transports are caught by kernel re-check.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris also re-check/validate, but their elaborators maintain typed constraints and contexts as invariants.
     - They do not generally build arbitrary possibly-ill-typed Core and rely on a later catch as the ordinary control flow.

     Fix:
     - Keep kernel re-check as defense-in-depth.
     - But elaborator must maintain:
       - typed metas,
       - checked substitutions,
       - solved constraints,
       - source-spanned diagnostics.
     - Add an elaborator invariant check before kernel handoff.

563. Cure has no real level-metavariable/universe-constraint integration in unification.
     Cure:
     - Metas are term metas only.
     - `Universe.le?`, `succ`, `max` work on integers.
     - `subtype?({:vtype,l1},{:vtype,l2})` checks integer ordering.
     - No level constraints are generated/solved by the elaborator.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean constants and sorts carry `Level`; unification collects level constraints.
     - Agda has level constraints/solver.
     - Idris tracks universe levels in core.

     Fix:
     - Add level metavariables and constraints.
     - Unification must handle both term constraints and level constraints.
     - Finalization rejects unsolved level metas.

564. Cure’s dependent pattern machinery still has no principled coverage + unification report.
     Cure:
     - Kernel coverage returns `:coverage`.
     - Branch type failure is collapsed to `:branch_type`.
     - Impossible branches are skipped.
     - Elaborator transforms guards/nested patterns before the kernel sees them.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris report missing clauses, impossible clauses, inaccessible patterns, coverage gaps, unification failures.
     - Lean reports match alternative/motive/elimination errors with local context.

     Fix:
     - Structured branch diagnostics:
       - missing constructor,
       - duplicate constructor,
       - impossible constructor,
       - branch arity mismatch,
       - branch motive mismatch,
       - index unification stuck,
       - branch body type mismatch with expected/inferred normal forms.

565. Cure’s Core `case` is a custom eliminator rather than generated recursor applications.
     Cure:
     - Core term has:
       `{:case, scrut, motive, branches}`
     - Kernel checks motive/coverage/branch bodies directly.
     - Eval has direct case reduction and stuck `:ncase`.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean elaborates pattern matching to recursors/casesOn/projections over inductive declarations.
     - Agda/Idris use case trees/internal eliminators but with a full coverage/case compiler.
     - Cure’s `case` is acceptable as a design choice, but the current one is under-specified relative to recursor typing.

     Fix:
     - Either:
       - define `case` formally as the primitive eliminator with complete typing rules,
       - or lower cases to generated recursor globals.
     - If keeping primitive case, it needs:
       - no duplicate branch checks,
       - impossible branch evidence,
       - exact motive typing,
       - structured index constraint handling,
       - proof of subject reduction for eval.

566. Cure’s “absurd” marker is elaborator-only but lives in Core syntax.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `infer(_ctx, {:absurd}) -> {:error, :absurd_in_reachable_position}`
     - It is described as an elaborator-only marker sitting in discharged unreachable branches.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris have absurd patterns or impossible terms in elaboration/case compilation, but final representation is typed through empty eliminators/impossible branches.
     - Lean uses no-confusion/recursor/match compilation rather than a raw untyped absurd sentinel in core terms.

     What Cure has done:
     - Added an untyped marker to Core with only a negative typing rule.

     Fix:
     - Represent absurdity by eliminating from an empty/impossible equality/constraint.
     - Or keep `absurd` only in elaborator IR, not Core.
     - Final Core should not contain untyped sentinels.

567. Cure’s proof erasure happens by evaluating `rewrite` away.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `eval({:rewrite, _proof, _motive, body}, env), do: eval(body, env)`
     - That means proof irrelevance for rewrite is hardcoded as an evaluator equation.

     Reference contrast:
     - Proof erasure in Lean/Agda/Idris is handled through proof irrelevance/Prop/erasure/extraction/quantity mechanisms, not by adding a primitive term constructor whose runtime evaluator ignores fields.
     - Equality eliminator reduction is typed and has computation rules.

     Fix:
     - Use canonical Eq eliminator.
     - Erasure pass removes proof/erased arguments based on binder relevance/proof classification.
     - Runtime evaluator should not need primitive rewrite.

568. Cure’s `Eq` builtin is not yet fully integrated as an inductive family.
     Cure:
     - Builtins seed `Eq`.
     - Elaborator constructs `{:data, :Eq, [ty], [a,b]}` and `{:ctor, :refl,[x]}`.
     - But kernel still also accepts `:veq`, primitive `{:eq}`, primitive `{:refl}`.
     - `eq_parts/1` accepts both `:veq` and builtin-inductive Eq.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems do not run two identity-type encodings simultaneously.
     - Builtin equality may have special treatment, but there is one canonical representation for proofs.

     Fix:
     - Make builtin `Eq` the only propositional equality.
     - Add eliminator/recursor as the only way to transport.
     - Delete primitive equality compatibility.

569. Cure’s Context/Env treatment of values depends on absolute levels in a fragile way.
     Cure:
     - Values use `{:nvar, level}`.
     - Quote converts level to index using `depth - level - 1`.
     - Kernel functions often manually compute:
       `idx = Context.length(ctx) - 1 - level`
     - Branch unification shifts reified terms by arity to enter branch context.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean uses locally nameless fvars/bvars with robust abstraction/instantiation primitives.
     - Idris uses scoped terms with type-indexed variables.
     - Agda has mature de Bruijn/context manipulation.

     Fix:
     - Centralize level/index conversion.
     - Add invariants/tests:
       - quote/eval round trips under binders,
       - branch context extension,
       - motive abstraction,
       - carried equality transport,
       - meta strengthening.
     - Avoid open-coded arithmetic on levels where possible.

570. Cure’s immediate priority versus references.
     Highest-priority alignment work:
     1. Define final Core syntax and make Eval/Quote/Normalise/Conv closed over it.
     2. Remove primitive equality/rewrite from final Core.
     3. Replace `{:prim, op, args}` with typed primitive globals.
     4. Replace fixed universes with level expressions/metas/constraints.
     5. Make globals/ctors/families qualified symbols with universe args.
     6. Move holes/metas entirely out of kernel-accepted final terms.
     7. Replace legacy dependent modules with Core elaboration.
     8. Replace sentinel/bridge dependent-match hacks with canonical abstraction + Eq eliminator.
     9. Integrate relevance/erasure into binders.
     10. Add final-Core validator and run it before kernel certification, serialization, emit, release, and publish.

Summary:
- The largest gap from Lean/Agda/Idris is not merely “Cure lacks features.”
- The deeper issue is that Cure currently has several extra trusted representations:
  - primitive propositional equality,
  - primitive rewrite,
  - primitive op nodes,
  - holes accepted by kernel checking,
  - flat data values requiring optional signature-aware quote,
  - legacy dependent/SMT tuple types,
  - surface reducer/CoreBridge,
  - sentinel-based dependent motive abstraction,
  - branch-skipping index unifier in the kernel.
- Those extra mechanisms are exactly where soundness and maintainability risk concentrates.
FOCUSED PASS: KERNEL AND SURROUNDING DEPENDENT CORE

571. Kernel still treats `{:absurd}` as a Core term instead of an elaborator-only unreachable marker.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `def infer(_ctx, {:absurd}), do: {:error, :absurd_in_reachable_position}`

     Problem:
     - `{:absurd}` is explicitly described as “an elaborator-only marker,” but it is present in the Core term language and kernel pattern matches it.
     - This is a negative typing rule, not a real eliminator.
     - Final Core should not need raw unreachable sentinels.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris absurd clauses are elaboration/coverage artifacts that compile into a well-typed impossible elimination or disappear.
     - Lean does not carry a raw “absurd marker” as an arbitrary expression term in checked Core.

     Fix:
     - Remove `{:absurd}` from final Core.
     - Use an empty eliminator / impossible equality eliminator when an impossible branch must be represented.
     - Keep any marker in elaborator IR only.

572. `check_case_branches` can skip branch bodies based on kernel index unification.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - branch index unifier can return `:impossible`
       - impossible branch bodies are not checked.

     Problem:
     - If `unify_indices/4` has a false `:impossible`, the kernel accepts an ill-typed branch.
     - That makes branch-body checking depend on a custom unifier’s soundness.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris do have impossible-branch handling, but it is backed by a coverage/unification system designed specifically for pattern elaboration.
     - Lean’s match compilation/recursor elaboration does not generally trust a small custom first-order unifier inside the kernel to skip arbitrary branch terms.

     Fix:
     - Either:
       - require impossible branches to be represented by a checked absurd eliminator, or
       - move impossible-branch pruning out of the kernel and into elaboration with proof evidence.
     - If kept, `:impossible` must be treated as a trusted theorem and tested accordingly.

573. `unify_indices/4` zips result indices and scrutinee indices without first checking equal length.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `Enum.zip(result_indices, scrut_indices)`

     Problem:
     - `Enum.zip/2` truncates.
     - If arity mismatch ever reaches this helper, extra indices are silently ignored.
     - Callers may often have prior checks, but this helper’s local invariant is too weak for trusted code.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference unifiers treat arity/spine mismatch as failure/stuck, not silent truncation.

     Fix:
     - Add:
       `if length(result_indices) != length(scrut_indices), do: :undecided_or_error`
     - For trusted branch impossibility, mismatch should never be silently solved.

574. `unify_spine/4` treats mismatched spines as success.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `defp unify_spine(_, _, _arity, subst), do: {:ok, subst}`

     Problem:
     - This is directly wrong as a unification primitive.
     - A mismatched nested spine should not preserve the current substitution and continue as if solved.
     - Even if earlier guards catch many length mismatches, this fallback is a latent unsoundness trap.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris do not treat mismatched application/constructor spines as solved equations.

     Fix:
     - Replace with:
       - `:undecided` for conservatively unresolved, or
       - `:impossible` for rigid constructor/data arity mismatch.
     - Add tests with nested constructor/data index spines of different lengths.

575. Kernel branch unifier drops `:undecided` constraints.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `reduce_index_pairs/3`
       - on `:undecided`, it continues with the existing substitution:
         `reduce_index_pairs(rest, subst, arity)`

     Problem:
     - An undecided index equation is discarded.
     - The resulting branch can still be checked under a partially specialized context.
     - This is safe only if the branch body is then checked in a way that does not depend on the dropped equality; that invariant is not explicit.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference elaborators keep unsolved constraints/metas and either solve, defer, or report them.
     - They do not silently forget required index constraints.

     Fix:
     - Return a structured result:
       - `{:solved, subst}`
       - `:trivial`
       - `{:stuck, constraints}`
       - `:impossible`
     - If a branch requires stuck constraints to type-check, reject or generate explicit equality assumptions.

576. Kernel `branch_unify/4` returns `:impossible` on unknown constructor/family.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `else _ -> :impossible`

     Problem:
     - Unknown constructor or family mismatch is not the same as an impossible branch.
     - It is a malformed Core/query error.
     - Returning `:impossible` can make elaborator-side branch reasoning discard real errors.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems distinguish malformed terms/names from logically impossible cases.

     Fix:
     - Return:
       - `{:error, {:unknown_ctor, cname}}`
       - `{:error, {:wrong_family, expected, got}}`
       - `:impossible` only for genuine impossible index equations.

577. Kernel equality detection accepts both primitive equality and builtin-inductive Eq.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `ensure_eq({:veq, ...})`
       `ensure_eq({:vdata, :Eq, [_ty, a, b]})`

     Problem:
     - The kernel currently has two trusted equality encodings.
     - Any rewrite/equality logic must reason about both.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris have one trusted identity type representation, plus separate boolean equality/typeclass equality if needed.

     Fix:
     - Delete `:veq`.
     - `ensure_eq/1` should accept only canonical builtin-inductive `Eq`.
     - Rewrite must become an Eq eliminator application.

578. Kernel `Eq` recognition is hardcoded to bare atom `:Eq`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `{:vdata, :Eq, [_ty, a_value, b_value]}`

     Problem:
     - This bypasses builtin registry/name resolution.
     - It will break once globals/families are qualified.
     - It allows any family named bare `:Eq` to get equality status if namespace hygiene is weak.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference languages use resolved builtin names or qualified constants, not raw host atoms.

     Fix:
     - Resolve Eq through the builtin registry:
       `Env.builtin(env, :eq_type)` or equivalent.
     - Store builtin symbols as stable qualified IDs.

579. Kernel primitive Bool result construction is hardcoded to `:True` / `:False`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `defp vbool(true), do: {:vctor, :True, []}`
       `defp vbool(false), do: {:vctor, :False, []}`

     Problem:
     - The evaluator has no signature/env parameter at the primitive folding point.
     - It assumes global bare constructor names for Bool.
     - This blocks proper namespacing and can collide with user constructors.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris constructors are resolved qualified constants.

     Fix:
     - Primitive reducers should have access to the primitive/builtin registry.
     - Or primitive comparison should reduce to typed primitive globals whose result constructors are known by resolved IDs.

580. Evaluator can crash when case branch is missing.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `{_cname, _arity, body} = Enum.find(branches, fn {c, _ar, _b} -> c == cname end)`

     Problem:
     - Missing branch causes a match failure.
     - Kernel coverage should prevent this, but artifact evaluation/normalization should still reject malformed Core structurally.

     Reference contrast:
     - Trusted core evaluators usually assume well-typed terms internally, but external artifact validators reject malformed terms before evaluation.
     - Cure currently lacks a strong final-Core validator.

     Fix:
     - Add final-Core validation before evaluation/serialization/emit.
     - Optionally make evaluator return structured `{:error, {:missing_case_branch, cname}}` in safe API.

581. Evaluator ignores the motive when reducing constructor cases.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       on constructor scrutinee, evaluates branch body directly and does not inspect motive.

     Problem:
     - This is operationally fine if kernel has already checked the case.
     - But it means malformed case terms can evaluate without motive consistency.
     - The evaluator is not a validating evaluator.

     Reference contrast:
     - Recursor reduction in reference cores is safe because recursor applications are well-typed by construction.
     - Cure’s primitive case needs final-Core validation before evaluation.

     Fix:
     - Require final-Core validation.
     - Consider representing case as generated recursor application, where motive correctness is enforced by recursor typing.

582. Normalizer public API returns `:fuel_exhausted` as a term-like result.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `@spec normalize(...) :: Term.t() | :fuel_exhausted`
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex uses fuel exhaustion as a sentinel.

     Problem:
     - A caller can accidentally treat `:fuel_exhausted` like a normal atom result.
     - Fuel exhaustion is not a normal form.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference checkers report timeout/interruption/fuel exhaustion as an error/status, not as a term.

     Fix:
     - Safe API:
       `{:ok, normal_form} | {:error, :fuel_exhausted}`
     - Reserve raw term-returning API for internal call sites with strict handling.

583. Normalizer fuel uses process dictionary state.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/normalise.ex:
       process dictionary fuel key.

     Problem:
     - Hidden mutable state affects normalization result.
     - Nested normalization can clobber outer fuel unless restored carefully.
     - This is not reproducible checker state.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris checker state is explicit in monads/contexts/options.

     Fix:
     - Thread fuel through normalizer state.
     - Restore previous process value if process dictionary remains temporarily.
     - Make fuel part of explicit normalization options.

584. Conversion’s “same neutral before δ” shortcut may mask intended transparency behavior.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/conv.ex:
       `same_neutral_no_delta?` accepts identical stuck neutrals before attempting δ.

     Problem:
     - This is useful to avoid recursive unfolding loops.
     - But it also means `f x` is definitionally equal to `f x` regardless of whether unfolding would expose malformed/non-final body details.
     - That is okay only if all certified globals are already final and checked.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems also use reflexivity/neutral equality before unfolding, but their environments contain only checked declarations.

     Fix:
     - This shortcut is fine only after certification is made airtight.
     - Strengthen `Env.certify/2` and final-Core validation before relying on this.

585. Conversion likely lacks a principled transparency mode.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/conv.ex and normalizer gate δ through certification.
     - No observed mode distinction like reducible/semireducible/irreducible, theorem/proof, opaque, runtime-only primitive.

     Problem:
     - As Cure grows, definitional equality needs stable transparency policy.
     - Currently everything certified appears δ-reducible in the same way.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean has reducibility/transparency distinctions.
     - Agda has abstract/opaque/rewrite/termination controls.
     - Idris has totality and visibility decisions around reduction.

     Fix:
     - Add declaration transparency:
       - reducible
       - semireducible
       - opaque
       - theorem/proof-erased
       - primitive-reducer
     - Conversion should take a transparency mode.

586. Kernel `check_def/2` checks body against type but does not enforce final-Core invariants.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `check_def/2` checks type sort and body type.

     Problem:
     - It does not centrally reject:
       - holes,
       - metas,
       - primitive equality,
       - primitive rewrite,
       - unresolved universe metas once added,
       - non-final primitive op nodes,
       - unsafe markers,
       - pending bodies.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems separate elaboration artifacts from final checked declarations.
     - Unsolved metas/holes never become accepted environment declarations.

     Fix:
     - Add:
       `Core.Final.validate_def(env, name)`
     - Run it before certification, serialization, emit, release, and publish.

587. `validate_certificate/2` is not enough if raw `Env.certify/2` remains callable.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex has `validate_certificate/2`.
     - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex exposes `Env.certify/2`.

     Problem:
     - The certification operation itself can be called outside the validating path.
     - Normalizer trusts certified globals for δ.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference kernels do not expose a public “mark this definition reducible/terminating” escape hatch.

     Fix:
     - Make certification require an opaque token returned by validation.
     - Or make `Env.certify/2` private/internal and re-check everything itself.

588. Termination certificate call graph still walks proof/type positions.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/certificate.ex:
       traversal walks many term forms including type/equality/rewrite positions.

     Problem:
     - A global mentioned only in a type, motive, or proof can be counted as a computational call.
     - This can cause false rejections and confuse the criterion.
     - More importantly, it shows the termination pass is not quantity/relevance-aware.

     Reference contrast:
     - Idris/Agda termination checking is tied to the computational content of definitions after relevance/erasure decisions.

     Fix:
     - Build call graphs over computationally relevant positions.
     - Integrate binder relevance/erasure into Core before certification.

589. Termination checker still knows primitive equality/rewrite.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/certificate.ex:
       `walk_node` has clauses for `{:eq}`, `{:refl}`, `{:rewrite}`.

     Problem:
     - The certificate pass still treats retired primitive equality as part of the Core grammar.
     - This prevents final-Core cleanup from being local.

     Fix:
     - Remove primitive equality/rewrite from certificate traversal once Eq eliminator lands.
     - Add test: final-Core certificate traversal rejects unknown/retired forms.

590. Positivity checker still knows primitive rewrite.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex:
       `occurs?(fname, {:rewrite, p, m, b})`

     Problem:
     - Inductive positivity is part of trusted declaration checking.
     - It should not include retired primitive proof syntax.

     Fix:
     - Remove primitive rewrite from final Core.
     - Make positivity traversal fail on unknown/retired nodes in strict mode.

591. Positivity checker likely treats globals opaquely.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/inductive.ex positivity checks syntactic occurrence in constructor argument types.
     - It does not appear to expand type synonyms/aliases or reducible type-level functions.

     Problem:
     - If type aliases or reducible globals can hide negative occurrences, positivity can be unsound.
     - If they cannot appear there, the kernel should enforce that explicitly.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Lean/Idris positivity checkers account for definitions/parameters according to precise rules and reject unsafe abstractions.

     Fix:
     - Define exactly which Core forms are allowed in inductive constructor argument types.
     - Either:
       - disallow reducible globals in positivity-relevant positions, or
       - normalize/expand them under a safe transparency policy before positivity checking.

592. Kernel field-level universe check is too simple for future universe polymorphism.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `check_field_levels(levels, fam_level)` with integer comparison.

     Problem:
     - This works only for fixed integer levels.
     - Once level variables/max/succ/cumulativity enter, this must become constraint solving.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda manage level constraints, not direct integer comparisons.

     Fix:
     - Replace `level <= fam_level` with generated universe constraints.
     - Solve constraints during declaration checking.

593. Kernel `infer_sort/2` only accepts exact `{:vtype, level}`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `infer_sort` accepts only direct `:vtype`.

     Problem:
     - No cumulativity or sort hierarchy beyond `Type i : Type i+1` at this point.
     - No `Prop`/proof-irrelevant universe if Cure later wants Lean-like proof erasure.
     - No level metavariable handling.

     Fix:
     - Fine for the current small core, but final design needs sort expressions and constraints.
     - Decide whether Cure has only `Type u` or also `Prop`.

594. `check/3` falls back to inference plus conversion, but error reasons are collapsed.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       fallback:
       - infer term
       - compare inferred/expected
       - return `{:type_mismatch, %{expected, inferred}}` or raw infer error.

     Problem:
     - For dependent elaboration, this loses path-specific information:
       - motive mismatch,
       - branch index mismatch,
