       - equality eliminator mismatch,
       - universe mismatch,
       - failed implicit solve.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris preserve rich elaborator contexts and expected/inferred terms.

     Fix:
     - Return structured typed errors with context:
       - current local context,
       - expected type,
       - inferred type,
       - normalized forms,
       - source span/declaration path.

595. Kernel case motive checking is value-level and difficult to audit.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `check_motive_wf/4` works on `motive_value`.

     Problem:
     - Motive is evaluated first, then applied to neutral index/scrutinee values.
     - This is compact, but hard to validate against source-level shape.
     - It depends on read-back/eval correctness and flat `vdata` conventions.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems type recursor motives as terms in context, with explicit dependent function types.

     Fix:
     - Check motives syntactically against an expected Pi telescope:
       `(indices...) -> D params indices -> Type level`
     - Then evaluate after typing.

596. Motive well-formedness depends on applying motive to generated neutral values.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       generates fresh neutral index values and scrutinee values for motive checking.

     Problem:
     - This is an NbE-style check, but it relies on correct level/index bookkeeping.
     - It should be backed by strong round-trip tests.

     Fix:
     - Add kernel tests:
       - motive arity too short,
       - motive arity too long,
       - motive result not a type,
       - motive depending on wrong index,
       - motive over parameterized family,
       - motive over computed index family.

597. Data values flatten params and indices, and case code repeatedly splits them.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `{:vdata, name, params ++ indices}`
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       split by `Inductive.param_count`.

     Problem:
     - This is fragile and infects kernel, quote, motive checking, branch unification, and conversion.
     - It is a root cause of optional signature-aware quote issues.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems do not require every consumer to remember how to split a flattened application by consulting metadata at the right time.

     Fix:
     - Change value representation to:
       `{:vdata, name, params, indices}`
     - Change conversion/quote/eval/kernel accordingly.

598. Constructor values do not carry family/parameter identity.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       `{:vctor, name, args}`

     Problem:
     - A constructor value only contains the constructor name and runtime args.
     - If constructor names are unqualified or collide, evaluator/case matching is ambiguous.

     Reference contrast:
     - Constructors are qualified constants in reference languages.

     Fix:
     - Use qualified constructor IDs.
     - Optionally include family ID in constructor value or make constructor ID globally unique.

599. Kernel constructor inference rejects parameterized constructors instead of using implicit metas.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       parameter-bearing constructor infer path returns:
       `{:ctor_requires_checking_mode, family_name}`

     Problem:
     - The kernel cannot infer constructor type for parameterized constructors.
     - Elaborator must work around with special bidirectional logic.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris elaborate implicit constructor parameters with metas and constraints.

     Fix:
     - Treat constructors as global constants with full Pi types.
     - Elaborator inserts implicit params.
     - Kernel inference then works on ordinary fully applied terms.

600. Constructor checking computes result params/indices by evaluating terms in an arg environment, but the representation is non-obvious.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - `check_ctor_app/4`
       - result params/indices evaluated in accumulated arg env.
       - comments note most-recent-first env conventions.

     Problem:
     - Correctness depends on fragile de Bruijn environment ordering across params and constructor args.
     - Several comments already document subtle indexing.

     Fix:
     - Add explicit telescope substitution utilities instead of manually constructing evaluation env lists.
     - Test constructors whose result indices reference:
       - params,
       - earlier args,
       - later impossible args,
       - computed indices.

601. `replace_branch_vars/2` manually recurses over retired forms and has a catch-all success.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       - handles many Core terms manually.
       - includes primitive equality/rewrite.
       - final catch-all:
         `defp replace_branch_vars(other, _subst), do: other`

     Problem:
     - New Core terms are silently left unreplaced.
     - If a new binder/term form contains index vars, specialization will be wrong.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems use central traversal/substitution infrastructure.

     Fix:
     - Replace catch-all with explicit known leaves only.
     - Use central Core traversal.
     - Unknown Core term in branch specialization should be an internal error.

602. `specialize_branch_context/2` reifies every context type, rewrites vars, then evaluates under the old env.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `Quote.reify(depth) |> replace_branch_vars(subst) |> Eval.eval(env)`

     Problem:
     - This is a powerful operation over all context types.
     - It relies on quote preserving enough structure and `replace_branch_vars` being binder-correct.
     - Since quote currently flattens data params/indices, branch specialization inherits that fragility.

     Fix:
     - Use a typed substitution operation over values/closures directly, or make read-back signature-aware.
     - Add tests for branch specialization under nested binders and parameterized indexed families.

603. Kernel branch substitution can bind outer index variables as well as constructor variables.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `unify_one(r, {:var, j}, arity, subst) when j >= arity`
       binds outer index variable to ctor result index.

     Problem:
     - This specializes the ambient branch context by solving outer index vars.
     - That is powerful and can be correct, but it resembles unification/refinement rather than simple branch checking.
     - It needs a clear invariant: which outer variables may be solved, and what assumptions enter branch context?

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris coverage/refinement engines track such index equalities explicitly.

     Fix:
     - Represent solved outer-index equations as explicit constraints/equalities.
     - Do not mutate/specialize unrelated context entries without recording the equality assumption.

604. `bind_index/4` uses a 100,000-depth defensive bound for substitution chasing.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `resolve_index_var({:var, k}, subst, depth) when depth < 100_000`

     Problem:
     - A trusted kernel algorithm relies on an arbitrary large bound.
     - If hit, it returns the term without signalling an error.

     Fix:
     - Maintain an acyclic union-find structure explicitly.
     - If a defensive bound is hit, return structured internal error, not apparent success.

605. `occurs_index?/2` over-approximates by tuple/list traversal without binder awareness.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       tuple/list traversal over all tuple elements.

     Problem:
     - The comment says it ignores binder-depth shifts.
     - Over-approximation is conservative for solving, but it may cause spurious undecided cases.
     - More importantly, it is another bespoke traversal over the whole Core shape.

     Fix:
     - Use central binder-aware traversal.
     - Distinguish rigid index terms from arbitrary Core terms.

606. `strongly_rigid_occurs?/2` only descends through ctor/data spines.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex.

     Problem:
     - This is intentionally conservative, but it is a hand-ported fragment of Agda’s cycle rule.
     - It is not backed by a full left-hand-side unification framework.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda’s cycle/conflict rules live inside a much richer unification/checking architecture.

     Fix:
     - Keep conservative cycle detection, but do not use it to skip branch checking unless backed by formal tests.
     - Add property tests:
       - false impossible must never occur,
       - missed impossible only causes rejection/stuck, not acceptance.

607. `infer_prim/3` types division as total Int/Float arithmetic.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       `infer_prim(ctx, op, [a,b]) when op in [:add,:sub,:mul,:div]`

     Problem:
     - `:div` is typed alongside total arithmetic.
     - Eval leaves division by zero stuck.
     - This means a closed term `1 / 0` may type-check and normalize to a neutral primitive rather than reject or return an option/result.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference kernels do not generally make partial machine division a silent stuck logical term.

     Fix:
     - Remove raw division from logic-total primitives.
     - Use proof argument / NonZero / Result.

608. `infer_prim/3` still accepts numeric `:eq` / `:ne`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       numeric equality primitive returns Bool.

     Problem:
     - This is runtime boolean equality, not propositional equality.
     - It is acceptable only if clearly a primitive Bool-returning function, not related to Eq proofs.
     - Current naming `:eq` overlaps with primitive propositional `{:eq,...}` and stdlib Eq.

     Fix:
     - Rename primitive ops internally:
       - `:int_eq`
       - `:float_eq`
     - Or move to typed primitive globals:
       `Int.eq : Int -> Int -> Bool`.

609. Primitive op unknown fallback is an error in kernel but stuck in evaluator.
     Cure:
     - Kernel:
       unknown prim => `{:error, {:unknown_prim, op}}`
     - Eval:
       unknown fold => `:stuck` => neutral `{:nprim, op, args}`

     Problem:
     - Evaluator can produce neutral unknown primitives if called on malformed terms.
     - The evaluator assumes kernel-checked terms, but artifact tools need validation.

     Fix:
     - Final-Core validation before eval.
     - Safe eval API should reject unknown primitive ops.

610. `Eval.eval({:global, name}, _)` is always opaque; δ is only in normalizer/conversion.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/eval.ex:
       globals evaluate to `{:nglobal, name}`.
     - Normalizer/conv handle certified δ elsewhere.

     Problem:
     - There are now two “evaluation” notions:
       - plain Eval without δ,
       - Normalise/Conv with certified δ.
     - Kernel uses Eval in many typing places, so whether a type normalizes through globals depends on where conversion is called.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems distinguish WHNF/reduction modes explicitly.
     - Cure’s plain `Eval.eval` name can mislead callers into thinking it performs full definitional evaluation.

     Fix:
     - Rename:
       - `Eval.eval` => `eval_opaque`
       - normalizer APIs => `whnf/nf` with transparency.
     - Audit kernel call sites that should use WHNF instead of opaque eval.

611. Kernel type formation can fail to reduce type-level aliases before `infer_sort`.
     Cure:
     - `infer_sort` calls `infer`, then expects inferred type to be `{:vtype, level}`.
     - Plain `infer` of a global returns evaluated type but global bodies are opaque.

     Problem:
     - If Cure later allows type synonyms or reducible aliases in type positions, formation may need WHNF/δ.
     - Current behavior may reject valid aliases or require elaborator hacks.

     Fix:
     - Define type synonym handling:
       - either expand aliases before kernel,
       - or allow transparent reducible globals in type formation.

612. `check_field_levels/2` prevents constructors from storing fields in higher universes, but no cumulative coercion is modeled.
     Cure:
     - field levels must be `<= fam_level`.

     Problem:
     - With fixed levels this is simple.
     - With cumulative universes and universe polymorphism, this becomes a constraint, not a boolean.

     Fix:
     - Replace with universe constraint generation.

613. `Context` length/index arithmetic is open-coded across kernel/elab.
     Cure:
     - Kernel:
       - `Context.length(ctx) - 1 - level`
       - shifting by branch arity
       - reifying at `outer_depth`
     - Elaborator has many similar calculations.

     Problem:
     - De Bruijn/level bugs become likely.
     - Reference implementations centralize this.

     Fix:
     - Add APIs:
       - `Context.level_to_index/2`
       - `Context.index_to_level/2`
       - `Context.fresh_neutral/1`
       - `Context.extend_many/2`
     - Ban open-coded arithmetic in kernel/elaborator except inside those helpers.

614. Elab.MetaCtx stores only a type term, not the local context.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       `%MetaCtx{types: %{id => type_term}}`

     Problem:
     - Meta type is recorded without the context in which it was created.
     - Solving under binders uses depth shifting/strengthening heuristics instead.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean metavariables carry local context.
     - Agda/Idris metas carry context/spine dependencies.

     Fix:
     - Store:
       - local context,
       - type,
       - universe context,
       - source span,
       - allowed dependencies.

615. Unifier WHNF pre-reduction is disabled under binders.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       `defp whnf_pre(t, _ctx, _sig, depth) when depth != 0, do: t`

     Problem:
     - Unification under binders is incomplete.
     - This is explicitly due to shifting complexity.
     - Dependent types frequently require solving under binders.

     Reference contrast:
     - Lean/Agda/Idris unification/reduction works under local contexts.

     Fix:
     - Make metas contextual and implement binder-aware WHNF.
     - Remove the depth-zero restriction.

616. Unifier preserves original term when WHNF produces a lambda from a non-lambda.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       if WHNF turns non-lambda into lambda, keep original.

     Problem:
     - This is a custom completeness hack to preserve neutral spines for first-order solving.
     - It means unification result depends on syntactic shape-preservation policy, not pure definitional equality.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference unifiers use reduction strategies with principled stuck/eta/constraint handling.

     Fix:
     - Represent partial applications and metas properly.
     - Use a real constraint solver that can handle lambdas/neutral spines instead of suppressing WHNF results.

617. Miller pattern detection accepts any distinct variables, but meta occurrences do not carry explicit spines.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       detects `?m x̄` by peeling `{:app, ...}` syntax.
       Meta itself is just `{:meta, id}`.

     Problem:
     - The occurrence form is syntactic, not part of meta representation.
     - This is fragile around normalization, shifting, and zonking.

     Reference contrast:
     - Idris/Agda metas are applied to their environments/spines explicitly.
     - Lean mvars have local contexts and assignments.

     Fix:
     - Represent meta occurrence as:
       `{:meta, id, spine}` or ordinary mvar with explicit local context.
     - Solve metas against their declared telescope.

618. Miller solver peels Pi domains syntactically from the recorded meta type.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       `peel_pi_domains(mtype, n)`

     Problem:
     - If the meta type reduces to a Pi only after normalization, solving fails.
     - If the type has unresolved metas/universe constraints, the solver may make a premature choice.

     Fix:
     - Normalize the meta type under its recorded context before peeling.
     - If not a Pi, defer the constraint instead of falling through silently.

619. Miller solutions are not immediately type-checked against the meta type.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/unify.ex:
       solution is inserted with `MetaCtx.put_solution`.

     Problem:
     - Comments say kernel will catch bad solves downstream.
     - That is not a good invariant for elaboration.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference elaborators maintain well-typed metavariable assignments.

     Fix:
     - After constructing a solution, check:
       `solution : meta_type`
       in the meta’s stored local context.
     - Reject or defer if not checkable.

620. `has_meta?/1` in elaborator is incomplete and has catch-all false.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       handles some Core forms:
       data, ctor, app, pi, lam, sigma, pair, fst, snd
       then:
       `defp has_meta?(_), do: false`

     Problem:
     - It misses forms such as:
       - case,
       - primitive equality,
       - rewrite,
       - primitive ops,
       - int/float primitives if nested,
       - future Eq eliminator,
       - future universe/metalevel forms.
     - Unsolved metas hidden in missed forms can bypass checks.

     Fix:
     - Replace with central Core traversal.
     - Unknown form should be an internal error in strict/final elaboration.

621. `has_meta?/1` does not inspect case motives or branches.
     Cure:
     - Same function as above.

     Problem:
     - A meta inside a dependent match motive or branch body may not be detected by local elaborator checks.
     - Kernel/eval may later crash or produce malformed terms.

     Fix:
     - Central meta-free validator over all Core forms.
     - Run after zonking and before kernel handoff.

622. `finish_global_app/6` swallows expected-type unification failure.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       comments say unification failure is swallowed to preserve “honest unsolved metavariables” errors.

     Problem:
     - This loses the real cause:
       - expected result mismatch,
       - implicit parameter mismatch,
       - universe mismatch.
     - It can report `:unsolved_metavariables` instead of an actual type error.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference elaborators preserve failed constraints and report the relevant mismatch.

     Fix:
     - Keep the unification failure as a diagnostic cause.
     - If metas remain unsolved, report both:
       - unsolved metas,
       - failed expected-result constraint.

623. `elaborate_free_name/3` falls back to global reference for any unknown name.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       - if not local, not constructor, not family:
         `{:ok, {:global, atom}}`

     Problem:
     - Unknown names become Core globals.
     - The error is deferred to kernel inference.
     - This also creates atoms eagerly.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference elaborators resolve names before producing constants; unresolved names are elaboration errors/metas only in controlled contexts.

     Fix:
     - Unknown free name should be an elaboration error.
     - If forward declarations are allowed, they must be registered explicitly as pending globals.

624. `elaborate_type/3` treats unknown type variable names as data families.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       if not in scope:
       `{:ok, {:data, String.to_atom(name), [], []}}`

     Problem:
     - A misspelled type becomes a family reference.
     - The error is deferred and atomized.
     - This conflates type variables, type constructors, and unknown names.

     Fix:
     - Resolve through environment.
     - Unknown type name = elaboration error.
     - Type variables must be in scope or explicitly generalized.

625. `elaborate_named_call_scoped/4` treats known constructor names specially but otherwise app-spines unknown globals.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex:
       if `Inductive.get_ctor(env, atom)` then ctor, else elaborate variable/global and apply.

     Problem:
     - Function, constructor, family, and primitive-global namespaces are not cleanly separated.
     - Unknown function calls can become unknown globals instead of early errors.

     Fix:
     - Use resolved names:
       - local var,
       - global def,
       - constructor,
       - family,
       - primitive,
       - builtin.
     - Reject unresolved names before Core generation.

626. Scoped/simple elaborator at bottom of file is a second elaborator subset.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/elab/elaborator.ex near bottom:
       - `elaborate_expr/3`
       - `elaborate_type/3`
       - `elaborate_named_call_scoped/4`
     - It supports a small subset and has its own name handling.

     Problem:
     - Cure has both the main bidirectional elaborator and this scoped helper path.
     - They may disagree on syntax, names, holes, constructors, implicit args, and dependent features.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference languages usually have one elaboration engine, with helper APIs sharing the same context/resolution machinery.

     Fix:
     - Merge this path into the main elaborator or mark it advisory-only.
     - Do not use subset elaborator in trusted static/dependent pipeline.

627. CoreBridge still lowers boolean literals to bare `:True` / `:False`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex:
       boolean literal becomes `{:ctor, :True/:False, []}`

     Problem:
     - This repeats the namespacing problem outside the kernel.
     - It is also part of the legacy dependent machinery, not the canonical elaborator.

     Fix:
     - Remove CoreBridge from trusted path.
     - If kept, use builtin registry IDs.

628. CoreBridge lowers surface binary ops to `{:prim, op, args}`.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex:
       `{:ok, {:prim, op, [l, r]}}`

     Problem:
     - This keeps primitive op nodes alive through legacy type-level reduction.
     - It conflicts with the goal of typed primitive globals.

     Fix:
     - Replace with primitive global applications, or quarantine CoreBridge as advisory only.

629. CoreBridge maps function calls to global applications by string-to-atom.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/core_bridge.ex:
       `Enum.reduce(cargs, {:global, String.to_atom(name)}, ...)`

     Problem:
     - No name resolution, no local context, no module qualification, no arity checking.

     Fix:
     - Do not use CoreBridge for trusted checking.
     - If retained, require a checked resolution context.

630. `Cure.Types.Reduce` normalizes by CoreBridge then falls back to structural surface recursion.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex:
       if CoreBridge works, eval/reify/from_core.
       otherwise structural congruence over surface AST.

     Problem:
     - This is neither full Core normalization nor a principled surface evaluator.
     - It can make equality decisions based on a mixture of semantics.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference systems do not use a second approximate normalizer for trusted dependent equality.

     Fix:
     - Remove from trusted path.
     - Use Core Normalise/Conv only.

631. `Cure.Types.Reduce` folds bool ops at surface level.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/reduce.ex:
       `fold_bool_binop`, `fold_bool_not`

     Problem:
     - Bool connectives are now stdlib functions over inductive Bool.
     - Surface folding bypasses that definition and may diverge from Core behavior.

     Fix:
     - Do not special-case Bool in legacy reducer.
     - Normalize through Core definitions if trusted, or do not normalize.

632. Legacy Pi application zips params and args.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/pi.ex:
       `Enum.zip(args)`

     Problem:
     - Zip truncation can ignore extra/missing args unless separately checked.
     - It is part of the legacy dependent calculus.

     Fix:
     - Remove from trusted path.
     - If kept, exact arity check before zip.

633. Legacy Sigma returns `:any` for malformed/missing second type.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/types/sigma.ex:
       `:any`

     Problem:
     - This directly conflicts with dependent pair precision.
     - No reference system treats malformed dependent pair projection as `Any`.

     Fix:
     - Error in static/dependent mode.
     - Lower Sigma syntax to Core Sigma.

634. Kernel and elaborator still depend on bare atom constructor/family collisions.
     Cure:
     - Core constructors/families are atoms.
     - Case branches match constructor atoms.
     - Builtin constructors are bare atoms.

     Problem:
     - Constructor namespace collisions can break typing and evaluation.
     - This becomes acute for Bool/Eq/Nat/List/Option/Result.

     Reference contrast:
     - Constructors are qualified constants.

     Fix:
     - Qualified symbol IDs for:
       - families,
       - constructors,
       - globals,
       - builtins.
     - Surface short names resolve to qualified IDs before Core.

635. Kernel `check_coverage/3` checks set inclusion but not duplicate branches.
     Cure:
     - lib/cure/core/kernel.ex:
       coverage uses sets of declared and covered ctor names.

     Problem:
     - Duplicate branch names collapse.
     - A duplicated branch plus missing branch is caught only if missing, but duplicate itself is not diagnosed.
     - Duplicate branches should be invalid Core or at least unreachable warning/error.

     Fix:
     - Reject duplicates.
     - Report duplicate branch constructor names with branch positions.

636. Kernel coverage does not distinguish impossible constructors from missing constructors.
     Cure:
     - It expects all declared constructors to be covered, then later branch unification may mark some branches impossible.

     Problem:
     - For indexed families, some constructors are impossible for a given scrutinee index.
     - Requiring syntactic coverage of all constructors may force dummy impossible branches.
     - This is acceptable as a simple core, but not ergonomic or reference-grade.

     Reference contrast:
     - Agda/Idris coverage can account for impossible clauses.
     - Lean match elaboration can omit impossible alternatives after recursor elaboration.

     Fix:
     - Either keep total constructor coverage as Core invariant, or add an elaborator that fills impossible branches.
     - Do not expose this limitation as the user-facing dependent pattern model.

637. Kernel currently lacks a small formal final-Core grammar boundary.
     Cure:
     - Kernel, eval, quote, normalizer, certificate, positivity, relevance all each know their own term subset.
     - Some accept primitive equality, some metas/hole paths, some catch-all.

     Problem:
     - There is no single “this is Cure Core” gate.
     - This is why retired syntax leaks everywhere.

     Fix:
     - Define:
       `Cure.Core.Final.term?/1`
       `Cure.Core.Final.validate/2`
     - Make every trusted consumer call it or pattern-match exhaustively over final forms.
     - Remove all retired forms from final grammar.

638. Kernel has no subject-reduction regression harness.
     Cure:
     - Given primitive case/eval/rewrite/prim reductions, there should be a property:
       if `Γ ⊢ t : T` and `t ⇝ t'`, then `Γ ⊢ t' : T`.

     Problem:
     - Current audit found several reductions that assume prior checking:
       - case reduction,
       - rewrite erasure,
       - primitive stuck behavior,
       - global δ through certificates.

     Reference contrast:
     - Reference kernels are designed around metatheory; Cure needs test evidence if not proof.

     Fix:
     - Add Antigen assays for preservation:
       - β,
       - ι-case,
       - fst/snd,
       - primitive folds,
       - δ-certified unfold,
       - Eq eliminator once implemented.
     - Mutate malformed Core and ensure validator rejects before eval.

639. Kernel has no progress-style final Core gate.
     Cure:
     - Closed checked terms can still normalize to stuck primitives:
       - e.g. division by zero as `:nprim`.
     - Unknown globals/constructors can become errors or neutrals depending on path.

     Problem:
     - If Cure wants total static code, closed well-typed computational terms should either evaluate to values or be explicitly effectful/partial/stuck by type.

     Fix:
     - Separate logical total fragment from runtime partial fragment.
     - Progress assay:
       - closed, final, total term either value or neutral only if it contains allowed opaque global.
       - no stuck partial primitive in logic-total code.

640. Most urgent kernel-surrounding cleanup from this pass.
     Priority order:
     1. Add final-Core validator and run it everywhere before eval/normalise/certify/emit.
     2. Remove primitive equality/rewrite from kernel/eval/quote/normalise/certificate/positivity.
     3. Fix `unify_spine` mismatched-spine success.
     4. Make branch unification preserve stuck constraints instead of dropping them.
     5. Remove `{:absurd}` from Core or encode it as checked empty elimination.
     6. Replace bare atom names with qualified symbol IDs.
     7. Make data values store params and indices separately.
     8. Move holes/metas fully out of kernel-accepted terms.
     9. Replace `{:prim, op, args}` with typed primitive globals.
     10. Collapse legacy Pi/Sigma/Dependent/Reduce/CoreBridge out of the trusted path.

Summary:
- The kernel is small, but it is not yet “reference-language small.”
- The main divergence from Lean/Agda/Idris is that Cure’s trusted boundary still includes bespoke convenience constructs:
  - primitive rewrite,
  - primitive equality,
  - absurd sentinel,
  - primitive op node,
  - bare atom globals/constructors,
  - flat data values,
  - branch-skipping index unifier,
  - hole acceptance path,
  - legacy dependent reducers/bridges around the real Core.
- Those are the places most likely to block a sound dependent kernel.
